> **AVIF slice 3bd DIAGNOSED 14/10 (pure Kof + a real encoder's file; nothing landed — tree clean): with the `txMode` mapping fixed (3bc), the COMPLEX real frame still diverges — but with a NEW, narrower signature — the SOLID frame now matches the reference, the testsrc frame reads bare DC predictions.** Reality check: the solid-gray 64x64 decodes to `Y=126` == libdav1d (3bc), but the 64x64 testsrc frame reads `Y0=128,0,10`, `Y32=128,128,83` against ffmpeg's `16,145,235` / `16,106,16`. `128` is the DC prediction for a block with NO neighbours — so the TOP-LEFT block's residual is not being applied, while the solid frame's IS (`126`, not `128`). The walk now reports 21 mode blocks / 63 transform blocks (structurally plausible for 64x64). **This localizes the remaining bug to the multi-block RESIDUAL path:** either the first block's coefficients decode as `all_zero` when they are not, or the residual is not added for a block whose neighbours are absent. It is NOT the txMode mapping (fixed), NOT the container/header/cfg (all pinned green), and NOT the solid-case residual path (that one works). Next: dump the first block's `all_zero` symbol, the decoded levels and the prediction for the testsrc frame, the same way the per-stage dump cracked 3bc. **SECOND PASS (measured):** the per-stage dump of the testsrc frame shows `D0 skip=0 txSz=1 y=0` (DC_PRED, TX_8X8, coefficients PRESENT — skip=0) but its first transform blocks decode as **`K0 nz=0` and `K1 nz=0`** while `K2 nz=26`, `K3 nz=8` are non-zero. So the FIRST transform block's coefficients are read as ALL-ZERO (the `all_zero` symbol misdecoded), reconstructing the bare DC prediction 128 where the encoder put a large negative residual (reference 16). The suspect is now the first-block `all_zero` / txb_skip CONTEXT (the `above`/`left` entropy contexts are 0 at the frame origin) or the tile's initial CDF state — NOT the reconstruction (K2/K3 prove the residual path works on this very frame). **THIRD PASS (measured, and the sharpest contrast yet):** driving TWO more real frames isolates the failure to the MULTI-BLOCK path. A uniform `#404040` 64x64 frame (a REAL non-zero residual — `K0 n=1024 nz=1 c0=-237`) decodes to `Y=71`, EXACTLY ffmpeg's value; and the solid-gray frame gives `126` (also exact). Both are SINGLE-block frames (`blocks=1`, `txSz=4` TX_64X64). The testsrc frame is the only MULTI-block one (`blocks=21`, `txSz=1` TX_8X8) and it fails at its FIRST blocks. So the residual / coefficient / prediction / reconstruction paths are all CORRECT on real frames; the bug is in the multi-block machinery — the partition walk, the transform-block grid order, or the per-block tx-size selection (`readTxSize` giving TX_8X8 where the encoder used something else would misplace every block). **FOURTH PASS (measured):** a SIMPLE multi-block real frame also decodes EXACTLY — a 2-tone 64x64 (left black = 16, right white = 235, a geometry that forces more than one block) matches ffmpeg at every sample (`Y=16,235,235` both rows). So the multi-block machinery is NOT broken in general. THREE real frames now match the reference exactly (solid, uniform-dark, 2-tone); only the COMPLEX `testsrc` frame diverges. The difference is what testsrc exercises and the simple frames do not: MANY mode blocks (21 vs 1), varied intra modes (angular), **CFL** (`uv_mode=13`), and **filter-intra** (`fi=1`) — so the remaining bug is in one of those features, most likely in how they consume the entropy stream (a misread symbol there would desync and make the following blocks read as all-zero). Next: bisect the testsrc divergence by feature — encode frames that add angular modes, then CFL, then filter-intra, and find the FIRST feature whose frame stops matching. **FIFTH PASS (measured — the discriminator is NOT the block count):** a DIAGONAL-GRADIENT 64x64 (also a SINGLE block, `blocks=1 txSz=4 y=0`) decodes EXACTLY at every sample (`Yr0=19,73,126`, `Yr32=73,127,180`, `Yr63=126,180,232`), while an 8x8 CHECKERBOARD 64x64 — ALSO a single block by the walk's own report — FAILS with `Y=128` (the bare DC prediction, coefficients misread as all_zero). So having ONE mode block is not the issue (the diagonal passes). **The contrast is the coefficient CONTENT: the smooth diagonal has a LOW EOB, the checkerboard is HIGH-FREQUENCY with a LARGE EOB.** The hypothesis is now that the coefficient decode misreads high-frequency blocks (large EOB / `eob_pt` / `dc_sign` edge cases) as all-zero — which would also explain the testsrc (`uv_mode=13` etc. aside, its first blocks were all-zero too). Next: encode frames of MONOTONE increasing high-frequency content and find the EOB/complexity threshold where the decode breaks. **SIXTH PASS (measured — the partition, not the coefficient content):** a 32x32 checkerboard (`blocks=4`) and a 16x16 checkerboard (`blocks=37`, `nzTot=24`) BOTH decode EXACTLY (`Yr0=16,235,16,235`). Only the 8x8 checkerboard fails — and it reports **`blocks=1`** where the encoder MUST have split (an 8x8 checkerboard is 64 alternating 8x8 tiles; libaom does not encode that as ONE 64x64 block). So the 8x8 case is a PARTITION misread: the walk stops at one block, then the coefficients are read from the wrong stream position and come out all-zero. This supersedes the fifth pass's coefficient-content hypothesis: the coefficient path is exercised and correct by the 16x16 checkerboard (37 blocks, 24 non-zero coefficients, exact match). **The suspect is the PARTITION symbol decode.** Next: dump the first partition symbols of the 8x8 checkerboard and compare with the split the encoder must have written. **EIGHTH PASS (measured — the sixth pass's partition conclusion is RETRACTED):** `blocks=1` for the 8x8 checkerboard is CORRECT, not a misread. The payload arithmetic rules out a split: the 21-block `testsrc` frame needs a **293-byte** tile payload and the 37-block checker-16 needs **111**, while checker-8's ENTIRE item is **36 bytes** — far too small to carry 64 blocks' partition symbols, mode infos and coefficients. A perfect checkerboard is a SINGLE spatial frequency, so libaom encodes it in ONE 64x64 block with a few high-frequency coefficients; the 36-byte payload is exactly those coefficients. **So the fault is the COEFFICIENT decode of that block, not the partition** — reinstating and sharpening the fifth pass's reading: the diagonal (1 block, TX_64X64, MODERATE EOB) decodes exactly, checker-8 (1 block, TX_64X64, LARGE EOB) reads its coefficients as all-zero. The discriminator is the EOB magnitude, i.e. the `eob_pt`/`eob_extra`/coefficient-walk beyond a threshold. Next: drive the solid frame (TX_64X64, nz=1, exact) against checker-8 (TX_64X64, should be nz≫0, reads 0) and dump the `all_zero` symbol, the `eob_pt`, and the EOB value for both. **NINTH PASS (measured — the coefficient stream is SKIPPED, and the bit count proves it):** `Av1Symbol.position()` is in BITS. SOLID consumes **16 bits** of its 2-byte (16-bit) payload — all of it, nz=1, exact; DIAG consumes **144 bits** of its 18-byte (144-bit) payload — all of it, nz=13, exact; CHECKER consumes only **21 bits** of a 19-byte (152-bit) payload and returns `nz=0`. So the checker block's `all_zero` is read as 1 (skip) and the walk STOPS, leaving ~16 bytes (~131 bits) of coefficients unconsumed. The block's mode info is IDENTICAL to the diagonal's (`skip=0 txSz=4 y=0 uv=0 fi=0`, TX_64X64 at the origin, same context), so the same txb_skip/`all_zero` context that is read correctly for the diagonal is read WRONG for the checker — the difference is upstream in the entropy stream: either the mode-info read consumed the wrong number of bits without changing the reported field VALUES, or a CDF the two frames diverge on. Next: instrument the reader POSITION immediately BEFORE `all_zero` for both frames and diff the symbol sequence, not just the final values. **TENTH PASS (measured — the ROOT: SEGMENTATION):** instrumenting the mode-info read position step by step across the three frames: SOLID `start p=15 -> prefix(skip=0,seg=0) p=16 -> body(y=0) p=16 -> txSz=4 p=16` (1 bit of prefix); DIAG `p=15 -> prefix(skip=0,seg=0) p=18 -> body(y=9) p=22`; CHECKER `p=15 -> prefix(skip=0,**seg=1**) p=19 -> body(y=0,**uvm=8**) p=30 -> txSz=4 p=30`. The checker's prefix consumes **4 bits and reads segment id 1**, so **the checker frame is SEGMENTED** (libaom split the qp across segments at crf 20) while the solid/diag are not. `AvifFrame.frameTailWalk` PARSES the segmentation (`segEnabled` at line 341, the `segAltQ[8]` table at 343-374) but `AvifFrameHeader` does NOT expose any of it — so the composition cannot supply the segment state (`segEnabled`, `segAltQ` for the per-segment qindex, and the segment SKIP/CDEF features), and the tile decode proceeds with the segment state absent. This is the SAME class of gap as `txMode` (3bc) and the delta-Q facts (3bb): a frame-level fact the decoder needs, parsed but not surfaced. Next: expose the segmentation state on `AvifFrameHeader` (a 3ax/3bb-style additive slice), pass it to the driver, and re-run checker-8. **ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED (measured):** re-running checker-8 with segmentation ENABLED: `segEnabled=0` -> `symPos=21` bits, `nz=0` (the coefficients are SKIPPED); `segEnabled=1, segIdPreSkip=0` -> **`symPos=152` = the ENTIRE payload, `nz=13`** (the coefficients decode!). So the frame IS segmented and the desync is the missing segment state. The reconstruction is still wrong (`Y=128,129,128,133`), which is the SECOND half: the per-segment qindex (`qIndex = baseQ + segAltQ[seg]`) is not applied, so the residual is dequantized with the wrong factor. **The fix is two parts, both requiring the frame header to EXPOSE the segmentation state** (`segEnabled`, `segAltQ[8]`, and the derived `lastActiveSegId`/`segSkipActive`): (1) pass `segEnabled` to the driver, (2) apply the segment qindex. This is the 3ax/3bb/3bc exposure pattern again. Next: the segmentation-exposure slice + the segment qindex. **ELEVENTH PASS — the exposure slice LANDED, and a real header-parse bug FOUND.** `AvifFrameHeader` now EXPOSES `segEnabled` + `segAltQ[8]` (the frame tail walk already computed both locally). But verifying end-to-end exposed the deeper cause: for the checker file the header reads **`segEnabled=0`** while the tile stream behaves as SEGMENTED — passing `segEnabled=1` fixes the tile decode (`symPos` 21 -> 152 bits, nz 0 -> 13). So the header's `segEnabled` is MISREAD, and since `using_qmatrix` IS read correctly (verified), the shift comes from an earlier field — the prime suspect is the SEQUENCE header's `separate_uv_delta_q` (if Kof reads 0 where the encoder wrote 1, the `diff_uv_delta` bit is skipped and every following frame-header field shifts by 1). The 8 synthetic frame fixtures all read `seg=0` so they cannot see it; only a REAL encoder file can. Next: cross-check the real file's seq facts (`separateUvDeltaQ`, `monochrome`, `bitDepth`) against the encoder and fix the seq walk's bit position. **TWELFTH PASS — the independent oracle found it: the container and the sequence walk DISAGREE.** Cross-checking the real checker file against the `av1C` codec-configuration record (an INDEPENDENT container-level parse, not used for this before): `av1C` says **`monochrome=true`, `chromaSubX=false`, `chromaSubY=false`**, while the Kof SEQUENCE walk (`av1ItemObus().seq`) says **`monochrome=false`, `subX=true`, `subY=true`**. Two parses of the same file must agree, so one is wrong — and the content IS grayscale (a black/white checkerboard with constant chroma), so `monochrome=true` is the plausible truth. **This ties the whole chain together:** `monochrome` sets `numPlanes` (1 vs 3); a wrong `numPlanes` makes the frame header read the phantom UV delta-Q fields (`DeltaQUDc/DeltaQUAc/...`) that a monochrome stream does not carry — shifting every following field by one or more bits, which is exactly why `segEnabled` reads 0 where the truth is 1, and why the tile decode then desyncs. Next: compare the seq walk's `mono_chrome` read position against the spec (`color_config`: `mono_chrome` is read only when `seq_profile != 1`) and find the mis-sized field before it. **THIRTEENTH PASS — DECISIVE, and it SUPERSEDES the segmentation hypothesis:** running the checker with `numPlanes=1` (monochrome, `segEnabled=0`) DECODES its coefficients (`symPos=130`, `nz=28`) where `numPlanes=3` skips them (`symPos=21`, `nz=0`). So the driver desyncs because it consumes CHROMA symbols a monochrome stream does not carry — `numPlanes`/`monochrome` is the cause, not segmentation (the twelfth pass's "passing segEnabled=1" was a coincidence: it re-synced the stream without being the true field). The Kof SEQUENCE walk reads `monochrome=false` while the container's `av1C` says `monochrome=true`; **the av1C record is the oracle and the seq walk is wrong.** The reconstruction is still wrong with `np=1` (`Y=130,128,127,127` vs the reference `16,235,16,235`), so a SECOND defect follows the desync (qindex/prediction). Next: fix the seq walk's `mono_chrome` read position (`color_config` reads it only when `seq_profile != 1`) against the `av1C` oracle, then re-drive. **FOURTEENTH PASS — ffprobe is the definitive oracle, and it RETRACTS the last two passes:** `ffprobe` reports the checker as **`yuv420p`**, NOT monochrome. So the Kof SEQUENCE walk's `mono=false subX=subY=true` is **CORRECT**, and **`readAvifMetadata`'s `av1C` parse (`monochrome=true, chromaSubX=false, chromaSubY=false`) is the BUGGY one** — a real container-parse defect, caught by an oracle outside Kof. Consequence: the thirteenth pass's `numPlanes=1` "fix" was a COINCIDENCE — with ONE block, skipping the chroma desyncs nothing after it, so a mis-set field can "work" by accident. **Methodological correction (important):** every 1-block experiment in this series is WEAK — a wrong field survives if nothing follows to desync. Only MULTI-block frames discriminate, and of those checker-16 passes while testsrc fails. The tile-decode desync cause is therefore STILL OPEN; the `av1C` mono/subsampling misparse is a NEW, independently-verifiable bug (ffprobe as the oracle). **FIFTEENTH PASS — the av1C claim is RETRACTED: it was MY HARNESS, not Kof.** The real file's av1C bytes are `81 00 0c` — byte 2 = `0x0c` = `0000 1100` -> `mono=0, subX=1, subY=1`, exactly what ffprobe says and what the Kof seq walk reads. The Kof av1C parse is CORRECT (`mono = (b2>>4)&1`). My probes read `mono=true subX=false subY=false` because the canonicalized fixture is built by the TEST helper `containerWithItem`, which writes ITS OWN av1C (`AvifMetadataSupport.av1c(..., true, ...)`) around ffmpeg's ITEM bytes — so the container and the item came from DIFFERENT sources. **A canonicalized fixture must preserve the encoder's own av1C**, or any av1C-vs-seq comparison is meaningless. So: no av1C bug, and the twelfth/fourteenth passes' "container disagrees" findings are withdrawn. **What stands:** the real file is yuv420p; the Kof seq walk is right; and with `numPlanes=3` checker-8 desyncs (`nz=0`) while checker-16 (37 blocks) decodes exactly. The desync is STILL OPEN and must be chased with MULTI-block frames. **SIXTEENTH PASS — the ffprobe oracle is now PINNED in the committed test.** `AvifRealFileE2ETest`'s `meta` row now asserts the real file's container facts against ffprobe: `sub=true/true bd=8/8 prof=0` on top of the existing `monochrome=false`. This is a PIN that would have caught my harness artifact at once — the real file parses as 4:2:0 8-bit (matching `ffprobe`), and only my canonicalized fixture (which mixes the test builder's `av1C` with the encoder's item) disagreed. `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **6/6**. The container facts, the sequence walk and ffprobe now agree on all three; the open item remains the TILE-decode desync, which needs a MULTI-block comparison (testsrc 21 blocks fails vs checker-16 37 blocks exact). **SEVENTEENTH PASS — a DECISIVE SPLIT: the entropy decode is FINE on a multi-block frame; the PIXELS are wrong.** Two multi-block controls: (1) a steep horizontal RAMP (`clip(X*4)`, `blocks=1`, `nz=13`) decodes **EXACTLY** (`Y r0=1,64,128,240` == the reference) — a SEVENTH exact real frame; (2) 4px vertical STRIPES (`blocks=22`, `payload=27`) consume their **ENTIRE** payload (`symPos=216` bits = 27 B x 8) and decode **481 non-zero coefficients** — the entropy stream is NOT desynced — yet the reconstruction is `Y r0=128,128,128,129` (the bare DC prediction) where the reference is `16,235,16,235`. So the STRIPES failure is NOT entropy: it is the PREDICTION/RECONSTRUCTION of a multi-block frame, whose neighbours should supply the reference samples but instead fall back to the neighbourless DC. **This splits the problem:** the earlier "desync" framing (checker-8/testsrc) and this "wrong prediction" framing are DIFFERENT failure modes, and this one is now localised to the intra availability / neighbour reference used by `av1IntraPredictBlock` on blocks after the first. Next: dump `haveAbove/haveLeft/haveAboveRight/haveBelowLeft` and the prediction inputs for block 2+ of the STRIPES frame. **EIGHTEENTH PASS — the `all_zero` read is wrong for the FIRST blocks (the origin-context signature), precisely located.** Dumping STRIPES per block: `nb=22 nlev=60`, and the first SIX blocks all read `nz=0` (`D0 txSz=1 y=0 nz=0`, `D1 txSz=1 y=0 nz=0`, `D2 txSz=6 y=12`, `D3/D4/D5 txSz=14 y=0`, all `nz=0 c0=0`) while the run's TOTAL non-zero count is 481 — so LATER blocks DO decode coefficients and the payload is fully consumed (`symPos=216` = 27x8). So the entropy stream is consistent and the fault is the `all_zero`/txb_skip read for the FIRST blocks, exactly the checker-8 signature. **The difference from the frames that work is the TRANSFORM SIZE:** the seven exact frames are single-block TX_64X64 (`txSz=4`), where the origin context reads correctly; STRIPES' first blocks are small transforms (`txSz=1` TX_8X8, `txSz=6`, `txSz=14`) at the frame origin. **Suspect: `av1TxbCtx`/`av1TxbEntropyContext` (the above/left entropy-context derivation) for a SMALL tx at the origin** — where the above/left contexts are 0 — so the `all_zero` CDF index is wrong and the block is read as empty. Next: dump `av1TxbCtx`'s inputs/outputs for block 0 (`txSz=1`, above/left all 0) and compare with the libaom `get_txb_ctx` value for that case. **NINETEENTH PASS — the ctx/CDF path is VERIFIED spec-correct; the field narrows to the block size fed to it.** Reading the code against the spec: `av1TxSzCtx(txSz) = (Tx_Size_Sqr + Tx_Size_Sqr_Up + 1) >> 1` matches AV1 8.3.2 exactly, so the `txbSkip[txSzCtx * 13 + ctx]` index is right; `av1AllZeroCtxY` returns **0 iff the tx size equals the block size** and **1 iff both neighbours are 0** (with tx != block) — both matching the spec; the 13 default `txbSkip` CDF tables are slice-3c-verified against libaom. **So the discriminator is the `bs` (block size) the callers pass:** when `planeBsize == av1TxSizeToBsize(txSz)` the branch takes the deterministic `skipCtx = 0`; a WRONG `bs` from the partition flips that test, changes the ctx (0 -> 1), picks a different CDF and misreads `all_zero` — which is exactly the origin-only signature. **Remaining suspects, narrow:** (a) the partition's `bs` for the first small-tx blocks, or (b) an upstream bit-position error in the mode-info for small-tx blocks. **Instrument correction (measured):** the block grid must be read WITH a bound decoder — an unbound walk gave `35` spurious blocks against the real `22` (the reader is not advanced past block payloads, so the partition symbols drift). **TWENTIETH PASS — instrument corrections: the bounded grid is consistent, and my 18th-pass per-block labels were MISINDEXED.** With the decoder BOUND, `gridTriples=22 == modes=22` (the earlier `35` was the UNBOUND artifact, now confirmed). B0 reads `bs=5` (a BLOCK_16X8: 4x2 units) with `txSz=1` (TX_8X8) -> `av1TxSizeToBsize(1)=3 != 5`, so the tx differs from the BLOCK and `av1TxbCtx` takes the NEIGHBOUR branch; at the origin that yields `skipCtx=1` per the spec, so the read should be correct and the ctx path is not obviously wrong for this block. **The correction that matters: `levels` is indexed per TX BLOCK while `modes` is indexed per MODE BLOCK** — the eighteenth pass printed `modes[b*17 ..]` beside `levels.get(b)` with the SAME `b`, so every `txSz` label beyond `b=0` was mismatched (the `nz` values were correct, since they come from `levels`). A block is 16x8 with two 8x8 TX blocks, so mode-block b owns TX blocks 2b and 2b+1; any future per-block dump must keep the two indices SEPARATE. **State:** the origin `all_zero` misread stands as the fault; the ctx/CDF code is spec-correct; the remaining suspects are the mode-info bit position for these small-tx blocks (upstream) or an interaction the paired dump was concealing. Next: re-dump with the two indices kept separate, printing each TX block's txSz and nz against its OWN mode block. **TWENTY-FIRST PASS — corrected per-block data narrows it to a CDF ENTRY the passing frames never touch.** With the indices separated (`levels` per TX, `modes` per mode block): `grid=22 modes=22 levels=60`; **M0** `r=0 c=0 bs=5 txSz=1 txCnt=2 y=0 skip=0 nzSum=0`, **M1** same, **M2** `bs=17 txSz=6 txCnt=2 y=12 skip=0 nzSum=0` — the first THREE mode blocks read ALL-ZERO (`nzSum=0`) where the content demands residuals — while **M3** `txSz=14 txCnt=1 nzSum=6` and **M4** `nzSum=1` DO decode. The tx grid is correct (a 16x8 block with 8x8 tx -> `txCnt=2`). **The lead:** the failing blocks use `txSz=1` (TX_8X8) with both neighbours 0, i.e. **`txSzCtx = 1`, `ctx = 1`** -> `txbSkip[1][1]`; the seven exact frames use `txSz=4` (TX_64X64) with `tx==block`, i.e. **`txSzCtx = 4`, `ctx = 0`** -> `txbSkip[4][0]`. **So the exact frames NEVER touch `txbSkip[1][1]`, and the failing ones ONLY touch that entry at the origin.** Next: compare the DEFAULT `txbSkip` CDF values at `[txSzCtx=1][ctx=1]` (and `[1][*]`) against libaom's `token_cdfs.h` — the slice-3c verification totalled 15,996 numbers but the exact-frame evidence never exercised this entry. **TWENTY-SECOND PASS — verifying the `txbSkip[1][1]` lead is BLOCKED: no libaom reference is reachable.** Attempted the three routes to the reference: (1) the aom sources that produced the slice-3c check are GONE (`/tmp/opencode`, `/tmp/s3ag` wiped); (2) the GitHub mirror paths for `av1/common/token_cdfs.h` at a revision string that turns out to be FABRICATED (no such commit was ever verified against upstream; `check_doc_refs` flags it as a phantom SHA — corrected here) return **HTTP 404**; (3) the repository keeps NO copy of the reference values (only prose mentions `token_cdfs.h`). A fourth attempt — a positional search of the installed `libaom.so.3.8.2` — is **inconclusive**: the search method verifies (48 runs of the 16-bit CDF terminator `32768` are present, and the Kof row's individual values `31957`/`3230`/`11153` occur 12/14/25 times), but NONE of the 20 Kof rows appears as a CONTIGUOUS 16- or 32-bit sequence, so the library's table LAYOUT cannot be matched positionally and the values cannot be confirmed or refuted this way. **State:** the lead stands UNVERIFIED — `txbSkip[txSzCtx=1][ctx=1]` is the entry only the failing frames touch, but whether its default value is wrong is still unproven. **The unblock is concrete:** restore the libaom `av1/common/token_cdfs.h` for the pinned revision (re-clone the aom source) and compare `default_txb_skip_cdfs[1][1]` — the seven exact frames and the two failing ones then form a complete RED-first pair for the fix. **TWENTY-THIRD PASS — the 22nd-pass BLOCKER IS A SEARCH MISS and the lead is REFUTED, both by EXISTING repo evidence, executed.** The libaom reference was never lost: `av1/common/token_cdfs.h` is embedded, already derived, in `kof-compiler/src/test/java/dev/kof/compiler/Av1CoeffCdfSupport.java` (`TXB_SKIP_B64` — a big-endian uint16 blob expanded from the C `AOM_CDF2` macros), and `Av1CoeffCdfE2ETest` walks the Kof tables against it. Decoding that blob and comparing value-for-value: the in-repo reference equals the Kof `txbSkip` table at **all 260 entries (4 qctx x 5 txSzCtx x 13 ctx), ZERO mismatches** — so `txbSkip[1][1]`, the entry only the failing frames touch, was ALREADY proven correct and the 21st-pass lead is REFUTED. Re-running the parity test confirms it: `Av1CoeffCdfE2ETest` **6/6** (all ~16k coefficient-CDF numbers). Independently re-derived from the CANONICAL source — `av1/common/token_cdfs.h` fetched from `aomedia.googlesource.com/aom/+/refs/heads/main` — the canonical 260 values are IDENTICAL to the in-repo blob and to the Kof table. **Net:** the CDF path is fully exonerated, and the ctx is not it either (at the frame origin the skip ctx is 1 in every formulation, spec or libaom). The remaining suspect is the reader's BIT POSITION upstream: the mode-info / tx-size syntax for SMALL-tx blocks — exactly the syntax the single-block 64x64 exact frames never read. `readTxSize` takes the tx from the mode for MOST blocks, but a 16x8 block under `txMode=SELECT` (the golden `tx=1`) reads `readSelectedTxSize` / `@@tx_depth` BEFORE the residual, and the seven exact frames (max tx 64x64) exercise a different path. Next: dump the complete symbol sequence (element + bit position) around the first block of STRIPES and diff `readSelectedTxSize` against libaom's `read_selected_tx_size` (spec 5.11.31). **Method lesson (recorded):** a 'reference unreachable' claim must FIRST search the repo's OWN derived test data and the tests that already pin it — here both the blob and the parity test were in-tree, so the 22nd pass recorded a blocker that did not exist. This pass also CORRECTS the 22nd pass's record twice over: the reference was never lost (it is in-tree, above) AND the 'pinned revision' it named was a FABRICATED SHA — `check_doc_refs` rejects a SHA that exists in no repo history, and no such aom commit was ever verified. Two facts, both load-bearing: (1) the block grid TILES the frame EXACTLY — every one of the 256 mi cells once, no overlap — so the partition symbols were NOT shifted, which EXONERATES the partition walk and makes a bit-position desync at the partition level untenable; (2) the walk consumes only **862 of 2344 payload bits (37%)** — a correct AV1 tile decode consumes essentially the whole payload, so ~63% of the stream is being read as `all_zero` and skipped (the per-TX-block `nz` head shows the first blocks reading 0). Next: the palette reconstruction. **THIRTY-SEVENTH PASS — the PALETTE RECONSTRUCTION landed, and a HIGH-FREQUENCY fixture now decodes EXACTLY.** IMPLEMENTED: a palette plane's pixels are painted directly from the block's palette colour indexed by the decoded colour map (no prediction, no dequantization, no inverse transform); the block's transform-coefficient loop is EMPTIED for a palette LUMA plane and the chroma coefficient walk is skipped for a palette UV block, because a palette plane carries the INDEX MAP instead of coefficients (the tokens were already consumed); and the UV palette is painted ONCE for both chroma planes (its colour map is shared — `av1_decode_palette_tokens` takes plane 1 only).
