That is a second, self-contained failure mode, and a geometry-derived index that overflows on a 32x32 frame is a plausible partner of the 64x64 testsrc desync. The fixture is deliberately NOT committed (it throws, and a red suite is not allowed); the recipe is recorded above. (3) testsrc (64x64, 26 blocks) still fails at 1257/2344. Next: investigate the 32x32 out-of-bounds FIRST — it is smaller, deterministic and self-contained (find which array has 1156 entries and where 1186 comes from), and it may well be the same root as the 64x64 desync. **FORTIETH PASS — the 32x32 out-of-bounds is NARROWED, and the palette paint is EXONERATED by a discriminating experiment.** (1) THE ARRAY IS IDENTIFIED: `1156 = 34 * 34` — a 32x32 plane PLUS its one-sample border (stride 34 = frameW + 2) — and `1186 = 34 * 34 + 30` reads as row 34, col 30: **ONE ROW PAST the last border row** (rows 0..33). So some writer paints/sets a row beyond the bottom border of a small frame. (2) THE PALETTE PAINT IS EXONERATED, by experiment not by argument. First I implemented the obvious fix — clip the palette paint to the plane (`pbh = min(pbh, frameH - r*4)`, likewise for cols and for the chroma paint) — and re-ran: the throw PERSISTED unchanged. Then I DISABLED the palette path entirely (`pal = 0` in `AvifRealTileProbe`, so `palSizeYAt` is 0 and neither the tokens nor the paint run) and re-ran: the throw PERSISTED **IDENTICALLY** (`Index 1186 out of bounds for length 1156`). A failure that survives the removal of the suspect code cannot be caused by it, so **the palette paint is not the writer** — the overrun is in the general (non-palette) path of an edge block. (3) Both changes were REVERTED (the clip did not fix anything observable, so it is unproven; the `pal = 0` edit was a temporary discriminator) — the tree stays at the last proven state, `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **10/10**. (4) THE DEFECT, as it now stands: a 32x32 frame throws an out-of-bounds in the NON-PALETTE path — a writer one row past the bottom border — while cb16 (64x64), the minimal screen-content fixture (64x64) and testsrc (64x64) do NOT throw, so it is specific to small frames / that edge geometry. Next: find the writer — the luma `av1ReconBlockInto`/the TX loop for a bottom-edge block, or the chroma path — using the recorded discriminator (`pal = 0` in `AvifRealTileProbe` keeps the failure while removing the palette code). **FORTY-FIRST PASS — two surgical diagnostics ELIMINATE the block-level writers for the 32x32 out-of-bounds.** (1) A guard at the top of `decodeBlock` — throw with a marker when the BLOCK's pixel extent (`r*4 + av1BlockHeight(bs)`, `c*4 + av1BlockWidth(bs)`) exceeds the plane — **NEVER FIRED**: no block the walk produces extends past the frame, which eliminates the out-of-frame-block hypothesis (a desynced partition producing a block past the frame). (2) A guard before the coefficient walk — throw when a TRANSFORM BLOCK's extent (`y + h > frameH || x + w > frameW`, `x = bc << 2`, `y = br << 2` absolute) exceeds the plane — **NEVER FIRED** either. CONFIRMED with them: the failing buffer IS the luma plane with its border (`34 * 34 = 1156`, `stride = frameW + 2`) and index `1186` is 30 PAST the END of it — not merely one row past the border (row 34 does not exist). ⇒ The overrun comes from INSIDE a stage whose nominal extents are all in bounds; cb16, the minimal screen-content fixture and testsrc (all 64x64) are clean. The remaining candidates are the border-touching helpers: `av1IntraPredictBlock`'s neighbour reads, the intra EDGE FILTER, or a luma side-effect of the chroma walk. Both diagnostics reverted (temporary); tree at the proven state, `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **10/10**. Next: instrument inside `av1IntraPredictBlock` (its border reads first), then the edge filter, with the same marker technique. **FORTY-SECOND PASS — the WRITER IS NAMED: the intra PREDICTION over-reads the frame border.** A marker in the TX loop, just before `av1IntraPredictBlock`, throwing when the prediction's neighbour reach would exceed the plane plus its one-sample border (`x + 2*w > frameW + 1 || y + 2*h > frameH + 1`) **FIRED IMMEDIATELY**: `IMAGE: DIAG predict-extent x=16 y=0 w=16 h=16 r=0 c=4 bs=6` — a 16x16 block at pixels x=16..31, i.e. flush against the RIGHT edge of the 32-wide frame, whose angular prediction wants samples out to `x + 2w - 1 = 47`, THIRTY-FIVE columns past the frame. That is the over-read that lands at index 1186 in the 1156-entry luma buffer (and it explains the row-34/index math that neither the block-extent nor the TX-extent guard could see, since both stay in bounds). WHY IT IS A DEFECT AND WHAT THE FIX IS: per the AV1 spec and libaom the intra predictors do NOT read the frame directly — they read a PREPARED `AboveRow`/`LeftCol` in which every sample beyond the frame (or not yet reconstructed) is marked unavailable and handled by explicit rules, and a `get_...` clamp bounds the extents. Kof's `av1IntraPredictBlock` reads the FRAME directly with raw offsets, so an angular mode on a block flush to the right edge reads past the plane+1 border. The proper fix is that availability/clamp model (it affects the PIXELS as well as the crash), which is the next slice; a bare coordinate clamp would stop the crash but not make the prediction spec-correct. Only the 32x32 frame reaches it today: the reach needs an angular mode on a right-edge block whose top-right extent exceeds the frame, which the 64x64 fixtures do not hit. The diagnostic was TEMPORARY and is REVERTED — tree at the proven state, `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **10/10**. Next: implement the predictor's availability/clamp model (spec `AboveRow`/`LeftCol`, libaom `get_...` clamps) for the angular intra predictors. **CORRECTION (same pass) — the previous pass's "writer is the prediction" is RETRACTED, and the prediction is arithmetically EXCLUDED.** I asserted the writer from a marker that THREW before `av1IntraPredictBlock`; but the predictor **already clamps**: it takes `maxX`/`maxY` (the driver sets them to `frameW`/`frameH`) and bounds every neighbour access by `limit = min(x + 2w - 1 [when above-right is available], maxX)` — and the driver passes bordered coordinates (`x + 1`) with `maxX = frameW`, so the bound is the spec's `maxX - 1` expressed in bordered coordinates, i.e. EQUIVALENT to the spec's clamp, not a missing one. THE ARITHMETIC IS DECISIVE: with `stride = 34` and every access bounded by row 32 / col 32, the predictor's largest reachable index is `32 * 34 + 32 = 1120` — far inside the 1156-entry buffer — while the crash is index **1186 = row 34, col 30**. A component whose maximum index is 1120 cannot write 1186, so the PREDICTION IS NOT THE WRITER. My marker was also methodologically weak: it threw BEFORE the call it was meant to exonerate, so the run never reached the real crash site and the marker could not localise anything. WHAT STANDS FROM THE PREVIOUS PASS: the chroma path writes no frame buffer, so the writer is in the LUMA path; and the block-extent and TX-extent guards really do exclude a block or TX block past the frame. Next, with the method fixed: a marker TRAIL that PRINTS (rather than throwing) at each candidate writer, so the LAST line before the crash names the writer — candidates, in order: `av1ReconBlockInto`, the intra EDGE FILTER (`av1ApplyIntraEdgeFilter`), the filter-intra prediction, and the coefficient walk. A writer that can reach row 34 needs either an offset beyond the block/TX extent or a wrong stride (e.g. indexing the plane with `frameW` where the buffer's stride is `frameW + 2`). **FORTY-FOURTH PASS — the overrun is NEUTRALISED by padding, and the 32x32 frame then shows the SAME DESYNC as the 64x64 testsrc.** EXPERIMENT: instead of a marker trail I gave the probe's frame buffer EXTRA ROWS (`new Int[stride * (mh + 8)]`, stride unchanged at `frameW + 2`) so a row-34 write lands INSIDE the buffer, and re-ran the 32x32 fixture. The crash DISAPPEARED and the decode COMPLETED — which confirms the failure really is a write at row 34 into a 34-row buffer, and that the whole pipeline is otherwise able to run. WHAT THE COMPLETED DECODE SHOWS, and it is the important part: `blocks=8 covered=64 frame=64 overlap=0` (the block grid still tiles the frame) but **`symBits=503 payloadBits=1968` — only 26% of the payload consumed**, i.e. the SAME DESYNC as the 64x64 testsrc (54%), with the first sample `Y (0,0)=17` MATCHING ffmpeg. So the 32x32 frame has TWO things: a DESYNC (the entropy is not in sync) and an OVERRUN that the desync drives — a garbage mode/geometry making some component write past the bordered plane. The overrun is a SYMPTOM; the shared root is the DESYNC. PROBE LIMITATION recorded: the tile probe's Y sample positions are hardcoded for a 64x64 frame, so on a 32x32 fixture only `(0,0)` is meaningful (the others read out of range and print 0). Both temporary changes were REVERTED — tree at the proven state, `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **10/10**. Next: the DESYNC itself, since it is now the shared root of testsrc-32 and testsrc-64 while cb8, cb16 and the minimal screen-content fixture are EXACT. The discriminator is CONTENT: testsrc mixes many angular intra modes over a colour-bar pattern, the exact fixtures are flat/checker/palette. Concrete probe: dump the per-block `yMode`/`uvMode`/`angleY` of a failing testsrc frame and look for a mode or angle the exact fixtures never exercise (a mis-read angular mode would desync exactly this way and would also explain the edge-case overrun). **FORTY-FIFTH PASS — the DISCRIMINATOR IS MEASURED: the failing frames read ANGLE DELTAS, the exact ones never do.** SLICE: the tile probe's per-block dump now carries `angleY` and `angleUV` (it printed `yMode`/`uvMode` only), so the mode mix of any fixture can be read off directly. MEASURED, per block as `(txSz, yMode, angleY, uvMode, angleUV)`: testsrc-64 (FAILS) — `(6,2,-2,2,3)`, `(6,0,0,9,0)`, `(6,0,0,13,0)`, `(5,2,2,9,0)`, `(5,2,0,13,0)`, `(7,0,0,0,0)`, `(2,0,0,13,0)` … i.e. ANGULAR luma modes (yMode 2 = V_PRED) with NON-ZERO angle deltas (−2, +2), directional UV modes (uvMode 9), CFL (13), and a **7-symbol read in the trace = the angle-delta symbol**. cb16 (EXACT) — `(2,0,0,0,0)` four times: DC only, every angle 0, and **ZERO 7-symbol reads**. pskip (EXACT) — `(3,0,0,0,0)`: DC only, every angle 0, **ZERO 7-symbol reads**. ⇒ **Every exact fixture never executes the DIRECTIONAL/ANGLE path; the failing frames do.** That is the first discriminator that separates the fixtures on an ENTROPY element (mode/angle reads) rather than on content alone. CAVEAT: the trace line prints only its first 24 symbols, so the "7-symbol read" count is a partial view (the MODE dump is complete, and it is the mode dump that carries the contrast). NEXT: isolate whether the trigger is the ANGLE DELTA specifically or the directional path in general. Recipe: encode a 64x64 frame whose structure forces an OFF-AXIS gradient (so libaom picks a directional mode with a NON-ZERO angle delta) but whose content is otherwise simple, and decode it — if it fails while the on-axis gradient (which was EXACT) passes, the angle-delta read is the trigger and a RED-first fix follows. Note the angle read itself was already verified against libaom in an earlier pass (7 symbols, `raw - 3`, gated on `bs >= BLOCK_8X8 && isDirectionalMode`), so the suspect is not the shape of the read but something it interacts with (its CDF adaptation across the tile, or the UV `get_uv_mode` mapping for the angle row). **FORTY-SIXTH PASS — the intra EDGE FILTER is EXCLUDED as the overrun's writer, and the remaining candidate is the angular-prediction path itself.** DISCRIMINATOR (same technique that worked before): I forced the frame's `enable_intra_edge_filter` to be ignored (`de = 1` in the probe, so the edge filter is never applied) and re-ran the 32x32 fixture. The crash PERSISTED with the IDENTICAL index — `Index 1186 out of bounds for length 1156` — so the edge filter is not the writer. WHAT HAS NOW BEEN EXCLUDED for the 32x32 overrun, each by experiment or arithmetic: the PALETTE paint (`pal = 0` still throws); a BLOCK past the frame (guard never fired); a TRANSFORM BLOCK past the frame (guard never fired); the PLAIN intra prediction (every access bounded by `maxX`/`maxY` ⇒ max index `32*34 + 32 = 1120`); the CHROMA path (writes no frame buffer at all); and now the intra EDGE FILTER (`de = 1` still throws). QUALIFICATION of the earlier arithmetic, to keep it honest: the bound `1120` covers the predictor's PLAIN neighbour reads. `av1IntraPredictBlock` also takes `useFilterIntra`/`filterIntraMode` and has a FILTER-INTRA branch whose reads are NOT covered by that bound, so "the prediction is excluded" holds only for the non-filter-intra path. WHAT THAT LEAVES, and it is consistent: the FILTER-INTRA prediction (its 2-3 sample border) or the luma prediction's edge/copy into the plane — BOTH ARE ANGULAR-ONLY paths, which matches the fact that the overrun appears ONLY in the failing (angular) fixtures while cb16, pskip and cb8 are non-angular and clean. testsrc reads `use_filter_intra` (`2:1` has been seen in its traces) while the exact fixtures do not. Next: force `useFilterIntra = 0` in the probe as the next one-shot discriminator (`de`-style) and re-run the 32x32 — if the crash disappears, the filter-intra branch is the writer and the fix is bounded to it. Tree at the proven state, `AvifRealFileE2ETest` 10/10. **FORTY-SEVENTH PASS — the FILTER-INTRA branch is EXCLUDED too, and the index arithmetic now says the writer is NOT using the block/TX convention at all.** DISCRIMINATOR: forced `useFilterIntra = 0` at the driver's prediction call and re-ran the 32x32 — the crash PERSISTED with the IDENTICAL index (`Index 1186 out of bounds for length 1156`). So the filter-intra branch is not the writer either. THE EXCLUSION SET for this overrun is now: the PALETTE paint, a BLOCK past the frame, a TRANSFORM BLOCK past the frame, the PLAIN prediction (arithmetic bound 1120), the CHROMA path, the intra EDGE FILTER, and the FILTER-INTRA branch. REFINED READ OF THE INDEX — and this is the useful part: with `stride = 34` the crash reads as row 34, col 30, i.e. a row TWO past the last interior row (31) and one past the bottom border (33). But the block-extent guard proved that NO block satisfies `y + h > frameH` (32), so a writer using the block convention `(x, y, w, h)` with `+'1'`-bordered coordinates can reach at most `32*34 + 32 = 1120`. A writer that reaches 1186 therefore uses EITHER A DIFFERENT STRIDE (e.g. `frameW = 32` where the buffer's stride is `frameW + 2 = 34`, which reads 1186 as row 37 col 2) OR an offset outside the block/TX extent. So the search should stop looking for a block-geometry error and look for a STRIDE or BASE-OFFSET error. METHOD FOR THE NEXT PASS — better than one-shot discriminators, which have now been exhausted: use the 44th pass's trick to make the decode COMPLETE (pad the frame with extra rows so the overrun lands inside the buffer), and then instrument every luma writer to RECORD its maximum touched index into a diagnostic list that the probe PRINTS after the decode. Because the decode no longer crashes, the print survives (stdout is LOST on a crash, which is why the marker-trail idea failed earlier), and the writer whose max exceeds 1156 is NAMED. That is the decisive method. Tree at the proven state, `AvifRealFileE2ETest` 10/10. **FORTY-EIGHTH PASS — a SHARPER hypothesis from data already on hand: the failing fixtures use CFL, and CFL is the chroma path's ONLY luma READ.** FROM THE 45th-PASS DUMP (already in the plan), the mode mixes are: testsrc-64 has `uvMode = 13` entries — `(6,0,0,13,0)`, `(5,2,0,13,0)`, `(2,0,0,13,0)` — while cb16 is `(2,0,0,0,0)` ×4 and pskip is `(3,0,0,0,0)`: **`uvMode = 13` is CFL, and it appears ONLY in the failing fixture**. cb8 is also exact and CANNOT use CFL: it is a 64x64 block, and `is_cfl_allowed` requires both dimensions ≤ 32. So the set of fixtures that fail is exactly the set that can use CFL. WHY THIS MATTERS MECHANICALLY, and it fits the stride/offset conclusion of the previous pass: the chroma plane walk is the only place that READS the luma plane, through `av1CflAcFromLuma(luma, lumaStride, ...)` (Av1DecodeChroma.kf:184) — the CFL average/subtract. A bounds or offset error in that read would (a) overrun the luma buffer when the block sits at an edge, and (b) it is in the PIXEL path, so it cannot be caught by any of the entropy-side discriminators tried so far. It also explains why the exact fixtures are clean: they never execute it. STATED AS A HYPOTHESIS, NOT A CONCLUSION — the 42nd pass taught the cost of naming a writer from an indirect signal: what is MEASURED is that CFL is present only in the failing fixtures and that the chroma walk is the only luma reader; the causal claim needs the test below. NEXT, one-shot and decisive (same pattern as `pal = 0`, `de = 1`, `useFilterIntra = 0`): neutralise the CFL branch — make the UV prediction plain (skip the `av1CflAcFromLuma` modulation) — and re-run the 32x32. If the crash AND the desync disappear, CFL is the trigger and the fix is bounded to that read. If only the crash disappears, CFL causes the overrun and the desync is a separate defect. **FORTY-NINTH PASS — the CFL hypothesis is REFUTED by its own test, and the failure of every call-site exclusion is itself the finding.** THE TEST, run as planned: `if (uvMode == 13)` was neutralised in `Av1DecodeChroma.kf`, which skips BOTH the modulation AND the `av1CflAcFromLuma` call — the chroma path's only luma READ, so the entire CFL luma access is off — while leaving the ENTROPY untouched (the uv mode and cfl alphas are still read), which keeps the desync measurement meaningful. Result: the 32x32 STILL throws the IDENTICAL `Index 1186 out of bounds for length 1156`. **CFL is not the writer.** PROCESS NOTE worth keeping: the previous pass labelled that claim a HYPOTHESIS rather than a conclusion, so this refutation cost nothing to correct — the plan needed no retraction this time. That is the difference the labelling makes. THE EXCLUSION LIST is now complete for every NAMED luma access: the PALETTE paint; a BLOCK past the frame; a TRANSFORM BLOCK past the frame; the PLAIN prediction (arithmetic bound 1120); the CHROMA plane writes (a different buffer); the intra EDGE FILTER; the FILTER-INTRA branch; and now the CFL luma read and modulation. AND THAT IS THE FINDING: **every named luma access has been excluded, yet the crash persists — so at least one exclusion rests on the PARAMETERS A CALLER PASSES, not on the accesses actually performed.** Each elimination above checked a caller's inputs (`pal = 0`, the extent guards, `de`, `useFilterIntra`, the uv-mode gate) or the helper's arithmetic as read; a helper whose own internal index arithmetic goes out of range while its inputs are all in bounds would survive every one of them. THEREFORE the next instrument must measure ACTUAL TOUCHED INDICES, not parameters — the 47th pass's plan, with one refinement this pass forces: the recording must live INSIDE THE ACCESSORS (the helpers), not at the call sites, because the call-site layers are now provably clean. Concretely: pad the frame so the decode COMPLETES, have each luma accessor update a shared `maxIndex` diagnostic on every access, and print it after the decode (stdout survives because there is no crash). **FIFTIETH PASS — a PROOF narrows the overrun to a STRIDE/BASE error, and the plumbing shows where one could hide.** THE PROOF (arithmetic, in the driver's own 34-stride convention for a 32x32 frame): (a) the intra prediction CANNOT exceed index 1120 — its above reads are clamped by `limit = min(x + 2w - 1 when above-right is available, maxX)` with `maxX = frameW`, and its left reads by the analogous `maxY`, so every access is `row <= 32, col <= 32` ⇒ `32*34 + 32 = 1120`; (b) the reconstruction CANNOT either — `av1ReconBlockInto` writes `plane[(y + i) * stride + (x + j)]` with `i < h`, `j < w` inside a block that the extent guards proved satisfies `y + h <= frameH`, so the same bound `1120` applies. Two conclusions follow, and they are decisive for the search: first, NO accessor using the block convention with stride 34 can produce index 1186, so the crash REQUIRES a different (larger) stride or a different base offset; second, every call-site guard I wrote was structurally incapable of finding it, because none of them examines the STRIDE an accessor uses — which is exactly the failure mode the previous pass identified. A CONCRETE AUDIT TARGET, from the plumbing: the chroma path carries BOTH the plane's own buffer and the luma's — `av1DecodeChromaPlane(Int[] frame, Int stride, Int frameW, Int frameH, Int[] luma, Int lumaStride, …)` (Av1DecodeChroma.kf:77) — so it has TWO `(buffer, stride)` pairs and TWO dimension pairs in scope at once. Any place that pairs one plane's buffer with the OTHER's stride or dimensions produces exactly a stride error that (i) stays in bounds for some block positions, (ii) overruns for others, and (iii) is invisible to every check made at the call site. That is where to look next. **FIFTY-FIRST PASS — the audit CLEARS the chroma plumbing, and the result is a HARD CONTRADICTION that only an index-level instrument can resolve.** AUDIT RESULT (negative, and that is a result): `av1DecodeChromaBlock` calls `av1DecodeChromaPlane(chroma.u, chroma.strideU, chroma.wU, chroma.hU, luma, lumaStride, …, lumaFrameW, lumaFrameH, …)` for U and the same for V. The chroma plane receives its OWN `(frame, stride, w, h)` and the luma's `(luma, lumaStride, lumaFrameW, lumaFrameH)` as SEPARATE parameters — there is no place where one plane's buffer is paired with the other's stride or dimensions. The 50th pass's audit target is therefore CLEARED, not confirmed. THE CONTRADICTION, stated plainly: (a) the intra prediction's accesses are bounded by `maxX`/`maxY` ⇒ index ≤ 1120; (b) the reconstruction's writes are within the TX extents, which are within blocks the extent guards proved satisfy `y + h <= frameH` ⇒ index ≤ 1120; (c) the palette paint and the CFL luma read are EXCLUDED by direct experiment (`pal = 0` and the uv-13 neutralisation both still crash); (d) the chroma plane's own accesses are bounded by its own dims into a 324-entry buffer. Yet the crash is index 1186 in a 1156-entry buffer. **One of those premises must be wrong**, and the only way to find out which is to observe the actual access rather than reason about the code. THE INSTRUMENT IS THEREFORE NO LONGER OPTIONAL — it is the only remaining method. Concretely, and now with the contradiction as the target: record `(index, stride, bufferLength)` at EVERY frame access inside the accessors, print the maximum after a COMPLETED decode (pad the frame so it does not crash), and label each record with its accessor. Either a record will show an `index > bufferLength` (naming the accessor AND the stride it believed in, resolving the contradiction), or NO record will exceed the length — in which case the crashing access is in code the instrumentation did not cover, and the list of instrumented sites becomes the map of what is left. **FIFTY-SECOND PASS — the overrun is a READ, not a write: with the buffer padded the decode COMPLETES and the tail is CLEAN.** THE EXPERIMENT: pad the probe's frame to `mh + 12` rows (44 * 34 = 1496 entries, well past the crashing 1186), let the decode complete, and SCAN every cell from `stride * (mh + 2)` (index 1156, the end of the legitimate plane+border) upward for non-zero values. Result: **`TAIL n=0`** — not one cell beyond the legitimate region was touched. WHAT THAT PROVES, and it is decisive: a WRITE past the plane cannot be what crashes the unpadded run, because the same code writes nothing there when the buffer is large enough. The crashing access must therefore be a **READ**. That single fact explains the whole shape of this search: every instrument I built was write-oriented — the extent guards compared a block's footprint, the padding experiment looked for stray writes, and the call-site gates (`pal = 0`, `de = 1`, `useFilterIntra = 0`) all suppress code paths without ever observing an access — so none of them could see a read. It also explains the 51st pass's contradiction: reads are unbounded by 'the writer's own extent', so a premise about WRITES says nothing about them. CONSEQUENTLY the exclusion list must be re-examined for READS specifically. The readers of the luma plane are: `av1IntraPredictBlock` (whose plain reads are bounded by `maxX`/`maxY` ⇒ 1120, so either that bound is not what I think or the read is in a branch I turned off by a gate that does not gate the READ), the intra EDGE FILTER, the FILTER-INTRA branch, and the CFL read (`av1CflAcFromLuma` — excluded by a test that neutralised the whole `if (uvMode == 13)` block, so this one really is off). Note the asymmetry that matters: for the CFL test I removed the CALL, whereas for the edge filter and filter-intra I only changed FLAGS — and a flag that the reader ignores leaves the read in place. Next, the READ-detecting instrument (a SENTINEL, not a scan): pad the frame, fill the tail with a distinctive value, and check whether the decode's OUTPUT changes when that value changes. If it does, a read reaches the tail; bisecting which tail region changes the output bounds the read's extent, and the value found tells which helper produced it. That instrument cannot be fooled by a flag. **FIFTY-THIRD PASS — RESOLVED: the "32x32 overrun" was MY OWN PROBE. The decoder never crashed.** THE SENTINEL FOUND IT: padding the frame and filling the tail with 111 made the printed sample read `3263=111` — the sentinel appearing in the OUTPUT. The sample at `(32,63)` is HARDCODED for a 64x64 frame, so on a 32x32 frame the probe read `frame[(32 + 1) * 34 + (63 + 1)]` = **index 1186** of a 1156-entry plane-plus-border buffer: exactly the "crash index" this lane has chased for eight passes. WHY EVERY INFERENCE WAS UNSOUND, and this is the lesson worth keeping: the probe prints its summary BEFORE the Y samples, so the DECODE had already completed when the print threw — and because `runJvm` returns stdout only on success, a throw loses the whole capture, which is why the summary was never seen. Every statement of the form "the crash happened before/after stage X" was therefore built on evidence that could not exist. THE FIX (landed): the tile probe now CLAMPS its Y sample positions to the frame (`sx1 = min(32, mw - 1)`, likewise for 63 and the row), which is a NO-OP for the 64x64 fixtures — their goldens are byte-identical — and in-range for smaller ones. The 32x32 fixture is now COMMITTED with a regression test: `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **11/11**, and that test would crash again if the clamp regressed. WHAT REMAINS REAL, and it is the actual target: the 32x32 frame decodes to `blocks=8 covered=64 frame=64` with **`symBits=503 payloadBits=1968` — a DESYNC of 26%**, of the same family as testsrc-64's 54% (its first pixel, `Y (0,0)=17`, matches ffmpeg). So there was never a decoder overrun to find: the 47th–52nd passes' hunt for a stride/base writer was chasing the harness, and the 52nd pass's "it is a READ, not a write" was RIGHT — the read simply came from the harness, not the decoder. THE REAL REMAINING QUESTION is unchanged and now unobstructed: why do the testsrc frames consume only 26%/54% of their payload while cb8, cb16, the minimal screen-content fixture and the solid frame are exact? **FIFTY-FOURTH PASS — the CFL ALPHA path is EXONERATED against libaom's own macros; the testsrc desync is not there either.** HYPOTHESIS TESTED (the sharpest one left, because CFL is present only in the failing fixtures): Kof's CFL alpha CDF row selection looked wrong on a first reading — `cflAlphaStore.get((signU - 1) * 3 + signV)` for U and `(signV - 1) * 3 + signU` for V — against my recollection that libaom used `joint_sign / 3` and `3 + joint_sign % 3`. REFUTED by reading the actual macros in `av1/common/enums.h`: `#define CFL_CONTEXT_U(js) (js + 1 - CFL_SIGNS)` and `#define CFL_CONTEXT_V(js) (CFL_SIGN_V(js) * CFL_SIGNS + CFL_SIGN_U(js) - CFL_SIGNS)`. With `CFL_SIGNS = 3`, `CFL_SIGN_U(js) = (js + 1) / 3` and `CFL_SIGN_V(js) = (js + 1) % 3`, the U row is `js - 2` and the V row is `(signV - 1) * 3 + signU` — and a joint-sign sweep shows Kof's `(signU - 1) * 3 + signV` equals `js - 2` for EVERY js with `signU != 0` (0..5 at js = 2..7), while Kof's V row is libaom's V row verbatim. The row and symbol counts also agree (`cflSignStore` 8 = CFL_JOINT_SIGNS; the alpha 16 = CFL_ALPHABET_SIZE) and the read conditions agree (`signU != 0` / `signV != 0` ≡ `CFL_SIGN_U/V(js) != CFL_SIGN_ZERO`). LESSON worth recording: this is the THIRD time a memory-based comparison produced a false lead (the scan order, the partition SPLIT branch, now the CFL rows) — in every case the code was right and my recollection of libaom wrong, and only fetching the actual source settled it. For this lane: NEVER compare against remembered libaom; FETCH it. STATE OF THE DESYNC'S SUSPECTS: every entropy path the failing fixtures exercise and the exact ones do not is now verified against libaom — the angle deltas (7 symbols, `raw - 3`, the directional gate), the filter-intra gate (`maxDim <= 32`), the palette mode/size reads, and now the CFL joint sign and alphas. The desync is therefore not any mode element I can name from the mode mix; the next instrument must compare the decoded SYMBOL SEQUENCE against something external rather than inspect individual fields. Next: extend the independent Python symbol decoder (31st pass) into the CFL/mode region of a failing frame — it already reproduces Kof's first symbols from the raw stream, so feeding it the same CDF rows Kof uses and walking further is the only step that distinguishes a correct-but-wrong-value decode from a desync, with no memory-based comparison. **FIFTY-FIFTH PASS — a per-block Y instrument LOCALISES the fault to the FIRST ANGULAR BLOCK.** INSTRUMENT LANDED: the tile probe now prints, for each of the first 12 blocks, the reconstructed Y at the block's own 4x4 origin (`BLKY (r,c)=Y ...`), pairing the walk's `decode_block` steps with the frame. Because it is emitted from the frame the driver produced, it can be matched pixel-for-pixel against ffmpeg's decode of the same fixture. MEASURED, testsrc-64 (`avif_real_64.avif`), Kof against ffmpeg at the block origins: - block 0 (0,0): `skip 0, txSz 7, y=0, angY 0, uv=0` -> Kof **16**, ffmpeg **16** — **EXACT**. The first block is now CORRECT (the 29th pass measured 128 against 16 here; the palette work since then fixed it). - block 1 (0,2): `skip 0, txSz 1, y=7 (D203), angY=-1, uv=7, angUV=2` -> Kof **16**, ffmpeg **81** — **THE FIRST DIVERGENCE**. - block 2 (0,4): `y=0, uv=13` -> 192 vs 145. Block 3 (4,0): `y=0, uv=0` -> 235 vs 16. THE FINDING, and it is the sharpest localisation this front has had: the first block (DC on both planes) is exact, and the first block that diverges is the FIRST BLOCK TO USE THE ANGULAR PATH — yMode 7 (D203), a NON-ZERO angle delta (-1), and a directional UV mode. That is exactly the entropy discriminator measured in the 45th pass (the exact fixtures never read an angle delta), now pinned to a specific block. WHAT THAT NARROWS, given the entropy verification already done: block 1 is NOT an all-zero skip (its transform block 2 reads `nz=2`), and every entropy element it uses — the y-mode CDF, the angle-delta read (7 symbols, `raw - 3`, the directional gate), the UV mode and its angle — has been verified against libaom (fetching the source, not memory). So what remains for block 1 is the PIXEL path for an angular mode: the prediction from its neighbours, the angle's application, or the edge filter. Next: dump block 1's PREDICTION array (before the residual) and compare it against a hand-computed directional prediction from its neighbours — the pixel-path successor of the entropy verification. **FIFTY-SIXTH PASS — the coefficient decode yields plausible values, so the residual question is now specific: is block 1's residual APPLIED?** INSTRUMENT EXTENSION (landed): the probe now also dumps the first three NON-EMPTY transform blocks as `LEVELS tx<i>:<first 10 coefficient values>`. MEASURED on testsrc-64: `tx2:[3,0,0,...]`, `tx5:[1,1,0,...]`, `tx6:[1,0,3,0,0,-1,1,-1,1,-1]`. So the coefficient decode produces non-trivial values with mixed signs — it is not collapsing everything to zero, and cb8/cb16/pskip/solid remain exact. THE QUESTION THIS POSES, stated with its uncertainty: block 1 reconstructs to exactly its prediction (16) while ffmpeg's value there is 81, and non-zero coefficients are decoded nearby (tx2 carries a DC of 3). A DC of 3 times the dequant factor at this qindex would move the reconstructed value substantially, so EITHER block 1's own residual is decoded but not added, OR the `tx<i>` indices in this dump do not map onto the mode block I think they do. The second possibility is a KNOWN trap on this front: an earlier pass paired a per-TX index with a per-mode-block index and mislabelled every block after the first. So the mapping must be pinned before any conclusion. NEXT, and it is now a bounded, mechanical step: pin the TX-to-mode-block mapping in the dump — the driver emits `levels` in the order of its own per-block transform enumeration, and the walk's `decode_block` steps give the block origins, so the two can be paired explicitly (the walk's step order vs the driver's per-block `txCnt`) — then report, for block 1 specifically, its prediction, its dequantized residual and its reconstructed value against the reference-implied 81 (whose prediction, if the DC-only block-0 column of 16s is the left neighbour, is plausibly 16, leaving a residual of about 65 to account for). Tree unchanged, `AvifRealFileE2ETest` 10/10. **FIFTY-SEVENTH PASS — the TX-to-MODE-BLOCK mapping is PINNED, and it DEAD-ENDS the 56th pass's alternative: block 1's residual IS decoded, yet its reconstruction is still bare.** The probe's per-block line now prints each sampled block's `bs` (`BLKY (r,c,bs<k>)=Y`). MEASURED on testsrc-64: `(0,0,bs4)=16 (0,2,bs4)=16 (0,4,bs6)=192 (4,0,bs4)=235 (4,2,bs4)=151 (4,4,bs16)=223 ... (0,8,bs9)=192 (8,0,bs4)=151 (8,2,bs4)=149`. Read in the BLOCK_SIZES_ALL order (0=BLOCK_4X4, 1=4X8, 2=8X4, 3=8X8, **4=BLOCK_8X16**, 5=16X8, 6=BLOCK_16X16, 9=BLOCK_16X32): block 0 `(0,0)` is `BLOCK_8X16` with `txSz=7` (TX_8X16) ⇒ tx size == block size ⇒ exactly ONE transform block, and it is `tx0` (nz=0) — so block 0's value 16 is its prediction alone, and the block-0 column of 16s is the left neighbour. Block 1 `(0,2)` is ALSO `BLOCK_8X16`, but with `txSz=1` (**TX_8X8**) ⇒ an 8x16 block on an 8x8 grid holds **TWO** transform blocks ⇒ **block 1 owns tx1 AND tx2**. `tx2` is the FIRST NON-EMPTY block (`[3,0,0,...]`, a DC of 3). So the 56th pass's second branch is eliminated on measurement: block 1 DOES carry a decoded residual. **Yet** its reconstruction at the sampled pixel — which lies inside block 1's FIRST (top) 8x8 transform block, the one reading `all_zero=1` — is exactly 16, where ffmpeg's frame reads 81. **Consequence, stated with its uncertainty:** either the top TX block's `all_zero=1` is a MISREAD, or block 1's prediction is itself wrong (16 is the flat left-neighbour value that a DC-like predictor yields, not the D203 directional prediction its mode/angle imply). The read CANNOT be a table error: the context is the both-zero branch (ctx=1) selecting `txbSkip[1][1]`, and all 260 `txbSkip` entries were verified entry-for-entry against libaom (the 22nd pass). So a misread would mean the reader POSITION is already wrong BEFORE this read — i.e. the desync sits UPSTREAM of block 1's coefficient read, in the partition/mode-info reads that precede it (block 0's mode info costs 77 bits by the probe's own `posMI`/`posEnd` deltas, which is worth re-examining next). TEN exploratory quantity-dumps were run to reach this (a `bs`-tagged BLKY sweep, a per-block `txCnt` print, a prediction-only pass) and are adjudicated here; the temporary `tmpBs` test used to obtain the `bs` column was REMOVED before the commit, the `bs` column itself kept as a permanent instrument improvement. NEXT, still bounded and mechanical: sample block 1 at its SECOND (bottom) 8x8 transform block — where tx2's DC of 3 actually lands — and dump block 1's PREDICTION explicitly (left-neighbour vs directional), the two measurements splitting 'prediction wrong' from 'residual applied to the wrong position' without a further table audit. Tree at the proven state, `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **11/11**. THE INSTRUMENT, refined by the proof: record `(index, stride, bufferLength)` at each frame access INSIDE the accessors (not at the call sites), with the frame padded so the decode COMPLETES and the post-decode print survives; an access whose `index` exceeds its own `bufferLength` names both the site and the stride it believed in. That is the decisive method. Tree at the proven state, `AvifRealFileE2ETest` 10/10. **TWENTY-SEVENTH PASS — the SCAN ORDER is pinned to the SPEC, EXONERATED, and a FALSE-BUG TRAP is recorded.** The scan order was the best remaining suspect for a large-EOB desync (it fixes both the level-walk positions and every coefficient context), and it was COMPLETELY UNTESTED. It is now pinned by a new suite, `Av1ScanOrderE2ETest` (`Av1ScanOrderSupport` + the resource `av1_scan_order_golden.txt.b64`): the Kof probe dumps the default, mrow and mcol scan of every transform size (19 default rows + the 9 sizes whose mrow/mcol the spec defines) and the golden is the SPEC §9.3 (`Default_Scan_*`, `Mrow_Scan_*`, `Mcol_Scan_*`). Proof: **2/2 (JVM + Script), BUILD SUCCESS** — every one of the 14 spec default scans and the 9 spec mrow/mcol scans equals the Kof generator output. **The trap (recorded because I nearly landed a false bug): libaom's `av1/common/scan.c` tables are the TRANSPOSE of the spec's** — libaom stores the coefficient buffer in a different layout, so its `default_scan_4x4` is `{0,4,1,2,5,8,...}` where the spec (and Kof) have `{0,1,4,8,5,2,...}`; every entry differs. Comparing Kof to libaom's scan tables therefore 'finds' a divergence on the whole table, including an apparent row/col swap on the rectangular sizes, and it is entirely an artifact. For scan order, libaom is NOT a valid oracle; the spec is. (A second consequence: the spec lists no mrow/mcol above 16x16 because a 1D transform type is not allowed there.) With the scan order verified, the coefficient PATH is now exonerated as far as tables go (CDFs 23rd–24th passes, walk order 3x, scan order here). Next: instrument the reader position PER BLOCK (mode-info start/end, the `all_zero` position) to find the first block that under-reads. **TWENTY-EIGHTH PASS — the CONSUMPTION INSTRUMENT is validated, and the coefficient-CONTEXT tables are confirmed against the SPEC (not libaom).** (1) INSTRUMENT VALIDATION (executed): corrupting a byte in the UNREAD tail (byte 285) leaves the walk dump IDENTICAL to the baseline (`blocks=21 covered=256 frame=256 overlap=0 symBits=862 ... nzHead=0,0,26,8,...`), while corrupting a CONSUMED byte (byte 10) changes it (`blocks` 21 -> 25, `symBits` 862 -> 1302). So `Av1Symbol.position()` is a consumed counter, the decoder really stops ~63% short of the tile, and the 26th pass's premise holds. This is recorded in the instrument's javadoc. (2) SPEC VERIFICATION of the coefficient-context tables (`Av1CoeffCtx.kf`), motivated by the libaom-transposition trap the previous pass exposed (the module header had verified `Coeff_Base_Ctx_Offset` against libaom's `av1_nz_map_ctx_offset`): re-checked against the SPEC §10 — `Coeff_Base_Ctx_Offset[19][5][5]` **475/475**, `Sig_Ref_Diff_Offset[3][5][2]` **30/30**, `Mag_Ref_Offset_With_Tx_Class[3][3][2]` **18/18**, `Coeff_Base_Pos_Ctx_Offset[3]` = {26,31,36} — all EXACT. No transposition artifact here. The existing `Av1CoeffCtxE2ETest` already re-derives every context from the spec §9.3.3/§10 in an independent plain-Java reader, so this corroborates rather than adds coverage — re-run for proof: **6/6, BUILD SUCCESS**. **Net:** the coefficient path is now exonerated against the SPEC at every table it touches (CDFs, tx-size, contexts, scan) and in syntax order; the sole discriminating datum remains the 37% payload consumption. Next: instrument the reader position PER BLOCK (mode-info start/end, the `all_zero` position) to find the first block that under-reads. **FIFTY-EIGHTH PASS — the prediction/reconstruction diagnostic is LANDED, it CORRECTS the 57th pass's mapping, and it ANSWERS its question: the branch is a MISREAD of `all_zero`, not a wrong prediction.** LANDED: `Av1TileDecode.predBlocks()` (`libs/image/Av1DecodeTileRecon.kf`) — additive, `predLog` initialized in the constructor exactly like `posOut` (NO signature change), recording 7 ints per TRANSFORM block: the TX index, the mode block's `(r, c)`, the TX block's pixel origin `(x, y)`, the PREDICTED sample at that origin and the RECONSTRUCTED sample there after the residual. The probe prints `PREDBLK ...` and `realTileWalkDoesNotReadPastThePayloadOnJvm` pins that it is wired. MEASURED (testsrc-64): `PREDBLK tx0(r0,c2)+8,0 p=16/r=16 tx1(r0,c2)+8,8 p=235/r=235 tx2(r0,c4)+16,0 p=192/r=192 tx3(r4,c2)+8,16 p=151/r=151 tx4(r4,c2)+8,24 p=205/r=205 tx5(r4,c4)+16,16 p=222/r=223 ...`, with `txb=62 nzTot=103 symBits=1257 payloadBits=2344`. **CORRECTION of the 57th pass (recorded as a correction, not a silent edit):** the pairing is now DIRECT rather than inferred, and it shows tx0 AND tx1 belong to mode block 1 `(r0,c2)` at origins `(8,0)` and `(8,8)` — NOT tx1+tx2 as the 57th pass inferred. Block 0 contributes NO transform block at all: its row is `B 0 77 77 0 7 0 0 0 0` (mode-info end == residual end, so no residual bits) yet `BLKY (0,0,bs4)=16`, and a neighbourless DC_PRED would predict 128, not 16 ⇒ **block 0 is a PALETTE block** (a fact this front had never recorded; it also explains why block 0's mode info costs 77 bits — the palette tokens live there). The 57th pass's CONCLUSION survives (block 1 owns TWO transform blocks); its INDICES shift by one. **THE ANSWER:** both of block 1's TX blocks read `all_zero=1` (nz=0), so there recon == pred — the top TX predicts 16, the bottom 235. ffmpeg's reference over block 1's TOP TX region (x 8..15, y 0..7) is FLAT 81, while block 0 (the left neighbour) is FLAT 16 and the above row is unavailable; a directional prediction (block 1's `yMode=7` = D203_PRED, `angleY=-1`) from FLAT neighbours can only be FLAT, so the reference's flat 81 CANNOT be prediction-only ⇒ a residual of about +65 is MISSING there. So the surviving branch is the MISREAD of `all_zero`, NOT a wrong prediction — and because the context (both-zero → `txbSkip[1][1]`) and its CDF were verified entry-for-entry against libaom, the reader POSITION is wrong AT that read. **A SECOND, independent anomaly:** the bottom TX's prediction is 235, which NO flat-16 neighbourhood can produce — the bottom TX shares the same flat-16 left column AND has its own top TX (reconstructed 16) directly above it, so it too should predict about 16. 235 is the frame's WHITE value and appears elsewhere (`BLKY (4,0,bs4)=235` = pixel (0,16)), so the prediction path is reading the WRONG reference samples for the second transform block of an 8x16 block. The fault is therefore NOT one thing: an entropy-position error at block 1's `all_zero` AND a pixel-path error in the bottom TX's prediction. PROOF/TREE: `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **11/11** (the temp probe test used to capture the dump was REMOVED before the commit); `mvn -pl kof-compiler -am compile` rc=0. NEXT, bounded: dump the predictor's reference samples (the above row and left column it actually reads) for block 1's BOTTOM TX — one dump splits 'wrong row read' from 'wrong availability', and it is the pixel-path twin of the `all_zero` position question. **FIFTY-NINTH PASS — the PIXEL anomalies are EXONERATED as an independent defect: they are a SIGNATURE of the desync.** Two probe diagnostics landed: `PREDTEST` (reproduces block 1's BOTTOM transform block — 8x8 at pixel origin (8,8) — by calling `av1IntraPredictBlock` DIRECTLY with explicit arguments, for all four `(aboveRight, belowLeft)` combinations) and `PATCH` (an 18x18 dump of the FINAL frame from the origin). MEASURED: `PREDREF above=235,235,235 left=235,235,235 corner=235` and `PREDTEST mode=7 ang=-1 fi=0 fim=0 ar0bl0=235 ar0bl1=235 ar1bl0=235 ar1bl1=235` — the predictor returns 235 for EVERY availability combination and the reference samples it derives are THEMSELVES 235, so **the predictor is CORRECT given its inputs and the inputs were already 235**. The 58th pass's 'the prediction path reads wrong reference samples' is therefore CONFIRMED as the MECHANISM and REFUTED as an INDEPENDENT defect: the frame had already been written with 235 there by earlier work of the desynced decode. MEASURED (PATCH, Kof, rows 0..17 x cols 0..17): rows 0-5 x cols 0-7 = 16 (matches the reference's flat 16); rows 0-1 x cols 8-15 = 16 where the reference has 81 (the missing residual again); rows 2-5 x cols 8-15 = a spurious GRADIENT (`12 2 13` / `14 4 10 50 129 208` / `6 8 33 112 191 239 249 239` / `95 174 237 247 241 235 235 235`); rows 6-9 = a SOLID 235 across cols 0-15; rows 10-15 = 16 for cols 0-5 but 235 for cols 6-15; cols 16-17 = 192/193. The reference over the same window is flat (16 left of col 8, 81/170 right of it), so the window is CHAOTIC exactly as a desynced stream produces. The PALETTE PAINT's geometry was re-read and is CORRECT (full block extent via `av1BlockWidth`/`av1BlockHeight`), so the region's loss is an OVERWRITE by later work, not a short paint. **CONCLUSION, revising the 58th pass: the fault is ONE root — the ENTROPY DESYNC — and the pixel anomalies ('the bottom TX predicts 235', the 235 rectangle) are its signature; chasing them block by block was chasing symptoms.** TREE/PROOF: `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **11/11**, compile rc=0, temp capture test REMOVED, both diagnostics pinned by the instrument test. NEXT: the standing 54th-pass step, now reinforced — extend the 31st-pass INDEPENDENT Python symbol decoder into the mode/coefficient region of a failing frame and diff Kof's symbol sequence SYMBOL BY SYMBOL (field inspection is exhausted and the pixel path is now exonerated, so the entropy stream is the WHOLE remaining question). **SIXTIETH PASS — TWO libaom-source VERIFICATIONS (fetched, never recalled) come back CLEAN, and the EOB multi-size class gains its first test.** (1) INTRA SYNTAX ORDER confirmed against libaom's `av1/decoder/decodemv.c` (`read_intra_frame_mode_info`) and `decodeframe.c` (`parse_decode_block`): segment id (pre/post skip) -> skip -> cdef -> delta-Q/delta-LF -> (intrabc) -> Y mode -> angle delta Y -> UV mode (+CFL alphas, angle UV) -> **palette MODE info** -> filter-intra, and `parse_decode_block` then calls `av1_visit_palette(av1_decode_palette_tokens)` **BEFORE** `read_tx_size` — so the 34th pass's order (mode info -> palette TOKENS -> tx size) is CONFIRMED, not assumed. Also confirmed from the same source: `read_tx_size` DOES read a symbol for intra when `tx_mode == TX_MODE_SELECT` (its guard is `(!is_inter || allow_select_inter)`), and `av1_allow_intrabc` sits between delta-Q and the Y mode — Kof reads the `allow_intrabc` HEADER bit and REFUSES the file when it is 1, so for the testsrc frame the branch is provably absent. (2) `Av1Coeffs.eobMultiSize` verified entry-for-entry against libaom's `txsize_log2_minus4[TX_SIZES_ALL]` (`av1/common/common_data.h`) — **19/19 EXACT**, including the `17||18 -> 5` special case (TX_16X64 / TX_64X16) and `TX_SIZE_SQR_UP == 4 -> 6`. SUB-FINDING worth recording because the tx-size ORDER is where this front has lost time twice: libaom's TX-size enum is the SPEC's PAIRED order (4X8 then 8X4, 8X16 then 16X8, ...), NOT a 'wide-then-tall' order, so indices 17/18 really are TX_16X64/TX_64X16 and Kof's special case is right. (3) LANDED `Av1EobMultiSizeE2ETest` (2/2, JVM + Script) pinning all nineteen values against the FETCHED table — the class was previously UNTESTED. RED-PROVEN: mutating the library (`17||18` -> `15||18`) makes it fail 2/2 with `multi 15 5` where libaom has `4`; the mutation was REVERTED and `libs/` is clean. KOOF TRAP re-encountered (cost one run): `import image.X` names the FILE, not the type — `Av1CoeffCdfStore` is declared inside `Av1Coeffs.kf`, so `import image.Av1CoeffCdfStore` fails with PKG006 and only `import image.Av1Coeffs` works. NET: no bug found; this pass is verification + new coverage, and it does NOT change the open desync question. NEXT (same cheap, productive method): verify the REMAINDER of `read_coeffs_txb` against the already-fetched `av1/decoder/decodetxb.c` — the `all_zero` -> `tx_type` conditional, the base/br loop bounds, the sign order and `read_golomb` — the last subsystem the front has not checked against the actual source. **SIXTY-FIRST PASS — the coefficient subsystem is verified against the FETCHED source (last unverified subsystem), a plausible HARNESS bug is REFUTED by measurement, and the EOB-geometry golden grows to 235 comparisons.** (1) LEVEL/SIGN LOOP vs `av1/decoder/decodetxb.c`: libaom splits the walk into `read_coeffs_reverse_2d` + `read_coeffs_reverse`, but BOTH iterate `for (c = end_si; c >= start_si; --c)` — the spec's single descending loop — so Kof's one descending loop is order-equivalent. The rules match too: the base symbol is 4-way for c < eob-1 and the EOB coefficient uses `coeff_base_eob_cdf[txs_ctx][ptype][ctx]` (3 syms, +1); the br loop runs `idx = 0; idx < 12; idx += 3`, breaking when k < 3; `level > NUM_BASE_LEVELS` (2); the sign is `dc_sign_cdf[ptype][dc_sign_ctx]` at c == 0 and a raw bit otherwise; `level >= MAX_BASE_BR_RANGE` (15) adds `read_golomb()`. (2) `get_br_ctx_eob` vs `get_br_ctx` — CHECKED BECAUSE IT LOOKED LIKE A DISCREPANCY: the libaom DECODER selects `get_br_ctx_eob(pos, bhl, tx_class)` for the coefficient at `c == eob - 1` (a POSITION-only closed form returning 0/7/14), while the ENCODER (`av1_write_coeffs_txb`) uses the generic magnitude `get_br_ctx` at the SAME index. It is NOT an inconsistency: at the EOB coefficient every level is still ZERO, so the generic form collapses to exactly 0/7/14 — and Kof's generic `av1GetCoeffBrCtx` (with `levels` zeroed by `readTxbSkip`) therefore already reproduces it. Now PINNED for 3 transform classes x 64 positions. (3) `av1_eob_group_start` and `av1_eob_offset_bits` (`av1/common/txb_common.c`): **12/12 EXACT** vs Kof. (4) NEW HYPOTHESIS TESTED AND REFUTED: the probe passes `segEnabled = 0` HARDCODED while the frame header exposes it — if the real frame were segmented, the segment-id symbol would not be read and everything would desync. MEASURED: `SEG segEnabled=0 altQ0=0 altQ1=0` for `avif_real_64.avif`, i.e. the frame really is NOT segmented and the hardcode is CORRECT here; the probe now prints that line permanently so the assumption is visible in every dump. LANDED: `Av1EobMultiSizeE2ETest` extended into a full EOB-decode-geometry golden (19 multi-size + 12 offset-bits + 12 group-start + 192 br-context = 235 comparisons), 2/2 (JVM + Script); RED-PROVEN (mutating `av1EobOffsetBits[11]` 9 -> 8 fails 2/2 with `off 11 8`; mutation REVERTED, `libs/` clean). `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **11/11** with the new SEG line. NET: no bug found in this pass either. Every named element, table and ORDER of the mode + coefficient syntax is now verified against libaom's actual source. NEXT: the standing 54th/59th-pass step — an INDEPENDENT symbol-sequence diff on a failing frame — since source-level field inspection is now exhausted. **SIXTY-SECOND PASS — a SMALL REPRODUCER is LANDED, and it BREAKS a criterion the front has been leaning on: full payload consumption is NOT sufficient for correctness.** LANDED `AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` + the fixture `avif_real_sq1.avif` (32x32 screen content, an 18-byte payload; recipe `color=black:s=32x32,geq=lum='if(lt(X\,16)\,100\,if(mod(floor(X/4)\,2)\,235\,16))'`). MEASURED: `blocks=10 covered=64 frame=64 overlap=0 symBits=144 payloadBits=144` — the payload is consumed to its LAST BIT — and the LEFT half is EXACT (one `BLOCK_16X16` = block 0, whose four TX_8X8 blocks all reconstruct to the flat 100; the whole `PATCH r0..r17` left region reads 100) while the RIGHT half is WRONG: the reference is 4px vertical 16/235 stripes and Kof reads `PATCH r0 ... cols 16,17 = 235,235` where the reference is `16,16`, then garbage (118, 99, 102, 105 …) below. **THE CRITERION FINDING:** the 36th pass recorded 'full payload consumption = the entropy-correctness criterion'. This fixture consumes 144/144 bits AND is wrong on half the frame, so the implication holds in ONE direction only (under-consumption proves a desync; full consumption proves NOTHING). Every measurement that leaned on 100% consumption as a green signal must be read with that in mind. **WHERE THE DESYNC IS:** the per-block positions are `B 0 25 74` then `B 1 … B 9` ALL reporting `144 144` — i.e. block 0's mode info costs 25 bits and its residuals 49 (74 total), block 1's mode info eats the remaining 70, and blocks 2-9 read NOTHING (the reader is saturated at the payload size). So on this frame the divergence begins at block 1 and the whole right half is decoded from an exhausted reader. HARNESS BUG FIXED (mine, the 53rd-pass class): the `PATCH` dump iterated rows 0..17 unconditionally and threw `Index … out of bounds` on any frame shorter than 18 rows; it is now clamped to `mw`/`mh`. NEW LEAD, fixture deliberately NOT COMMITTED (it throws; recipe here): a 64x16 NON-SQUARE screen-content frame — `color=black:s=64x16,geq=lum='if(mod(floor(X/4)+floor(Y/4)\,2)\,235\,16)'` — THROWS `Index 64 out of bounds for length 64` INSIDE the Kof decode EVEN WITH the PATCH guarded, and the failing length 64 matches `(frameW >> 2) * (frameH >> 2)` = 16 x 4 (the driver's `cdefUnits`, or the probe's coverage array) — i.e. a NON-SQUARE-frame geometry assumption, exactly the geometry every previously-exact fixture (square or flat) never exercised. PROOF: `AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` 1/1, `check_test_hygiene` rc=0 (the new class uses uniquely-named helpers so it adds no `dupname` debt). NEXT: bisect on `avif_real_sq1.avif` — 10 blocks, 18 bytes, one exact half — by dumping block 1's mode info and the reader position bit by bit; it is the first frame on this front small enough to reason about block by block. **SIXTY-THIRD PASS — a PER-STAGE reader-position instrument localizes the over-consumption to a STAGE, not a block: on the small reproducer the payload is EXHAUSTED inside block 1's palette tokens.** LANDED (additive diagnostic, the `predLog`/`posOut` pattern — no signature change): `Av1TileDecode.stages()` records 2 ints per block — the reader position after the mode-info BODY and after the palette TOKENS (i.e. immediately before the transform size) — which, paired with `positions()` (after the transform size and after the residuals), gives the four stages of each block. The probe prints `STAGES b<i>:body>palette>txsize>residual`. MEASURED on `avif_real_sq1.avif` (32x32, **144-bit** payload): `b0:24>24>25>74 b1:107>144>144>144 b2:144>144>144>144 b3:144>144>144>144`. So block 0 has NO palette, its mode-info body ends at 24 and its tx size at 25, and its residuals run 25 -> 74 (49 bits); block 1 then spends 74 -> 107 = **33 bits of mode-info body** and 107 -> 144 = **37 bits of PALETTE TOKENS**, which takes the reader to the very END of the payload. Blocks 2-9 therefore decode from an EXHAUSTED reader (every stage reports the saturated position 144), which is exactly the garbage seen in the PATCH. **This is the first time the front can name a STAGE — the palette colour map of block 1 — as the over-consumer on a frame whose left half decodes EXACTLY.** SAME INSTRUMENT on `avif_real_64.avif` (2344-bit payload, the long-standing 54% desync): `b0:54>76>77>77 b1:89>89>90>90 b2:99>99>101>111 b3:219>468>470>470`. So block 0 IS a palette block (mode-info body 54, palette tokens 54 -> 76 = 22 bits, no residuals) — the fact the 58th pass inferred — block 1's mode info costs 12 bits and has NO residuals (all_zero, confirming the 58th pass), and **block 3 spends 108 bits of mode-info body and then 249 bits of palette tokens** — by far the largest single stage on the frame. NOTE the two frames fail in OPPOSITE directions (sq1 OVER-consumes to exhaustion, testsrc UNDER-consumes to 54%), so 'how much of the payload was eaten' is not by itself the diagnostic; the STAGE positions are. PROOF: `AvifRealFileE2ETest` re-run green after the instrument; `AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` 1/1; compile rc=0. NEXT: bisect block 1's palette colour map on `avif_real_sq1.avif` — its `n` (palette size), its `rows`/`cols` (the block's dims) and the per-symbol positions are all now reachable with this instrument plus the existing TRACE. **SIXTY-FOURTH PASS — the palette stage of block 1 is MEASURED (37 bits, >= 61 symbols) and the measurement method's LIMIT is exposed: the symbol count is only a LOWER BOUND because the reader saturates.** LANDED (probe-only, no library change): the probe now counts the traced symbols whose reader position falls inside a block's palette-token window (the window coming from `STAGES`) and prints `PALTOK window=<a>..<b> bits=<b-a> symbols=<n> lastPos=<p> total=<all>`. MEASURED on `avif_real_sq1.avif`: `PALTOK window=107..144 bits=37 symbols=61 lastPos=144 total=550`. So block 1's palette-token stage costs **37 bits** and read **at least 61 symbols**. **THE METHOD'S LIMIT (recorded, because it invalidates an obvious next step):** the count CANNOT be made exact on a frame that exhausts its payload — the reader saturates at 144, so the ~432 reads that follow all report the SAME position and are indistinguishable from palette reads. An earlier, looser window counted 493 'palette' symbols for the same 37 bits, which is an artefact. DIMS HYPOTHESIS CLOSED: `av1BlockWidth(5) = 16` and `av1BlockHeight(5) = 8` in `libs/image/Av1Block.kf` (matching libaom's `block_size_wide/high`), so block 1 (`bs5` = `BLOCK_16X8`, confirmed by the walk) would need 16 x 8 = 128 cells, i.e. 127 index symbols from the wavefront plus the one uniform symbol. Kof read **>= 61** in 37 bits, so EITHER the remaining ~66 indices were read at the saturated position 144, OR Kof UNDER-READS the colour map — and the position window cannot separate the two. SAME INSTRUMENT on `avif_real_64.avif`: `PALTOK window=89..89 bits=0 symbols=0` (block 1 has no palette — consistent with `STAGES b1:89>89>90>90`) and `total=1150` traced symbols for the frame (against 550 on sq1). PROOF: `AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` **1/1** with the measurement pinned as a RECORDED assertion (`PALTOK window=107..144 bits=37 symbols=61`), compile rc=0, `check_test_hygiene` rc=0. NEXT INSTRUMENT (named, bounded): expose the palette SIZE per block — a counter INSIDE `av1PaletteColorMap` returning how many index symbols it actually read, plus `palY`/`palUv` per block — so the actual count can be compared with the expected `rows * cols - 1`; that is the only way to settle 'read at the saturated position' vs 'under-read the map', since the position window is defeated by saturation. **SIXTY-FIFTH PASS — a SOURCE-PROVEN DEFECT in the palette colour map: Kof codes the FULL block, libaom codes only the IN-FRAME part.** CHAIN OF EVIDENCE (all fetched this cycle, never recalled): `av1/decoder/detokenize.c` `av1_decode_palette_tokens` does NOT pass the block dims to `decode_color_map_tokens`; it calls `av1_get_block_dimensions(mbmi->bsize, plane, xd, &params.plane_width, &params.plane_height, &params.rows, &params.cols)` and then `decode_color_map_tokens` uses `int rows = param->rows; int cols = param->cols;` for the wavefront bounds `for (i = 1; i < rows + cols - 1; ++i)` — i.e. the CODED extent. `av1/common/blockd.h` `av1_get_block_dimensions` computes exactly those: `block_rows = (xd->mb_to_bottom_edge >= 0) ? block_height : (xd->mb_to_bottom_edge >> 3) + block_height;` and the same for `block_cols` with `mb_to_right_edge`, then `*rows_within_bounds = (block_rows >> pd->subsampling_y) + 2 * is_chroma_sub8_y` (and the cols analogue). So whenever a palette block OVERHANGS the frame's bottom or right edge, libaom REDUCES `rows`/`cols` to the in-frame part and `decode_color_map_tokens` then COPIES the last coded column/row into the overhang (`if (cols < plane_block_width) { … }`, `for (i = rows; i < plane_block_height; ++i) memcpy(...)`). KOOF passes the FULL block dims unconditionally: `av1PaletteColorMap(reader, modeInfo, 0, palY, pw, ph, ph, pw)` and `(…, 1, palUv, puw, puh, puh, puw)` — `rows = ph, cols = pw` — so every palette block that overhangs the bottom/right edge makes Kof read index symbols the encoder NEVER WROTE, i.e. an OVER-CONSUMPTION that desyncs the stream and can exhaust the payload. The copy-back branches already exist in `av1PaletteColorMap` (`if (cols < planeW) …` and the last-row loop) — the front had written the COPY but never wired the REDUCED `rows`/`cols` that make it meaningful, so in Kof they are dead code for every block, since `cols == planeW` and `rows == planeH` always. HONEST SCOPE: this is a source-proven divergence from the reference implementation with a precise fix direction (derive `rows`/`cols` from `mb_to_right_edge`/`mb_to_bottom_edge`, i.e. from `(miCols - blockW4 - miCol)`/`(miRows - blockH4 - miRow)` as `av1IntraAvail` already does for §660), NOT yet a RED-proven failure: sq1's block 1 is (0,4) BLOCK_16X8 which exactly REACHES the 32-wide frame's right edge (`mb_to_right_edge == 0`), so it is not itself reduced; the over-consumption measured on sq1 must therefore come from a block that overhangs the BOTTOM edge (the 32x32 frame sits in one 64x64 superblock) or compound with it. NEXT (bounded, in order): (1) fix the reduced `rows`/`cols` in the driver and (2) prove it RED-first on a fixture with a palette block that overhangs the bottom edge — a frame height that is NOT a multiple of the block grid (e.g. a 32x24 or 32x8 screen-content frame), which the front has never tried. **SIXTY-SIXTH PASS — two OVERHANG fixtures are built and MEASURED, and they REFUTE the 65th pass's defect as the trigger of the observed desync.** BUILT (recipes in the plan): 32x24 and 32x40 screen-content frames — the same content as `sq1` but with a height that is NOT a multiple of the block grid, so blocks at the bottom edge overhang the frame and `av1_get_block_dimensions` must reduce their `rows`. LANDED with a test (`AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` + `avif_real_oh24.avif`, `avif_real_oh40.avif`). MEASURED: both fail in the SAME pattern as the 32x32 frame — `STAGES b0:24>24>25>74 b1:107>136>136>136` (the 32x24 has a 136-bit payload) and `STAGES b0:25>25>26>76 b1:109>152>152>152` (32x40, 152-bit). In both, block 1's palette-token stage eats the remainder of the payload (29 and 43 bits), every later block reads from a SATURATED reader, and the bottom-right pixel reads 81 where the reference is 235. **THE REFUTATION (the important result): the 65th pass's overhang defect CANNOT be the trigger.** Block 1 is at `(0,4)` `BLOCK_16X8` in a 32-wide frame, i.e. `mb_to_right_edge == 0`, so `av1_get_block_dimensions` does NOT reduce it, and the failure reproduces at a NON-OVERHANGING block — on a frame whose height is 24 or 40 (not a multiple of the 64x64 superblock) the overhang is at the BOTTOM, while the failure is at the TOP. **So the overhang defect stays CATALOGUED and is explicitly NOT taken as 'the fix'**: a fix that does not address the observed failure would be a false green. The trigger remains block 1's palette-token stage — 29 / 37 / 43 bits across the three failing frames, in every case the stage that exhausts the payload. PROOF: `AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` **2/2** with both stage-position records pinned as RECORDED assertions; compile rc=0; `check_test_hygiene` rc=0. NEXT (instrument named): dump block 1's palette MODE info — `has_palette_y`, `palette_size_y_minus_2`, `has_palette_uv`, the UV size — on the NEXT block after the mode-info body. The question it answers: does the REAL stream even HAVE a palette there? If the encoder coded no palette on that block, Kof takes the palette path where the encoder wrote none and over-consumes exactly as observed. **SIXTY-SEVENTH PASS — the palette MODE info per block is measured, and it REFUTES the 'spurious palette path' hypothesis; a new lead appears in block 0's RESIDUAL.** LANDED (additive, the `predLog`/`stagesOut` pattern — no signature change): `Av1TileDecode.palInfos()` records 2 ints per block (the decoded Y and UV palette sizes, 0 = no palette) and the probe prints `PALINFO b<i>:palY<n>/palUv<m>` for the first 6 blocks. MEASURED `avif_real_sq1.avif`: `PALINFO b0:palY0/palUv0 b1:palY2/palUv0 b2:palY0/palUv0 …` — so block 1 IS signalled as a **2-colour palette block over a 16x8 map**, which answers last pass's question and REFUTES the 'Kof takes the palette path where the encoder wrote none' hypothesis: 2 colours is exactly right for the reference's 4px 16/235 stripes, and ~37 bits for its 128-cell index map is plausible. MEASURED `avif_real_64.avif`: `PALINFO b0:palY2/palUv0 b1:palY0/palUv0 b2:palY0/palUv0 b3:palY6/palUv7 …` — block 0 is a 2-colour palette over a 16x16 map (its palette stage cost 22 bits, consistent with a flat region) and block 3 carries palettes on BOTH planes (`palY=6`, `palUv=7`) with the 249-bit palette stage, the largest stage on that frame. So the palette flags are being read plausibly on both frames: the palette path is not spurious. **NEW LEAD (stated as the next measurement, not as a claim):** sq1's block 0 — a 16x16 DC block with NO palette — costs **74 bits**, of which **49 are residual** bits for ONE non-zero coefficient (`nzHead 1,0,0,0`: only `tx0` is non-zero). On a frame whose whole payload is 144 bits with 10 blocks, that single block is the largest coherent cost left. CAVEAT: the `stagesOut` body end (24) INCLUDES the partition symbols the walk read before block 0, so the window must be decomposed before calling 74 bits anomalous. PROOF: `AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` **2/2** with the `PALINFO` record pinned as a RECORDED assertion; compile rc=0; `check_test_hygiene` rc=0. NEXT (instrument named): decompose block 0's 74 bits — dump the TRACE's first entries with their positions to separate the PARTITION cost from the mode-info cost, and dump `tx0`'s coefficient walk (eob, level, per-symbol positions). **SIXTY-EIGHTH PASS — a block-ENTRY position instrument DECOMPOSES each block into partition / mode-info / residual, and it RETIRES the 67th pass's reading of block 0.** LANDED (additive, no signature change): `Av1TileDecode.blockStarts()` records the reader position at each block's ENTRY — after every partition symbol the walk read for that leaf, before the mode info — and the probe prints `BSTART b<i>:<pos>`. MEASURED `avif_real_sq1.avif`: `BSTART b0:19 b1:76 b2:144 b3:144 …` with `STAGES b0:24>24>25>74 b1:107>144>144>144`. Decomposed: bits 0..18 are the TILE HEADER (the trace's first symbol sits at 18, so those reads are not traced); block 0 = **1 bit of partition** (18->19) + **5 bits of mode-info body** (19->24) + **1 bit of tx size** (24->25) + **49 bits of residual** (25->74); block 1 = **2 bits of partition** (74->76) + **31 bits of body** (76->107) + **37 bits of palette tokens** (107->144), which exhausts the payload. **THIS RETIRES the 67th pass's reading** that block 0's 74 bits were the anomaly: decomposed, its mode-info + partition is only 7 bits and its RESIDUAL is 49 — and 49 bits for `tx0`'s single non-zero coefficient (eob=2, a large DC step) is plausible. The 67th pass's caveat (the window included the partition symbols) was the right one to state. MEASURED `avif_real_64.avif`: `BSTART b0:24 b1:77 b2:92 b3:114 b4:470 b5:485` with `STAGES b0:54>76>77>77 b1:89>89>90>90 b2:99>99>101>111 b3:219>468>470>470`. So block 0 = 30 bits of body + 22 of palette; block 3 = **3 bits of partition** (111->114) + **105 bits of mode-info BODY** (114->219) + **249 bits of palette tokens** (219->468). **NEW LEAD (named): the mode-info BODY is the disproportionate stage** — 105 bits for testsrc's block 3, the block that carries palettes on BOTH planes (`palY=6`, `palUv=7`), against a plausible minimum of ~35 bits. The next instrument is the per-FIELD positions inside `readBodyBd` (y mode, angleY, uv mode, CFL alphas, angleUV, palette mode info, filter-intra), to see which field eats the 105 bits. PROOF: `AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` **2/2** with the `BSTART` decomposition pinned as a RECORDED assertion; compile rc=0; `check_test_hygiene` rc=0. **SIXTY-NINTH PASS — the per-FIELD body positions LOCALIZE the over-consumption: it is the palette MODE info inside `readBodyBd`.** LANDED (additive, no signature change): `Av1ModeInfo.bodyPositions()` records 4 ints per block — after the Y mode, after the Y angle group, after the UV/CFL/UV-angle group PLUS the palette MODE info, and after the filter-intra field (the end of the body) — exposed through `Av1TileDecode.bodyFields()` and printed by the probe as `BODYF`. MEASURED `avif_real_sq1.avif`: `BODYF 21 21 22 24 78 78 105 107 …`. Block 0 (entry 19) = `[21,21,22,24]`: Y mode 2 bits, angle 0, UV+palette 1, filter-intra 2. Block 1 (entry 76) = `[78,78,105,107]`: Y mode 2 bits, angle 0, **UV+palette 78 -> 105 = 27 bits**, filter-intra 2. Since block 1's `palUv = 0` and its `uvMode` is DC, the UV part is ~1-2 bits, so **the palette MODE info costs about 25 bits for a 2-colour palette**. MEASURED `avif_real_64.avif`: `BODYF 25 25 53 54 82 85 89 89 93 93 98 99 115 115 216`. Block 0 (entry 24) = `[25,25,53,54]`: the UV+palette group costs **25 -> 53 = 28 bits** (`palY = 2`). Block 3 (entry 114) = `[115,115,216,…]`: the group costs **115 -> 216 = 101 bits** (`palY = 6`, `palUv = 7`). And the blocks WITHOUT a palette are normal: block 1 `85 -> 89` = 4 bits, block 2 `93 -> 98` = 5 bits. **CONCLUSION: the over-consumption is localized to the palette MODE info (`readPaletteY`/`readPaletteUv`) called from `readBodyBd`** — ~25 bits for a 2-colour palette on a 16x8 block, ~28 for a 2-colour palette on a 16x16, ~95 for 6 colours + UV — against a handful of symbols (`has_palette_y`, `palette_size_y_minus_2`, `has_palette_uv`, `palette_size_uv_minus_2`). The group is NORMAL when no palette is signalled and explodes exactly when one is. NEXT (named, sharp and cheap): verify the palette MODE-info CDFs — `palette_y_mode_cdf`, `palette_y_size_cdf`, `palette_uv_mode_cdf`, `palette_uv_size_cdf` — against libaom's `av1/common/entropymode.c` FETCHED from source. A wrong table there prices every symbol far above its real probability and produces exactly this signature. PROOF: `AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` **2/2** with the `BODYF` decomposition pinned as a RECORDED assertion; compile rc=0; `check_test_hygiene` rc=0. **SEVENTIETH PASS — the palette MODE-info CDFs are verified against the FETCHED source and they MATCH, which RETIRES the 69th pass's conclusion as stated; the colour reading matches libaom step for step; and one concrete divergence surfaces in the palette-mode CONTEXT availability predicate.** (1) CDFs VERIFIED (fetched `av1/common/entropymode.c`, never recalled): Kof's `paletteYSizeData`, `paletteUvSizeData`, `paletteYModeData`, `paletteUvModeData` are IDENTICAL to libaom's `default_palette_y_size_cdf` (7 x 6), `default_palette_uv_size_cdf` (7 x 6), `default_palette_y_mode_cdf` (7 x 3) and `default_palette_uv_mode_cdf` (2) — entry for entry. So the CDFs are NOT the cost, and the 69th pass's named next step comes back NEGATIVE. (2) STRUCTURE VERIFIED: Kof's `readPaletteY` colour reading matches libaom's `read_palette_colors_y` step for step — the cache loop `for (i = 0; i < n_cache && idx < n; ++i) if (aom_read_bit(r)) cached_colors[idx++] = color_cache[i];`, then a raw `bit_depth` literal, then `bits = (bit_depth - 3) + read_literal(2)` and the descending delta loop; and `av1GetPaletteCache` merges/dedups exactly like `av1_get_palette_cache`. (3) REVISED READING, retiring part of the 69th pass's conclusion: ~25 bits for a 2-colour palette is NOT prima facie anomalous — following that syntax the cost is mode(1) + size(~3) + literal(8) + 2 + bits(5..8) = 19-22 bits plus the cache loop, so calling the palette MODE info the over-consumer is TOO STRONG as stated. What is anomalous is only that the TOTAL exceeds the payload. The honest question is therefore: does the real stream even have a palette on that block? — the decoded `palY = 2` may itself be a misread. (4) CONCRETE DIVERGENCE FOUND (from the fetched source): libaom's `av1_get_palette_mode_ctx` tests availability with `above_mbmi != NULL` / `left_mbmi != NULL`, while Kof guards the ABOVE neighbour with `r % 16 != 0` — the first mi ROW OF THE 64x64 UNIT, a different predicate that reports above-unavailable at every unit-row start (the left guard `c > 0` does match). A wrong palette-mode context changes the SYMBOL PRICES and can flip the decoded `has_palette_y` without changing neighbouring values, which is exactly the shape of what we see. NEXT (named): verify the palette-mode CONTEXT availability predicates against a FETCHED libaom `av1_get_palette_mode_ctx` plus the spec's `is_available` rules, and dump the per-block palette-mode context and `n_cache`. **SEVENTY-FIRST PASS — the palette MODE-info call site is verified against the FETCHED source, and ONE divergence is CONFIRMED: the above-neighbour availability predicate.** (1) GATES VERIFIED: libaom's `read_palette_mode_info` (fetched `av1/decoder/decodemv.c`) reads the Y palette only when `mbmi->mode == DC_PRED` and the UV palette only when `num_planes > 1 && mbmi->uv_mode == UV_DC_PRED && xd->is_chroma_ref` — Kof's gates are `yMode == 0` and `hasChroma == 1 && uvMode == 0` ✓ equivalent. (2) CONTEXTS VERIFIED: the Y size symbol is read with `palette_y_size_cdf[bsize_ctx]` and 7 symbols ✓, and the UV palette MODE context is the BOOLEAN `(palette_size[0] > 0)` — Kof's `readPaletteUv` uses `palSizeYMap[this block] > 0 ? 1 : 0` ✓ equivalent. (3) **DIVERGENCE CONFIRMED (fetched `av1_get_palette_mode_ctx`):** libaom tests the neighbours with `above_mi != NULL` / `left_mi != NULL` (i.e. `mi_row > 0` / `mi_col > 0`), while Kof's `readPaletteY` guards the ABOVE neighbour with **`r % 16 != 0`** — the first mi row OF THE 64x64 SUPERBLOCK. The two agree at r = 0 but diverge at EVERY superblock row start: at r = 16, 32, 48 Kof reports "above unavailable" while libaom reports it available, so Kof would use cdf row 0 instead of row 1 or 2, pricing the symbol at the wrong probability and possibly flipping `has_palette_y`. (4) HONEST SCOPE: this divergence is DORMANT on both fixtures that reproduce the failure — sq1's block 1 is at r = 0 and the 64x64 testsrc has mi rows 0..15, so r = 16 never occurs. It is therefore a REAL divergence to fix, but NOT yet shown to be the trigger; nailing it needs a fixture taller than 128 px whose palette block starts at mi row 16 or 32. NEXT (named): fix the predicate to `r > 0` (Kof's `r` is the mi row within the tile, so `r > 0` matches `above_mi != NULL`) and prove it RED-first with a tall screen-content fixture — plus catalogue the divergence as `known-bugs` §NNN in the same unit as the fix. **SEVENTY-SECOND PASS — the palette-mode CONTEXT divergence is FIXED and RED-PROVEN (the first genuinely RED-proven change on this thread).** (1) MEASURED FIRST, on a NEW tall fixture (`avif_real_tall.avif`, 64x256 screen content, a 296-bit payload, built precisely to hit the superblock row starts): FULL consumption (`symBits=296 payloadBits=296`) and WRONG pixels (`Y 032=235` where the reference is 16, `Y 063=0` where the reference is 235). (2) The candidate fix (`r % 16 != 0` -> `r > 0`) was applied and MEASURED: the tall fixture output is BYTE-IDENTICAL before and after — because this fixture has only ONE palette block (block 1) and no palette block sits at `r = 16/32/48` with an above palette neighbour, so the predicate is never consulted in the changed case. (3) That left the fix unprovable end to end, so the context was EXTRACTED into an exported, testable function `av1PaletteModeCtx(r, c, palSizeYMap, miCols)` in `libs/image/Av1ModeInfo.kf` (with `readPaletteY` calling it) and PINNED by a new `Av1PaletteModeCtxE2ETest` whose oracle is the REFERENCE rule transcribed from the fetched `av1_get_palette_mode_ctx`. (4) **RED-PROVEN:** restoring the old predicate makes the test FAIL with `ctx 16 0 = 0` and `ctx 16 1 = 1` where the reference requires 1 and 2 (with every cell carrying a palette, the context is exactly the number of neighbours that EXIST); the fix restores 1 and 2 and the test is GREEN. PROOF: `Av1PaletteModeCtxE2ETest` 1/1 after the fix, 1/1 RED with the predicate reverted; compile rc=0. NEXT: catalogue this as `known-bugs` §NNN (EN+PT ledger + the live-count/queue updates in the SAME unit, which is why it is not bundled here) and then keep chasing the desync on `avif_real_tall.avif`, which also consumes its whole payload and decodes wrong. **REGRESSION FOUND AND FIXED IN THIS PASS (mine, from the 64th pass):** the 64th pass's `PALTOK` counter indexes `sg[2]`/`sg[3]` (the first two blocks' stage positions) UNGUARDED, so it threw `Index 2 out of bounds for length 2` on the ONE-BLOCK fixtures (cb8, pskip) — and it went unnoticed for EIGHT passes because those passes ran the structural gates but never re-ran `AvifRealFileE2ETest`. Running the suite now caught it; the block is guarded by `sg.size >= 4` and the suite is **11/11** again. Lesson: a probe change must re-run every suite that drives the probe, not just the one being extended. **CATALOGUED 16/10 — `known-bugs` §751 (EN+PT, Status FIXED).** The 72nd pass's cross-implementation divergence is now in the machine ledger with its proof, so it survives the next context: `## §751 — the palette-Y MODE context treated the above neighbour as unavailable at every 64x64 superblock row start`, `**Status:** ✅ FIXED 16/10`, the fetched-source chain, the fix, and the RED-first proof, mirrored in `known-bugs.pt_BR.md` with the `pt-switch` anchors both ways. The three ledger gates (`check_known_bugs_status`, `check_ledger_anchors`, `check_live_records`) are all rc=0 — the anchor gate initially reported the exact slug it expected (accents stripped to their BASE letters: `indisponivel`/`inicio`), which is the cleanest possible correction signal.
