(2) Kof's `readPaletteY` colour reading MATCHES libaom's `read_palette_colors_y` structurally, step for step: the cache loop, `colors[idx] = readLiteral(bitDepth)` (libaom `aom_read_literal(r, bit_depth)`), `bits = (bitDepth - 3) + readLiteral(2)` (libaom `min_bits = bit_depth - 3` then `bits = min_bits + aom_read_literal(r, 2)`), the delta loop `delta = readLiteral(bits) + 1` with `colors[idx] = clamp(colors[idx - 1] + delta)`, `range -= (colors[idx] - colors[idx - 1])` and `bits = AOMMIN(bits, ceil_log2(range))`. Since the delta scheme FORCES a monotonically increasing palette, the encoder's two colours are `{16, 235}` in that order and the reference's index 0 is the value 16. (3) **SHARPENED SCOPE:** with the colours and the merge exonerated, the inverted cells — x = 16, 17, i.e. THE FIRST CELLS of the palette block — must come from the MAP's index values or from `av1ReadUniform` (which fills `map[0]`). The block opens with the fewest neighbours possible, so a context or uniform-cdf defect would show up exactly there and nowhere else. NEXT (named): verify `av1ReadUniform` and the FIRST-CELL context against the fetched source — `av1_read_uniform` uses the equal-probability `uniform_cdf[n]`, and `av1_get_palette_color_index_context` is only called for `r > 0 || c > 0`, so the origin is a special case on both sides. **SEVENTY-NINTH PASS — BOTH alternatives of the 78th pass come back CLEAN, so the DECODE side of the palette path is now EXHAUSTED; the remaining space is the PAINT/colour-table semantics.** (1) `av1ReadUniform` VERIFIED against the fetched `av1_read_uniform`: Kof computes `l = ceil(log2(n))`, `m = (1 << l) - n`, reads `l - 1` bits and, when `v >= m`, adds one extra bit as `(v * 2) - m + extra` — libaom's `(v << 1) - m + extra_bit` ✓ IDENTICAL, including the n = 2 case (l = 1, m = 0, so `v = 0` is not `< m` and the extra bit decides 0/1) ✓. (2) The FIRST-CELL handling VERIFIED: the origin `map[0]` is filled by that uniform read on BOTH sides and the context is only used for `r > 0 || c > 0` (verified in the 75th pass) ✓ — so the origin is a consistent special case, not a divergence. MEASURED on `avif_enc_ll.avif`: `PALBLK pal(r0,c4,bs5,n2) col0=16 col1=235 idx0=1 idx1=1 idx2=1 idx3=1 idx4=1 idx5=1 idx6=1 idx7=1`. (1) **The COLOURS are CORRECT:** 16 and 235 — the reference's two values, in monotone order, exactly as the 78th pass predicted the delta scheme forces. (2) **The INDICES are WRONG:** all EIGHT cells of the map's first row decode to 1 (→ 235) where the reference needs `0,0,0,0,1,1,1,1` (x = 16..23 are `16,16,16,16,235,235,235,235`). (3) **CONCLUSION: the paint is fed a wrong INDEX, not a wrong colour** — so the fault is in the MAP's decode (the wavefront and the index context/CDF it uses), NOT in the colour reading (verified in the 78th), NOT in the colour table (checked in the 79th) and NOT in the paint's indexing. And because the payload is still consumed to the last bit (152/152), the map's reads are POSITIONED right but DECODE wrong values — the signature of a CONTEXT/CDF that is wrong for every cell rather than of a desync. (4) **THE HYPOTHESIS THIS PRODUCES, and it is sharp: the index context is CONSTANT** (most plausibly always 0) where it must vary with the neighbours — a constant context prices every cell identically, so a run of identical values (all 1s) is exactly what it yields. NEXT (named): dump the CONTEXT of each of the first eight map cells and check it by hand against `av1_get_palette_color_index_context` (verified in the 75th pass) — if the context is constant, the defect is in how the map's CONTEXT is computed at the call site, not in the function. **EIGHTY-FIRST PASS — the index CONTEXT is verified END TO END (the last unread piece), which REFUTES the constant-context hypothesis and moves the target to the map's SEED.** (1) VERIFIED against the fetched `av1_get_palette_color_index_context`: Kof's `av1PaletteColorIndexContext` matches the reference in every component — the neighbours (`colorMap[r*stride + c-1]` LEFT, `[(r-1)*stride + c-1]` ABOVE-LEFT, `[(r-1)*stride + c]` ABOVE), the weights `{2, 1, 2}`, the top-3 descending sort INCLUDING the shift loop and the `inverse_color_order` bookkeeping, the hash multipliers `{1, 2, 2}` accumulated over the first three SORTED scores, and the lookup `{-1, -1, 0, -1, -1, 4, 3, 2, 1}`. (2) **CONSEQUENCE: the constant-context hypothesis of the 80th pass is REFUTED — the context is computed correctly and fed the right map.** So the run of identical indices must come from the map being seeded wrong: `map[0] = av1ReadUniform(sym, n)` decoded to **1** where the reference's first cell needs **0** (x = 16 is `16` = colour 0), and with `av1ReadUniform` itself verified (79th) — including the n = 2 case, which reads exactly ONE bit — the only remaining possibility is that the READER POSITION at the map's start is wrong, i.e. that the palette COLOUR reading consumed the wrong number of bits. (3) **NEW TARGET (precise and measurable): the bit position immediately AFTER the colour reading and BEFORE the map.** NEXT (named): dump that position for the LL fixture's palette block and check the colour reading's bit count by hand (8-bit literal + 2-bit selector + `bits`-wide delta = 15-18 bits for a 2-colour palette), which either confirms the boundary or shows the exact number of bits gained/lost. **EIGHTY-SECOND PASS — ROOT CAUSE FOUND, FIXED AND PROVEN: the filter-intra guard was missing libaom's `palette_size[0] == 0` term.** (1) THE TRACE, read with the windowed dump landed this pass (`TRACEW`), shows the palette block's decode bit by bit: the partition, the Y/UV modes, `has_palette_y`, the palette SIZE, then the COLOURS — and they are all CORRECT (`2:0@98 … 2:0@105` = the 8-bit literal the 8-bit literal = **16**, the 2-bit selector the 2-bit selector value 3, the 8-bit delta the 8-bit delta = 218 → +1 → **235**, i.e. exactly the reference's two values) — and then a **`2:0@116` read that libaom NEVER makes on a palette block**. (2) FETCHED CONFIRMATION: `av1_filter_intra_allowed` (`av1/common/blockd.h`) is `mbmi->mode == DC_PRED && **mbmi->palette_mode_info.palette_size[0] == 0** && av1_filter_intra_allowed_bsize(...)`. Kof's guard was `enableFilterIntra == 1 && yMode == 0 && av1ModeInfoMaxDim(bs) <= 32` — **the palette term was MISSING**, so on every palette block Kof read a filter-intra flag the encoder never wrote, shifting the stream by ~2 bits and corrupting the whole colour-index MAP (which is why the COLOURS were right and the INDICES were wrong, and why the payload was still fully consumed). (3) FIX: the guard now requires `palSizeYMap[r * miCols + c] == 0`. (4) **PROOF — the map becomes EXACT:** `PALBLK pal(r0,c4,bs5,n2) col0=16 col1=235 idx0=0 idx1=0 idx2=0 idx3=0 idx4=1 idx5=1 idx6=1 idx7=1` (the reference's stripe pattern), the paint reads `PATCH r0 … 16 16` (was `235 235`), and the palette block's transform blocks alternate `p=16/r=16` and `p=235/r=235` — the reference's 4-px stripes. (5) **IMPACT ACROSS EVERY FIXTURE (no regression):** testsrc-64 (the long-standing desync) goes from **1257 to 1779** consumed bits (54% → **76%**); sq1 now matches 4 of its 6 sampled pixels (was 1); ll, oh24 and tall all get their first palette block exact; cb8, cb16 and pskip are UNCHANGED and still exact. PROOF: `AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` **4/4** (with the recorded assertions updated to the post-fix values, several now STRENGTHENED into correctness assertions) and `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **11/11**; compile rc=0; `check_test_hygiene` rc=0. (2) THE CONSISTENCY CHECK THAT FAILS: `stagesOut[2i]` (the position after `readBodyBd`, recorded in `decodeBlock`) and `bodyPosOut[4i + 3]` (the position after the SAME call, recorded inside `readBodyBd`) agree for blocks 0, 1 and 2 (24, 105, 134) but NOT for block 3 — **144 vs 136** — even though both are taken at the same point of the same call. One of the two records is therefore off for that block, and any further stage-based inference has to wait until it is explained. (3) WHAT THE SOLID PART SAYS: with blocks 0 and 1 now EXACT (their bit costs are therefore what the encoder wrote), the exhaustion must come from the blocks whose VALUES are still wrong — block 2 onwards — which is exactly where the 84th pass pointed. NEXT (named, two steps): (a) settle the `stagesOut`/`bodyPosOut` disagreement for block 3 (one record is wrong; find which), and (b) once the records are trustworthy, decompose block 2 — the first block whose values are wrong and the one whose reads exhaust the payload. **EIGHTY-SIXTH PASS — the 85th pass's INSTRUMENT INCONSISTENCY is RETRACTED: it was MY OWN PROVENANCE ERROR, and the two per-block records agree exactly.** MEASURED with the probe now printing both list sizes: `BODYF n=8 stages=8 21 21 22 24 78 78 105 105 133 133 134 134 **144 144 144 144**` against `STAGES b0:24>24>25>74 b1:105>127>128>129 b2:134>134>138>144 b3:144>144>144>144`. Both lists hold EIGHT entries (the frame's eight blocks) and the fourth block's body end is **144** in BOTH — `stagesOut[2i]` and `bodyPosOut[4i + 3]` agree for EVERY block. The `136 136 136 136` the 85th pass reported for block 3 came from a DIFFERENT RUN's log, paired with this run's `STAGES` line: **I compared numbers from two different executions as if they were one record.** THE LESSON (recorded, and it is the fifth time this front has caught itself this way): a per-block record is only meaningful WITH its provenance — never pair a value from one dump with a value from another; print the sizes (or the whole record) and read them from a SINGLE execution. The instrument itself is FINE, and no code change is needed. WHAT STANDS from the 85th pass (measured in one run): block 0 (16x16 DC) = 74 bits and EXACT; block 1 (the palette block) EXACT; the frame's 18-byte payload is EXHAUSTED by block 3, leaving blocks 4-7 undecoded. NEXT (unchanged and now unblocked): decompose block 2 — the first block whose values are wrong and the one whose reads exhaust the payload. **EIGHTY-SEVENTH PASS — sq1's block-by-block record is read from ONE execution (provenance-clean), and it shows the payload being spent on the blocks that are only ACCIDENTALLY exact.** MEASURED, all from a single run: `B 0 25 74 0 1 0 0 0 0` / `B 1 128 129 0 1 0 0 0 0` / `B 2 138 144 0 1 0 0 12 0` / `B 3 144 144 0 1 0 0 0 0` / `B 4 … 144 144` / `B 5 … 144 144` / `B 6 … 144 144` / `B 7 … 144 144`, with `BLKY (0,0,bs6)=100 (0,4,bs5)=16 (2,4,bs5)=16 (4,0,bs5)=100 (6,0,bs5)=100 (4,4,bs3)=24 (4,6,bs3)=75 (6,4,bs5)=71`. Read together with the stage record of the SAME run: block 0 costs 25 -> 74, block 1 (the palette block) 105 -> 129, block 2 129 -> 144, and **blocks 3-7 read NOTHING (all report 144, the payload's end)**. (2) THE STRUCTURE OF THE FAILURE: blocks 0 and 1 are EXACT and their costs are therefore the encoder's; block 2 — a 16x8 block in the FLAT left half — is also exact (`BLKY 100`) but **its exactness cannot be evidence, because a flat block's PREDICTION alone yields 100**: with `nzHead` all-zero after `tx0`, blocks 2-7 carry no coefficients, so any of them can print the right value without having decoded a single correct bit. Block 2 is the last block that still has bits (129 -> 144) and the first whose correctness is unverifiable by its value. (3) THE TWO STAGES TO SUSPECT in block 2: its `tx size` read costs 134 -> 138 = **4 bits** for a `txSz = 1` (TX_8X8) symbol, and its residual 138 -> 144 = **6 bits** with no non-zero coefficient anywhere (`nzHead` is zero from `tx0` on) — both are priced high for a flat block. NEXT (named): dump block 2's individual SYMBOLS from the trace window (its y mode, uv mode, tx size and the coefficient walk) and price each against the syntax, since a flat block with `txSz = 1` and zero coefficients has a small, hand-checkable expected cost. **EIGHTY-EIGHTH PASS — block 2's SYMBOLS are dumped, and they raise a CONTRADICTION that must be settled before any pricing.** The trace window was moved to 120..150 (it now covers the tail of the palette block plus block 2) and the reads are, in order: a long run of 2-way symbols (block 1's colour-index map) ending at 128, then `3:1@128`, then `2:1@129 2:1@129 2:0@129`, then **`13:12@133`** — a 13-way symbol, i.e. the intra Y MODE, with value **12 (PAETH)** — then `14:12@134` (the 14-way UV mode, value 12), then `3:2@138` (a 3-way symbol at the tx size's position) and the residual reads from 138. **THE CONTRADICTION: block 2's own record prints `yMode = 0`** (`B 2 138 144 0 1 0 0 12 0` — the fields being skip, txSz, yMode, angleY, uvMode, angleUV) while the trace shows the Y-mode READ returning 12. Either (a) the reads in the window belong to a different block than I attribute them to, or (b) the mode the decoder USES differs from the mode it REPORTS — which would be a serious defect of its own, and one the pixel values cannot reveal because a flat block's prediction is 100 whichever mode is used. This is exactly the trap the 9th pass recorded: the reported values do not capture the state. NEXT (named, and it is a check of MY OWN reading first): attribute every read in the window to a block by the entry positions already measured (`BSTART b2:129`, `b3:144`) — if the 13-way read at 133 belongs to block 2, then the reported `yMode = 0` and the decoded `12` must be reconciled, starting with whether the two come from the same value. **EIGHTY-NINTH PASS — the CODE PATH is checked FIRST, and it makes the 88th pass's contradiction a problem of MY INSTRUMENTS, not of the decoder.** The whole data path is a single value: `Av1ModeInfo.kf:760` writes `out[0] = yMode` (the value the 13-way read returned), `Av1DecodeTileRecon.kf:334` takes `var yMode = body[0]` for the predictor and `Av1DecodeTileRecon.kf:392` does `modeOut.add(body[0])` for the record, and the probe prints `modes[mi + 5]` with `mi = b2 * 17` (index 5 = `body[0]`). **There is NO path by which the decoder could read 12 and report 0** — the reported value IS the read value. So the contradiction the 88th pass raised is between two of MY OWN instruments (the chronological TRACE and the per-block `modeOut` record), and the first thing to fix is the attribution: either the trace window contains a read that is not the block's Y mode, or my field indexing in the `B` line is off. (2) WHY THIS IS THE RIGHT ORDER: the 86th pass's lesson was that a per-block record is only meaningful WITH its provenance, and the 88th pass's contradiction was raised as a question precisely so that this check could come first. The decoder is EXONERATED by its own data path; what remains is a measurement that must be made to agree with itself. (3) NOTE what the trace and the record DO agree on: the UV mode is 12 in both. So the disagreement is confined to the Y-mode field — which is exactly the shape of an INDEXING error in one of the two printers rather than of a decode difference. NEXT (named, and it removes the attribution question entirely): print ONE line per block carrying BOTH the Y-mode value as the decoder read it AND the `modeOut` value for the same block — a purpose-built record that cannot be mis-attributed, after which the Y-mode question is settled one way or the other. **NINETIETH PASS — block 2's reads are CORRELATED with its own span, and TWO more checks come back clean, confining the contradiction to ONE field.** (1) THE CORRELATION (single run, no cross-log pairing): block 2's span is entry 129 -> body end 134. Of the reads in the window, `13:12@133` ends at 133 and `14:12@134` ends at 134 — BOTH fall INSIDE that span — while `3:2@138` ends at 138, i.e. AFTER the body, which is where the transform size is read. So the attribution IS consistent: the 13-way read is block 2's Y mode (13 symbols) and the 14-way read is its UV mode (14 symbols, i.e. CFL allowed), and the third is the tx-size symbol. (2) CHECK CLEAN: the tx-size field is NOT a disagreement at all — the trace's symbol is 2 and the record's `txSz` is 1, and a symbol-to-transform-size mapping is exactly what one expects there (the record stores the TX-SIZE INDEX, not the symbol). (3) CHECK CLEAN: `modeOut` gets EXACTLY 17 ints per block — the `deltaLf` loop is `while (lk < 4)` and pads with zeros — so my probe's stride (`mi = b2 * 17`, index 5 = `body[0]`) is RIGHT. (4) WITH THE CODE PATH ALREADY EXONERATED (89th pass: `out[0] = yMode` -> `body[0]` -> `modeOut.add(body[0])` is one value) and the stride verified, the contradiction is now confined to ONE field — the Y mode, read as 12 and reported as 0 — while its UV mode (12) agrees on both sides. **A single field disagreeing, with the data path and the indexing both verified, is the signature of a MEASUREMENT problem in the trace's reading, not of a decode difference** — but that is a hypothesis, not a result. NEXT (named, and it cannot be mis-attributed): record, PER BLOCK, the Y-mode value AND the reader position immediately BEFORE and AFTER its read, from the same call site — then the trace's `13:12@133` can be checked against a span recorded at the read itself rather than against a span I derive from two other lists. **NINETY-FIRST PASS — the 88th pass's "contradiction" is RETRACTED: it was MY OWN CROSS-LOG PAIRING ERROR, and the instrument I built to settle it proves it.** (1) THE INSTRUMENT (landed): `Av1ModeInfo.ySpans()` records, PER BLOCK and AT THE READ ITSELF, three ints — the reader position before the Y-mode read, the value the read returned, and the position after it — exposed through the driver and printed as `YSPAN n=<blocks> b0:<before>v<value>><after> …`, now ALSO carrying the `modeOut` value for the same blocks (`modesY=…`) so the pairing is PRINTED rather than inferred. (2) MEASURED, in ONE run: `YSPAN n=8 b0:19v0>21 b1:77v0>78 b2:129v12>133 b3:144v0>144 modesY=0,0,12,0` — the Y-mode read at block 2 runs 129 -> 133 and returns **12**, and the `modeOut` record for the SAME block says **12** as well: **they AGREE.** The B line of the same run reads `B 2 138 144 0 0 12 0 12 0`, whose Y-mode field is **12** too. (3) THE ERROR, named: in the 88th pass I paired the TRACE of one execution with the `B` line of ANOTHER (whose Y-mode field read 0) and reported a contradiction between the decoder's read and its report. **It was the SECOND cross-log pairing error in five passes** — the 86th pass had the same failure with a `BODYF` line, and the lesson recorded then ("a per-block record is only meaningful WITH its provenance") was not enough, because I still held the pairing in my head instead of in the output. (4) THE STRUCTURAL FIX this time puts the pairing IN the output: the `YSPAN` line prints the read's value and the recorded value side by side, so the two can no longer be taken from different runs. **The decoder was never implicated** — the 89th pass had already shown the data path is a single value, and this pass closes the loop with the measurement. NEXT (named): resume the thread where it was before this detour — sq1's block 2 is the last block with bits (129 -> 144) and the first whose correctness the VALUE cannot verify, so its `tx size` read (134 -> 138) and its residual (138 -> 144) are the two stages to price against the syntax. **NINETY-SECOND PASS — block 2's span is read in full from ONE execution, and it is NOT what the 87th pass inferred.** MEASURED (single run): block 2's span (entry 129 -> 144) contains, in order, `2:1@129 2:1@129 2:0@129` (its prefix), `13:12@133` (the Y mode), `14:12@134` (the UV mode), `3:2@138` (the tx size), then **TEN two-way reads** (138 -> 142) and **a six-way read `6:0@143`**, and from 144 on every read reports 144 (the saturated reader). (2) WHY THIS MATTERS: the 87th pass reasoned that "blocks 2-7 carry no coefficients" from `nzHead` (all zero after `tx0`), and built the whole "accidentally exact" argument on it. The trace shows block 2 spending **twelve reads on its residual stage** — a block with no coefficients has nothing to decode there. `nzHead` counts NON-ZERO levels, so it cannot distinguish "no residual" from "a residual that decodes to zeros": **the count is not a cost, and I used it as one.** (3) WHAT IS STILL UNRESOLVED, stated as questions rather than claims: ten two-way reads in a row are the signature of a LITERAL (per-bit reads) or of a run of binary symbols, and a six-way symbol is either an `eob_flag_cdf32` (`eob_pt` for a TX_8X16/16X8 transform, whose `eob_multi_size` is 1) or a palette-related size. Which one it is decides whether block 2's residual belongs to the LUMA (2 transform blocks for a 16x8 at TX_8X8) or to the CHROMA (whose tx type is derived and reads no symbol). NEXT (named): identify those twelve reads by their CONTEXT — the reads immediately around them in the same run — rather than by their arity, since two symbols of the same arity come from different CDFs and only the surrounding sequence tells them apart. **NINETY-THIRD PASS — block 2's reads are explained: the trace, the record and the CODE now agree on every one of them.** (1) THE OBSERVATION I was about to report as a discrepancy: the driver's order for a block is prefix -> Y mode -> UV mode -> **palette flag** -> **filter-intra** -> tx size -> the transform-block loop, and the trace shows the Y mode (ending 133), the UV mode (ending 134) and then the tx size (ending 138) with NOTHING between — as if the palette flag and the filter-intra were missing. (2) THE EXPLANATION, and it is in the code: `readPaletteY` is called only when `yMode == 0` (DC) and the filter-intra guard requires `yMode == 0` as well — and block 2's Y mode is **12 (PAETH)**, not DC. So neither read happens, and their absence from the trace is CORRECT. (3) WHAT THIS SETTLES: this is the first pass where the THREE sources agree completely — the code's order, the trace's reads and the per-block record. It also confirms, independently of the log forensics, that the `yMode = 0` I had paired with this trace (in the 88th pass) could NOT have come from this block: a DC block would have had a palette flag and a filter-intra read in that span, and the trace has none. **The cross-log pairing error is now visible from the code alone, not only from the file names.** (4) THE TRACE'S OWN LIMIT, recorded: `readSymbol` appends to the log but `readBool`/`readLiteral` DO NOT (the module says so), so a literal's bits never appear — which is why the ten two-way reads of the transform-block loop can be counted but a literal cannot be seen at all. NEXT (named): price block 2's transform-block loop from the reads that ARE visible (ten two-way symbols plus the six-way one) against what a 16x8 block at TX_8X8 owes — two transform blocks, each an `all_zero` plus, when it is 0, a transform type and an `eob_pt` — since the arities now constrain the candidates tightly. **NINETY-FOURTH PASS — the 93rd pass's "trace limit" is CORRECTED (the trace captures the WHOLE read sequence), and the reads are now accounted for.** (1) THE CORRECTION: the 93rd pass recorded that `readBool`/`readLiteral` are not logged. That is WRONG, and the module says so itself — `Av1Symbol.kf:121-122` reads "every readSymbol appends `n` then the decoded `symbol` to `symLog`. readBool/readLiteral route through readSymbol, so this captures the WHOLE read sequence", and `readBool`'s body (line 142) is `var s = this.readSymbol(cdf, 2)` with a uniform cdf. **I quoted the first sentence of that comment and assumed the rest.** (2) WHAT IT CHANGES: the ten two-way reads inside block 2's span are the coefficient walk's BINARY symbols — the sign bits, the `eob_extra` bits and the `golomb` bits — which exist only when a block HAS a residual. So the 92nd pass's refutation of "blocks 2-7 carry no coefficients" is now confirmed from the CODE side as well as from the trace: a block with no residual produces no such reads. (3) THE ENUMERATION, from the library: every two-way symbol read in `libs/image` is one of `Av1Partition.kf:199/209` (partition bits), `Av1ModeInfo.kf:500/550` (the palette Y/UV mode flags), `:769` (filter-intra), `:944` (skip) and `Av1Symbol.kf:142` (`readBool` itself). For a PAETH block inside a partition walk that has already finished, only the last one is reachable — **so the ten reads are `readBool`s, and they can only be a residual's.** (4) A NEW, CONCRETE DISCREPANCY the arities expose: the six-way read `6:0@143` is an `eob_pt`, and an `eob_pt` CDF has six symbols only when `eob_multi_size` is 1 — i.e. for a `TX_8X16`/`TX_16X8` transform — while the block's record says `txSz = 1` (`TX_8X8`), whose `eob_multi_size` is 2 and whose CDF has SEVEN symbols. NEXT (named): read `readSelectedTxSize` and check its symbol-to-transform-size mapping for a 16x8 block against the record — the two cannot both be right, and the mapping is a small, self-contained table. **NINETY-FIFTH PASS — the tx-size tables are READ, and they make the six-way read IMPOSSIBLE for block 2 — which turns it into a question about WHICH BLOCK the read belongs to.** (1) THE TABLES, read from the library: `av1BsizeToMaxDepth(BLOCK_16X8 = 5)` is **2**, so the transform-size read is `readSymbol(cdf, 3)` — a THREE-way symbol, which matches the trace's `3:2@138`. `av1DepthToTxSize(depth, BLOCK_16X8)` starts from `av1BlockMaxTxRect(BLOCK_16X8)` = `TX_16X8` (index 8) and applies `av1SplitTxSize` `depth` times, and that table gives `av1SplitTxSize(8) = 1` (`TX_8X8`) and `av1SplitTxSize(1) = 0` (`TX_4X4`). So for a 16x8 block the depth read can only mean: **0 -> TX_16X8, 1 -> TX_8X8, 2 -> TX_4X4.** (2) THE CONSEQUENCE, and it is a contradiction with BOTH records: the trace's symbol is **2**, i.e. `TX_4X4`, whose `eob_multi_size` is 0 and whose `eob_pt` CDF has **five** symbols; the block's own record says `txSz = 1`, i.e. `TX_8X8`, whose `eob_multi_size` is 2 and whose CDF has **seven**. **Neither five nor seven is the six the trace shows.** (3) WHAT THAT LEAVES: a six-symbol `eob_pt` CDF exists only for `eob_multi_size == 1`, i.e. for a `TX_8X16`/`TX_16X8` transform — and NO depth reachable for a 16x8 block yields one (the split table never produces `TX_8X16`/`TX_16X8` from `TX_16X8`). So the six-way read most plausibly belongs to a DIFFERENT block than block 2 — the walk's next one — and the "block 2's residual" reading of the 92nd pass needs its attribution checked the same way the Y mode's was. (4) WHAT THIS DOES NOT TOUCH: the ten two-way reads are still `readBool`s and still imply a residual; and block 2 is still the last block with bits. What is now uncertain is only WHICH block owns the six-way symbol. NEXT (named): attribute the six-way read the way the Y-mode span was attributed — by recording, per block, the reader position at the transform-size read and at the end of each transform block, so the read can be placed inside a block's own span rather than inferred from a window. **NINETY-SIXTH PASS — the transform-block END POSITIONS are landed, and they attribute the six-way read: it is AFTER block 2's luma coefficients.** (1) THE INSTRUMENT (landed): `Av1TileDecode.txEnds()` records the reader position right after EACH transform block's coefficients, so a symbol can be placed INSIDE one transform block's span instead of being inferred from a window. MEASURED on `avif_real_sq1.avif` in ONE run: `TXEND n=19 44,45,45,46,138,139,139,139` (the first eight of nineteen transform blocks) beside `BODYF n=8 stages=8 21 21 22 24 78 78 105 105 133 133 134 134 144 144 144 144`. (2) WHAT IT ATTRIBUTES: entries 0-3 (44, 45, 45, 46) are block 0's four transform blocks; entry 4 is **138** — which cannot be block 1's, because block 1 is the PALETTE block whose transform loop is EMPTY by construction (`if (palY > 0) { n = 0 }`), so the record simply has no entry for it — and must therefore be **block 2's single luma transform block**, ending at 138. **The six-way read is at 143, i.e. AFTER 138** ⇒ it is NOT one of block 2's luma coefficient symbols. (3) THE GAP, and it is a new measurable quantity: a block's residual end (`posOut`) minus its LAST transform-block end is the CHROMA's cost — for block 0 that is 46 -> 74 = **28 bits of chroma**, and the luma and chroma stages are separable in the record for the first time. (4) WHAT REMAINS UNEXPLAINED, stated plainly: the six-way read is now localised to block 2's CHROMA stage (or to a later block), and a chroma `eob_pt` cannot have six symbols either — an 8x4 chroma plane block at 4:2:0 takes `TX_4X4`, whose `eob_multi_size` is 0 and whose CDF has five. So the six-way symbol is still not accounted for by the syntax I have, and it now sits in the chroma stage rather than the luma one. NEXT (named): price block 2's CHROMA stage against the syntax — the chroma's derived transform size, its per-plane entropy contexts and its `eob_pt` arity — since the luma stage is now bounded by the record and the chroma is the only place the six-way symbol can live. **NINETY-SEVENTH PASS — the six-way read is EXPLAINED: it is block 2's CHROMA `eob_pt` for a `TX_8X4` transform, and the 95th pass's "computable contradiction" is RETRACTED.** (1) THE ARITHMETIC, applied to Kof's own `eobMultiSize`: `eobMultiSize(TX_8X4) = min(3,5) + min(2,5) - 4 = **1**`, and `eob_multi_size == 1` selects the **SIX-symbol** `eob_pt` CDF. For a 16x8 LUMA block at 4:2:0 the CHROMA plane block is 8x4, so the chroma's derived transform size is exactly `TX_8X4` — **the six-way read `6:0@143` is block 2's chroma `eob_pt`, and it is fully consistent with the syntax.** (2) THE RETRACTION: the 95th pass computed `av1BsizeToMaxDepth`, `av1DepthToTxSize` and `av1SplitTxSize` and concluded that a six-way `eob_pt` was IMPOSSIBLE for block 2. The arithmetic was right and the ATTRIBUTION was wrong: those tables describe the LUMA's transform size, and the read is the CHROMA's. The 96th pass's per-transform-block instrument is what localised it — the luma's single transform block ends at 138 and the read is at 143 — so the two passes together settle it: **no discrepancy, no bug, an attribution error.** (3) WHAT IS NOW ACCOUNTED FOR, read by read: block 2's prefix (three two-way symbols), its Y mode (13-way, PAETH), its UV mode (14-way), its transform size (3-way), its luma transform block (coefficients to 138), and its chroma (the ten two-way `readBool`s plus the six-way `eob_pt`) — **every read in the block's span is explained by the syntax.** (4) CONSEQUENCES WORTH KEEPING: block 2's CHROMA has a residual (an `eob_pt` is read only when `all_zero` is 0), so the 87th pass's "blocks 2-7 carry no coefficients" is refuted for the chroma as well as for the luma; and the 95th pass's "impossible" is retracted in full. (5) THE PATTERN, now unmistakable and worth writing down: **every error on this front in the last ten passes has been an ATTRIBUTION error — which LOG a number came from, which BLOCK a read belongs to, which STAGE a table describes — and not one has been a table, a formula or a CDF error.** NEXT (named): return to the thread that the attribution detours interrupted — the frame's payload is exhausted by block 3 while the encoder coded eight blocks, and with the block-2 span now fully explained, the cost of each stage can finally be compared against what the syntax owes. **NINETY-EIGHTH PASS — the per-stage costs are compared against what the syntax owes, and the arithmetic names the inflation: Kof's first three blocks consume 142 of the frame's 144 bits, while the ENCODER fits EIGHT blocks in the same 144.** (1) THE ARITHMETIC, from the single-run records of `avif_real_sq1.avif`: the tile header costs 0 -> 19 = **19 bits**; block 0 costs 19 -> 74 = **55**; block 1 costs 76 -> 129 = **53**; block 2 costs 129 -> 144 = **15**. The first three blocks plus the header therefore take **142 of 144 bits**, and **the encoder's average per block — eight blocks in the remaining 125 bits — is 15.6**. (2) WHAT THAT MEANS: block 2's 15 bits MATCH the encoder's average, while blocks 0 (55) and 1 (53) are **three and a half times** it. Both are EXACT in value (block 0 reconstructs the reference's flat 100, block 1's palette map is the reference's stripe pattern), and that is precisely why the inflation is invisible in the pixels: **a value can be right while its COST is inflated if the extra symbols decode benignly** — the 9th pass's lesson ("the reported values do not capture the state") and the exact shape of the `3bd` bug the 82nd pass found in the filter-intra guard. (3) WHERE THE COST SITS, from the records already in hand: block 0's mode info is 5 bits, its transform size 1, its four luma transform blocks end at 44, 45, 45 and 46 (so the first costs 19 bits and the other three 1 bit each), and its CHROMA costs 46 -> 74 = **28 bits for two planes**; block 1 spends 29 bits on its body (modes, palette flag, size and the two COLOURS) and 22 on its 128-cell index map. Each of those is plausible IN ISOLATION — which is why the inflation has survived this long: it is not a wrong number, it is a sum that cannot be paid. (4) THE BUDGET TO TEST AGAINST, and this front has not had one before: **15.6 bits per block.** NEXT (named): dump block 0's individual symbols — its 19-bit first transform block and its 28-bit chroma — and price each against the syntax, the same treatment block 2 got, now with a known budget to compare against instead of a plausibility judgement. **NINETY-NINTH PASS — block 0's symbols are dumped, and the first transform block reads THREE more symbols than the syntax owes.** (1) THE DUMP (window 19..74, one run): `10:0@19` (the partition), `2:0@19` (skip), `13:0@21` (Y mode DC), `14:0@22` (UV mode DC), `2:0@22 2:0@22` (the palette flag and the filter-intra), `2:0@24` (the last prefix field), `3:1@25` (the transform size), then the transform-block loop: `2:0@28` (all_zero = 0, so the first block HAS a residual), `7:1@30` (the transform TYPE — seven symbols is TX_SET_INTRA_1, which is what a frame with `reduced_tx_set == 0` uses), `7:0@32` (the `eob_pt` — seven symbols is `eob_multi_size == 2`, i.e. TX_8X8, and the value 0 means `eob_pt = 1`, so `eob = 1`: ONE coefficient), `3:2@37` (its `coeff_base_eob`), `4:3@39 4:2@43` (its base-range loop), `2:1@44` (its sign) — and then **`2:1@45 2:1@45 2:1@46`**, three two-way reads the syntax does not owe for a single-coefficient block, before the next transform block's `2:0@49`. (2) THE COUNT, against the budget of the 98th pass: a one-coefficient transform block owes SEVEN symbols (`all_zero`, the transform type, the `eob_pt`, `coeff_base_eob`, two base-range reads and the sign) and the trace shows TEN. **Three extra binary symbols per transform block, and block 0 has six transform blocks (four luma and two chroma)** — a plausible ~18 bits of the 55 that block 0 spends against a 15.6-bit budget. (3) WHAT THEY ARE NOT: not a `golomb` escape (that needs `level >= 15` and this level is 8), and not the next block's `all_zero` (that is the `2:0@49`, and it precedes the next block's transform type `7:0@50`). **What they ARE is the open question, and it is now a three-symbol question rather than a 55-bit one.** NEXT (named): identify those three reads from the code path — enumerate the binary symbols the coefficient walk can emit AFTER a block's sign when `eob == 1`, and compare with what the trace shows; the same enumeration that resolved the two-way reads for block 2 applies, and it has a small, closed answer. **ONE HUNDREDTH PASS — the 99th pass's "three extra binary symbols" is RETRACTED: the count is EXACTLY right, and the correction redirects the whole thread to the PARTITION.** (1) THE ARITHMETIC I failed to do: block 0 is `BLOCK_16X16` at `TX_8X8`, so it has **2 x 2 = FOUR luma transform blocks**, not one. The first owes seven reads (`all_zero`, the transform type, the `eob_pt`, `coeff_base_eob`, two base-range reads, the sign) and each of the other THREE owes a single `all_zero` — because they are EMPTY. **Seven plus three is TEN, which is exactly what the trace shows**: `2:0@28 7:1@30 7:0@32 3:2@37 4:3@39 4:2@43 2:1@44` (the first block) then `2:1@45 2:1@45 2:1@46` (the three empty ones, each all_zero = 1). The `TXEND` record agrees: 44, 45, 45, 46 — the four luma transform blocks, three of them costing one bit. (2) THE RETRACTION, and it is the same failure mode as the last five: I compared a COUNT against a geometry I had assumed instead of reading. The 87th pass assumed a block had no residual from a level count; the 95th assumed a read belonged to the luma; this one assumed a block had one transform block. **Every time, the arithmetic was right and the object it was applied to was wrong.** (3) WHAT FALLS WITH IT: block 0's 55 bits are now FULLY accounted for by the syntax — mode info 5, transform size 1, the luma 21 (ten reads), the chroma 28 — so there is **no extra-symbol inflation in block 0**, and the 98th pass's 55-versus-15.6 comparison is NOT evidence of one. (4) WHAT THAT LEAVES, and it is a redirection rather than a dead end: if Kof's per-block costs are what the syntax owes, then the payload runs out early because **Kof's PARTITION codes more or smaller blocks than the encoder's** — the per-block budget is only comparable when the trees match, and the tree is the ONE thing on this front that has never been compared against an oracle. NEXT (named): compare Kof's partition tree with the encoder's — the walk's block list against the reference's, using the payload size as the coarse check it has always been (a tree that codes eight blocks where the encoder coded four cannot fit the same bits). **ONE HUNDRED AND FIRST PASS — the coarse check localises the suspicion to the PARTITION, and the trace's first partition symbol contradicts the tree Kof built.** (1) THE COARSE ARITHMETIC: the tile payload is 144 bits and Kof's tree has 8 blocks, i.e. 18 bits per block; the measured per-block overhead (mode info plus transform size, block 0) is 6 bits, so eight blocks owe at least 48 bits of pure overhead and leave 96 for the residuals. That does NOT refute eight blocks — but the shape does: **the frame's LEFT half is FLAT (a single value, 100) and Kof covers it with THREE blocks** (`(0,0)` 16x16 plus `(4,0)` and `(6,0)` 16x8), spending roughly 55 + 15 + 15 = **85 bits on a region with no detail at all**, while block 0 alone is **38% of the whole payload**. No encoder spends 85 bits to say "flat" — a flat half-frame is one block with a cheap DC residual. **So the arithmetic does not just fail to confirm the tree, it makes the tree implausible for the one region whose content is known independently of any decoder.** (2) THE CONTRADICTION IN THE TRACE: the walk's first partition read is `10:0@19` — a ten-way partition symbol (the partition CDF has ten values for a block this size) with the value **0**, which in the spec's order is `PARTITION_NONE`, i.e. ONE block covering the whole region — while the tree Kof built puts a `BLOCK_16X16` at `(0,0)`, which is what `PARTITION_SPLIT` (value 3) would produce. **A partition symbol that says NONE and a tree that says SPLIT cannot both be right**, and this is the first time on this front that a symbol's VALUE has been set against the tree the walk produced from it. (3) WHAT MUST BE CHECKED BEFORE CALLING IT A BUG (the discipline of the last ten passes): whether the walk's first partition read is the one at this level at all — for a 32x32 frame inside a 64x64 superblock the top level is FORCED to split and reads no symbol, so the first read should be the 32x32 level's — and whether the ten-way symbol's value 0 really maps to `PARTITION_NONE` in the CDF Kof selects (the order is the spec's, but the CDF ROW is chosen by the partition context, and a wrong row changes the value's meaning). NEXT (named): read the partition walk's first read against the code — which CDF row it selects for a 32x32 block at the superblock's origin, and what value 0 means there — because a single wrong partition read would produce exactly the three-blocks-where-one-belongs that the arithmetic finds implausible. **ONE HUNDRED AND SECOND PASS — the partition contradiction is confirmed with THREE components verified, and the next instrument is named.** (1) WHAT WAS CHECKED, all against the code: the walk's dispatch (`Av1Partition.kf`) handles `partition == 0` as ONE LEAF — `this.addStep(1, r, c, subSize, 0)` — and takes `subSize` from `av1BlockPartitionSubsize(partition, bs)`; the subsize TABLE (`Av1Block.kf`) gives `av1BlockPartitionSubsize(0, 9) = 9`, i.e. `PARTITION_NONE` on a `BLOCK_32X32` yields a `BLOCK_32X32` (the table is right); and the LEVEL is right — for a 32x32 frame inside a 64x64 superblock the top level is FORCED to split and reads no symbol (`hasRows` and `hasCols` both false), so the walk's first traced read IS the 32x32 level's, and it is a ten-way read with the value **0**. (2) THE CONTRADICTION THAT REMAINS: `PARTITION_NONE` at the 32x32 level means ONE block covering the whole frame, and the walk emitted **eight blocks whose first is a `BLOCK_16X16`** at `(0,0)`. **With the dispatch, the table and the level all verified, the tree the walk produced does not follow from the symbol the walk read** — and that is a stronger statement than the 101st pass's, because two of the three things that could have explained it away are now excluded by reading, not by assumption. (3) THE ONE THING NOT YET VERIFIED, and it is the next instrument: the walk RECORDS every partition decision — `addStep(0, r, c, bs, partition)` — and the probe prints only the LEAVES. **Printing the internal steps would show, in one line per node, which `partition` value the walk acted on at each level; if the recorded value is 0 at the 32x32 level while the leaf is a 16x16, the fault is in the bookkeeping between the two calls; if the recorded value is 3 (SPLIT), then the symbol the trace shows is not the one the dispatch acted on, and the trace's own attribution is wrong again.** NEXT (named): print the walk's internal partition steps and settle it in one run — a probe-only change with a small, closed answer, and the last unchecked link in the chain from symbol to tree. **ONE HUNDRED AND THIRD PASS — the partition contradiction is RETRACTED, and the cause is MY OWN TRACE WINDOW: it excluded the very symbol I was attributing.** (1) THE INSTRUMENT THAT SETTLED IT: the walk records every partition decision (`addStep(0, r, c, bs, partition)`) and the probe now prints them. MEASURED: `PART n=6 (r0,c0,bs12)p3 (r0,c0,bs9)p3 (r0,c0,bs6)p0 (r0,c4,bs6)p1 (r4,c0,bs6)p1 (r4,c4,bs6)p4` — the 64x64 superblock splits (p3, forced), **the 32x32 level splits (p3)**, the 16x16 at `(0,0)` is NONE (p0, one leaf), the 16x16 at `(0,16)` is HORZ (p1, two 16x8), the one at `(16,0)` is HORZ (p1), and the one at `(16,16)` is HORZ_A (p4) — **which reproduces the tree the leaves show, exactly.** (2) THE RESOLUTION: the trace's first partition symbol is `10:3` — value **3 = SPLIT** — and it sits at position **18**, i.e. **BELOW the window's lower bound of 19** that I set in the 99th pass to look at block 0. So my window silently DROPPED the very symbol that the walk acted on, and the next entry (`10:0@19`, the 16x16's NONE) was read as if it were the 32x32's. **With the symbol restored the trace and the tree agree PERFECTLY**: `10:3@18` = the 32x32 SPLIT, `10:0@19` = the 16x16 NONE, and every recorded partition follows. (3) THE RETRACTION: the 101st and 102nd passes' "a symbol that says NONE and a tree that says SPLIT cannot both be right" is WITHDRAWN — the symbol says SPLIT, the tree says SPLIT, and the contradiction was manufactured by a measurement bound. The 102nd pass's three verified components (the dispatch, the subsize table, the level) were all correct precisely because there was nothing wrong to find. (4) THE LESSON, and it is the sharpest of this series: **a window bound is an ATTRIBUTION DECISION** — choosing where a dump starts decides which symbol you will attribute to which event, and it is invisible in the output. Every one of the last seven errors on this front has been an attribution error, and this is the first where the instrument's own PARAMETER, rather than a log's provenance or a block's identity, did the mis-attributing. (5) WHAT IS NOW EXONERATED: the PARTITION — the tree matches the stream symbol for symbol — so the 98th pass's arithmetic (85 bits for a flat half, over three blocks) is the ENCODER's own choice, not a Kof error, and the cost question returns to the residual and the syntax with the tree ruled out. NEXT (named): with the partition and the tree in agreement, re-price block 0's residual (49 bits over six transform blocks) against the syntax, now that the one structural alternative — a wrong tree — is eliminated. **ONE HUNDRED AND FOURTH PASS — block 0's residual is RE-PRICED against the syntax, and it is EXACT: every read is one the syntax owes, so the localisation is complete and the desync is provably UPSTREAM.** (1) THE RE-PRICING, read by read from the single-run trace, now with the partition symbol restored (the 103rd pass's correction) so the attribution is right: mode info plus transform size = **6 reads, 6 bits**; the first transform block = **7 symbols, 19 bits** (`all_zero`, the transform type, the `eob_pt`, `coeff_base_eob`, two base-range reads, the sign); the other three luma transform blocks = **3 reads, 2 bits** (one `all_zero` each, they are empty); the chroma = **10 reads, 28 bits** (two planes, each `all_zero` plus `eob_pt` plus a coefficient and its sign). **Total 55 bits over 26 reads — and every one of those reads is a symbol the syntax owes for the state the decoder is in.** (2) WHAT THAT CLOSES: there is no extra symbol in block 0, no wrong table, no wrong tree. Combined with the 103rd pass (the partition matches the stream symbol for symbol) and the earlier passes (every CDF, context and table verified against the fetched source), **every LOCAL explanation for the payload running out early is now excluded.** (3) THE CONTRADICTION THAT REMAINS, and it is now sharp: Kof's first three blocks consume 142 of the frame's 144 bits, the encoder fits eight blocks in the same 144, and Kof's reads are what the syntax owes GIVEN ITS OWN STATE — so **the state must have diverged before block 3**, and no instrument built on Kof's own reader can see it, because every such instrument measures that same consistent state. The values being right for blocks 0 to 2 does not save this: the 9th pass recorded that reported values do not capture the state, and a desynced reader can produce right values for a while. (4) WHAT THAT LEAVES, and the 50 passes of verification have made it CHEAP: an INDEPENDENT symbol decoder. Its scope is now small and precisely bounded — the tile header, the partition walk and the mode info for the first blocks — and it needs no new reference data, because every CDF and context it must use has already been verified against libaom and is pinned in this repository. NEXT (named): build that decoder over `avif_real_sq1.avif` (18 bytes of payload, the smallest fixture) and compare its symbol sequence with Kof's trace ENTRY BY ENTRY, starting at the first symbol; the first entry where the two disagree is the desync, and it is the one thing in this whole investigation that Kof's own instruments cannot produce. **ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTH PASS — the reader's origin is settled, loop restoration is REFUTED by a byte-identical re-encode, the partition and mode-info syntax/order/gates are verified against the FETCHED reference, and this pass's own two mistakes are retracted with the tests that now pin what they confused.** (1) THE ORIGIN (the 104th pass's open question): `Av1Symbol`'s constructor sets `bitPos = 0` and then consumes `Min(8*len, 15)` seed bits, so `bitPos = 15` when it returns; `position()` IS that counter; and the probe calls `sym.enableTrace()` BEFORE any decode work. The trace's first entry `10:3@18` is therefore the FIRST read of the tile and cost 3 bits (15 -> 18): no untraced prelude and no origin offset — an independent decoder starts its own counter at 15. (2) LOOP RESTORATION, REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT: libaom's `decode_partition` reads `loop_restoration_read_sb_coeffs` BEFORE the partition symbol, and Kof's tile decoder has no such read (restoration appears only in the header's bit-length walk), so a fixture with restoration on would desync at its very first symbol. MEASURED: the sq1 recipe and the same command plus `-enable-restoration 0` produce BYTE-IDENTICAL files (324 bytes — the committed fixture's size), i.e. the encoder chose RESTORE_NONE and nothing is missing on sq1. (3) THE PARTITION SYNTAX against the FETCHED `decodeframe.c` / `av1_common_int.h`: the two gathers, their call-site pairing, the outcome mapping (SPLIT : HORZ / SPLIT : VERT), `partition_cdf_length` (4/10/8), `partition_context_lookup`, `partition_subsize` (all ten partitions at every square size) and `update_ext_partition_context` (the ten call shapes) ALL MATCH. What does NOT match is a NAME: libaom's `partition_gather_vert_alike` is Kof's `av1PartitionGatherHorzAlike` — libaom names the helper after the branch's IMPOSSIBLE direction, Kof after the mass it collects. (4) MISTAKE ONE, RETRACTED: reading the file I took partition 6's DECODE-ORDER branch (three `addStep` calls) for its UPDATE branch and reported "VERT_A writes an extra region"; the edit that would have applied the fix could not find the text and `git diff` is empty — the file has TWO calls, matching libaom, and the claim was refuted by the tool that would have applied it. MISTAKE TWO, also in the test's oracle: it applied libaom's `cdf_element_prob` (`cdf[e-1] - cdf[e]`, the INVERSE form) to a SPEC-form row (cumulative, `cdf[N-1] == 1<<15`), which flips the sign of every element probability and made a correct gather read as wrong (23417 vs 42119). PAID FOR: both gathers' doc comments (they described the OTHER gather, never named libaom's counterpart, and never stated the `bs > BLOCK_8X8` precondition their element indices require) are rewritten, and the new `Av1PartitionReadE2ETest` pins both gathers' element sets — computed from the row Kof itself prints — and the update's write-set for every partition over a sentinel-filled context array. (5) THE MODE INFO against the FETCHED `decodemv.c`: the prefix order (`segid_preskip`), skip, cdef, delta-q/delta-lf, the Y mode (`get_y_mode_cdf` + the `intra_mode_context` CLASS mapping), the angle delta, the UV mode (`uv_mode_cdf[cfl_allowed][y_mode]`, n = 14/13), CFL, palette, filter intra, and every GATE that decides whether a symbol is read at all: `is_cfl_allowed` (including the lossless `plane_bsize == BLOCK_4X4` case), `av1_use_angle_delta` (`bsize >= BLOCK_8X8`) and `av1_is_directional_mode` (`V_PRED..D67_PRED`). NO DEVIATION. (6) THE ONE REAL DEVIATION THE PASS FOUND, and it is NOT the desync: libaom sizes the mi grid `ALIGN_POWER_OF_TWO(width, 3) >> MI_SIZE_LOG2` = `2 * ceil(w/8)` (fetched, `av1/common/alloccommon.c`), the spec's `MiCols`; the probe computes `mw >> 2` (floor). Every fixture here measures a multiple of 8 pixels, so the two agree on all of them; on a frame whose dimensions are not multiples of 8, `has_rows`/`has_cols` at the edge change the partition BRANCH (a full read where libaom gathers) and an 8x8 block can reach a gather whose row carries only 4 symbols — the test tripped exactly that (`Index 6 out of bounds for length 5`) before the precondition was honoured.  (7) A PRE-EXISTING SUITE RED, FOUND AND FIXED: `AvifRealFileE2ETest` ran with TWO errors on the PRISTINE tree — proven by reverting this pass's only library edit and re-running (`Index 22 out of bounds for length 17`, identical) — because the probe's `YSPAN` diagnostic samples the Y modes at FIXED indices (`modes[5]`, `[22]`, `[39]`, `[56]`) and a ONE-BLOCK fixture (cb8, pskip) has fewer entries than the sample points. The sample is now guarded (it prints `-` past the end), the 64x64 fixtures' output is unchanged, and the suite is 17/17 (2 new + 11 + 4).NEXT (named): (a) align the walk's mi grid to `2 * ((w + 7) >> 3)` and prove it with a fixture whose dimensions are NOT multiples of 8 (30x30 / 20x24), RED-first against the current grid and with ffmpeg as the pixel oracle; (b) the independent symbol decoder of the 104th pass, now with its alignment pinned: seed 15, absolute positions, first symbol `10:3` ending at 18. **ONE HUNDRED AND SIXTH PASS — the NON-SQUARE frame is FIXED: the partition walk decodes blocks that OVERHANG the frame, and Kof's frame-sized buffers crashed on every one of them; a 64x16 checkerboard that used to THROW now decodes to ffmpeg's reference EXACTLY.** (1) THE RED (the recipe the 53rd-pass-era plan left uncommitted, now a committed fixture): `color=black:s=64x16,geq=lum='if(mod(floor(X/4)+floor(Y/4),2),235,16)'` => `avif_real_ns6416.avif`, and the probe THREW `Index 64 out of bounds for length 64` at `Av1ModeInfo.setSkipSeg`. (2) THE ROOT CAUSE, read from the FETCHED reference: libaom's partition walk really does decode a block whose edge lies PAST the frame — at the 64x64 root of a 64x16 frame `has_rows` is false and `has_cols` true, so `read_partition` takes the GATHER branch and a HORZ partition gives a **64x32 block in a 16-pixel-tall frame** (Kof's own `PART n=1 (r0,c0,bs12)p1` is exactly that single read). libaom survives because its buffers are PADDED — `set_mi_row_col` keeps the FULL extent (`xd->width = bw`, no clamp) while `alloccommon.c` allocates the contexts at `ALIGN_POWER_OF_TWO(num_mi_cols, MAX_MIB_SIZE_LOG2)` and the planes at `ALIGN_POWER_OF_TWO(plane_w, 5)` — and because the palette map codes only the WITHIN-BOUNDS cells: `av1_get_block_dimensions` (av1/common/blockd.h) returns `block_rows = block_height + (mi_rows - bh - mi_row) * 4` as the wavefront's `rows`/`cols`, while the map's own width/height stay the FULL block, the cells past the edge being filled by the last-column/last-row copy. (3) THE FIX — five sites, one policy (a frame-sized buffer holds the frame's cells, and the cells past the edge are dropped; nothing reads them, because every neighbour lookup is up/left and the prediction clamps to the frame's border): `Av1ModeInfo.clampedWidth`/`clampedHeight` + the three broadcasts (`setModes`, `setSkipSeg`, `storePalette`) — the first crash (`setSkipSeg`, index 64); the palette colour-INDEX map paint in the driver, both planes — the second crash (index 1024 = `frameW * frameH`); `Av1TxSize`'s context arrays PADDED to the superblock grid (`av1TxCtxExtent` = the mi extent rounded up to 16), libaom's own model, because CLAMPING them would be WRONG — the transform-size context is READ over the block's own columns and rows, so those cells must EXIST; a new `av1ReconBlockIntoClip` (beside the plain writer) for the transform-block reconstruction, used by the driver and the chroma walk — a block that STRADDLES the edge defeats an "is it outside" guard; and the palette RECONSTRUCTION loops (luma + chroma), which read the index map over the block's extent. (4) THE DECISIVE ONE, and the reason the pixels were still wrong once the crashes stopped: Kof passed the BLOCK's dims as the palette map's `rows`/`cols`, where libaom passes the WITHIN-BOUNDS dims — with the full dims the wavefront walks cells the encoder never coded, and since the reader saturates at the payload end, the 100% consumption it reported was NOT evidence of sync (the one-way implication recorded in the 37th pass). With the rule applied the fixture decodes EXACTLY: `blocks=1 covered=64 frame=64 overlap=0 symBits=240 payloadBits=240` and `Y 00=16 032=16 063=235 320=235 3232=235 3263=16` = ffmpeg's reference on all six samples. (5) PROOF: `AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` 5/5, the new `nonSquareFrameDecodesToReferenceOnJvm` RED (it threw) on the pre-fix tree and GREEN now; zero regression on the exact fixtures, because every clamp is a no-op for an in-frame block (the clamped dims ARE the block's dims, the clipped writer IS the plain writer) and the padding only enlarges arrays. NEXT (named): (a) the mi-grid alignment `2 * ((w + 7) >> 3)` with an unaligned-dimension fixture (20x24 / 30x30) — still the 105th pass's queue; (b) the CFL AC's within-bounds handling, which libaom clamps at the frame edge the same way (`av1_get_block_dimensions` feeds `av1_cfl_ac` too) and which no exact fixture has exercised yet; (c) the independent symbol decoder. **ONE HUNDRED AND SEVENTH PASS — the walk's mi grid is aligned with the reference, an unaligned-dimension frame joins the fixtures, and the 105th pass's REACHABILITY CLAIM IS CORRECTED: the floor-vs-aligned deviation is REAL but LATENT.** (1) THE FIX: the probe derived the walk's grid with a FLOOR (`mw >> 2`); the spec's `MiCols` and libaom's decoder round the frame UP to whole 8x8 luma blocks — `dec_set_mb_mi` (FETCHED, av1/decoder/decoder.c) carries the comment "Ensure that the decoded width and height are both multiples of 8 luma pixels" and sets `mi_cols = ALIGN_POWER_OF_TWO(width, 3) >> MI_SIZE_LOG2`, i.e. `2 * ceil(w / 8)`. The probe now computes exactly that, and `Av1Partition`'s class doc states the caller's contract (the mi grid is the caller's, and it must be the spec's `MiCols`/`MiRows`, with the fetched evidence). The 105th pass's quote for the same alignment was taken from `av1_get_MBs` (a memory-estimate helper in alloccommon.c); the DECODER's own function is the one above, read in full this pass. (2) THE CORRECTION (my own claim, retracted): the 105th pass recorded the deviation as "reachable on 30x30 / 20x24". MEASURED: a 20x20 frame — where the two grids really do differ (5 vs 6 mi) — decodes EXACTLY under BOTH, with a 4px checkerboard AND with a 2px one, and an A/B over all six geo fixtures (floor vs aligned) is behaviourally NEUTRAL. WHY: the difference only flips `has_rows`/`has_cols` for a block whose extent crosses the mi boundary — for a 20-tall frame that is an 8x8 block at mi row 4, which requires the 16x16 at (4,0) to choose SPLIT, and none of these encoder trees does. So the grid is wrong and the fixtures cannot see it: recorded as LATENT, with the exact reachability condition, instead of being claimed as fixed by a new fixture. (3) THE NEW FIXTURE, kept as coverage rather than as the fix's proof: `avif_real_ua20.avif` — 20x20, a 2px 16/235 checkerboard, the first frame in the suite whose dimensions are NOT multiples of 8 — and `unalignedFrameDecodesToReferenceOnJvm` asserts the PATCH rows 0 and 2 against ffmpeg's plane (the 2px checker inverts on odd 2-row bands, so the two rows differ). It decodes exactly BEFORE and AFTER the grid change, and the plan says so: it pins the fixture, not the fix. (4) THE LIBRARY'S OWN mi COMPUTATION (`AvifFrame.tileWalk`, `(codedW + 3) >> 2`) is deliberately LEFT ALONE, and here is why it needs no change: it feeds only `sbCols = ceil(miCols / 16)`, and the two values differ by +1 only when `ceil(w / 4)` is ODD (w = 4 mod 8) — and an odd mi extent can never be a multiple of 16, so `ceil(mi / 16)` is identical for both. Provably no behavioural difference ⇒ editing it would be an unproven change. (5) PROOF: `AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` 6/6 with the aligned grid (the A/B run), and the three AVIF suites re-run on the final tree. NEXT (named): (a) reach the latent grid difference with content that forces a fine partition at the bottom edge (the condition is spelled out above) — or record it as latent for good, since no encoder tree observed so far needs it; (b) the CFL AC's within-bounds handling, which `av1_get_block_dimensions` feeds as well — the 106th pass's overhanging fixtures are PALETTE blocks, so the CFL path at a frame edge is still unexercised; (c) the independent symbol decoder. **ONE HUNDRED AND EIGHTH PASS — the DRIVER's mi grid now matches the WALK's, closing an inconsistency my OWN 107th pass introduced: every mi-keyed array is on one grid again, as libaom has it.** (1) THE FINDING (self-caught): the 107th pass aligned the WALK's grid with the reference (`2 * ceil(w / 8)`, the spec's `MiCols`) but left the tile driver's own mi-keyed state on the FLOOR (`frameW >> 2`): the mode-info maps (`Av1ModeInfo`'s stride and the 105th pass's clamps), the transform contexts (`Av1TxSize`), the CDEF units, the transform-type map (both the luma read and the chroma call's `txTypeStride`) and the intra-availability bounds (`av1IntraAvail`). For any frame whose dimensions are not multiples of 8 the two grids disagree, and a block's cells land in the WRONG SLOT: with the floor stride, cell `(r, miW_floor)` IS cell `(r + 1, 0)` of the aligned grid, so a block at the frame's last floor column writes the cell the aligned grid assigns to the next row's first column — and a neighbour lookup (skip, mode, palette size/colours) can then read another block's values. (2) THE FIX: the driver computes the aligned dims ONCE (`miW`/`miH`, with the fetched `dec_set_mb_mi` evidence — av1/decoder/decoder.c: "Ensure that the decoded width and height are both multiples of 8 luma pixels", `mi_cols = ALIGN_POWER_OF_TWO(width, 3) >> MI_SIZE_LOG2`) and EVERY mi-keyed array and stride uses them: the two sub-objects, the CDEF array and its index, the luma transform-type map, the chroma `txTypeStride` and the availability call. libaom keeps exactly ONE such value (`mi_params->mi_cols`) and uses it for `read_partition`, the mode info, the transform contexts and the CDEF units alike. (3) THE PROOF: `mvn -pl kof-compiler -am -Dtest='AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest,AvifRealFileE2ETest,Av1PartitionReadE2ETest'` => **19/19 (2 + 11 + 6), BUILD SUCCESS**. The change is a NO-OP for every aligned fixture (the two grids coincide when both dimensions are multiples of 8) and behaviourally neutral on the unaligned 20x20 one (measured: it decodes to the reference exactly under both) — so this pass is recorded as removing an INCONSISTENCY, NOT as a behavioural repair, and the discriminating tree is named instead of claimed: it needs an unaligned frame whose blocks sit BOTH at the last floor column and in the following row with DIFFERENT modes (for a 20x20 frame: an 8x8 at `(0,4)` and a block at `(0,1)` whose left neighbour is `(0,0)`). (4) A FALSE LEAD OF MINE, retracted in the same pass: I read the driver's chroma call as passing `frameW >> 2` for `lumaFrameW` — mi where pixels are expected, and the width where the height belongs. Recounting the ARGUMENT POSITIONS showed that value is `txTypeStride`, and `lumaFrameW`/`lumaFrameH` do receive the pixel dims. Lesson, the third time on this front: read a parameter list position by position before calling a call wrong. (5) The driver's stale doc comment ("`frameW >> 2` columns" for the TxTypes map) is updated to `miW`. NEXT (named): (a) a discriminating fixture for the grid (the condition is above) — or record the whole class latent for good; (b) the CFL AC's within-bounds handling at a frame edge, still unexercised because the 106th pass's overhanging fixtures are PALETTE blocks; (c) the independent symbol decoder. **ONE HUNDRED AND NINTH PASS — the MULTI-TILE path is exercised for the first time: a 2x2-tile frame proves the tile-group split and decodes its first tile to the reference EXACTLY, and the reference shows why the REMAINING tiles need a per-tile CDF store.** (1) THE GAP: every fixture in this suite was SINGLE-tile, so the tile-group header's per-tile sizes and the payload split — the one place where a tile's entropy does not start at the frame's first byte — had never been exercised. (2) THE FIXTURE AND THE INSTRUMENT: `avif_real_mt128.avif` — 128x128, the same 4px 16/235 checkerboard, encoded with `-tiles 2x2` (488 bytes) — and the probe gained a `TILES n=<count>` line (it re-parses the payload list once and prints its size), so a test can PROVE the fixture is multi-tile instead of assuming it. (3) THE PROOF: `AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` 7/7 — `TILES n=4` (Kof's parser sees four tiles in the stream) and the FIRST tile (the top-left 64x64) decodes to ffmpeg's plane on all six samples: `Y 00=16 032=16 063=235 320=16 3232=16 3263=235`. The new test passed on its first run: this is a CAPABILITY PROOF, a coverage slice — not a bug fix — and it is recorded as such. (4) THE FINDING THAT LIMITS IT, fetched: libaom gives every tile its OWN frame context — `tile_data->tctx = *cm->fc` (av1/decoder/decodeframe.c, the same in both the single-thread and the threaded setup) — so the CDF ADAPTATION IS PER-TILE and no tile inherits another's. Kof's driver holds ONE adapting store per decode, so it can decode exactly one tile; the rest of a multi-tile frame needs a fresh store AND a tile-scoped walk, because the walk is frame-based today (`Av1Partition.walk` starts at `(0, 0)` and runs to `miRows`/`miCols`, where libaom bounds each tile by `TileInfo.mi_row_start`/`mi_row_end`). That is the named next step, and the fixture plus ffmpeg's plane are already in place to prove it. NEXT (named): (a) the tile-scoped walk + a per-tile CDF store, so a multi-tile frame decodes WHOLE; (b) the CFL AC's within-bounds handling at a frame edge, still unexercised because the 106th pass's overhanging fixtures are PALETTE blocks; (c) the independent symbol decoder. **ONE HUNDRED AND TENTH PASS — a MULTI-TILE frame now decodes WHOLE: the walk takes a tile rectangle (`walkTile`), every tile gets its own reader, CDF store and driver, and the 2x2 fixture matches ffmpeg's plane on all four tiles.** (1) THE LIBRARY: `Av1Partition.walk()` was frame-only (superblock loop `(0,0)` to `miRows`/`miCols`), so a tile could not be walked at all. It now delegates to a new `walkTile(r0, c0, r1, c1)` — libaom's `decode_tile` iterates `mi_row` from `tile->mi_row_start` to `tile->mi_row_end` and `mi_col` likewise (av1/decoder/decodeframe.c) — while the partition's EDGE logic inside (`has_rows`/`has_cols`) keeps using the FRAME's `mi_rows`/`mi_cols`, because a tile's right edge is not the frame's and the two bounds must not be confused. `walk()` = `walkTile(0, 0, miRows, miCols)`, so every existing caller is unchanged. (2) THE HARNESS: the probe decodes EVERY tile, each with a FRESH reader, `Av1CoeffCdfStore` and `Av1TileDecode` driver — libaom gives each tile its own copy of the frame context (`tile_data->tctx = *cm->fc`) so no tile may inherit another's adapting CDFs — and its own walk bounded by the tile's mi rectangle, derived from the header's `tileColumns`/`tileRows` over the superblock grid (uniform tiles, which is what `-tiles 2x2` produces). The coverage summary is now the SUM over tiles, and the new `TILEY` line reports, per tile, the Y at its origin and 4 pixels right of it. (3) THE PROOF: `AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` **8/8** — `TILEY t0(0,0)=16(4,0)=235 t1(64,0)=16(68,0)=235 t2(0,64)=16(4,64)=235 t3(64,64)=16(68,64)=235` (the checkerboard's even cell and the one 4px right, at EVERY tile's origin — a tile decoded with another tile's CDFs, or a walk that ran past its tile, breaks the pattern) plus `covered=1024 frame=1024` (the four tiles cover the whole mi grid). (4) THREE HARNESS TRAPS MET ON THE WAY, all recorded because they cost three runs: the probe's program is a JAVA TEXT BLOCK passed through `String.formatted`, so a Kof `%` (the modulo, here `ti % tileCols2`) is a format specifier — it must be DOUBLED, and a lone percent inside a COMMENT breaks it just as well; and the per-block diagnostics pair a driver's own lists (`positions`, `stages`) with `modes`/`levels`, so those must be rebuilt PER TILE too — sharing them made one tile's block count index another tile's list (`Index 2 out of bounds for length 2`). (5) ZERO REGRESSION: the single-tile fixtures are unchanged by construction (`walk()` delegates, one loop iteration, the totals of one tile are its own) and the whole suite re-runs green.  (6) THE SPLIT THE RATCHET FORCED: with the tile loop the harness class crossed the repository's 500-line limit, so the embedded Kof program moved to its own class (`AvifRealTileProbeProgram`, 469 lines; the probe is now 77) — split by responsibility, and the tests re-ran green after it (21/21). NEXT (named): (a) NON-UNIFORM tile grids (the header parses the per-tile superblock sizes but keeps only the counts, so a frame with `-tiles 2x1`-style unequal tiles still needs them exposed); (b) the CFL AC's within-bounds handling at a frame edge; (c) the independent symbol decoder. **ONE HUNDRED AND ELEVENTH PASS — UNEQUAL tiles decode to the reference: the 2x2 fixture of the 110th pass has EQUAL tiles, so the CLAMP that keeps a tile's mi rectangle inside the frame never fired; a 192x64 frame with `-tiles 2x1` exercises it and matches ffmpeg.** (1) WHAT WAS MISSING: the 110th pass's fixture is a 2x2 SB grid cut into 2x2 tiles, so every tile's rectangle ends exactly at the frame's extent and the probe's clamp (`if (t1c > miw) { t1c = miw }`) was a no-op. libaom spaces uniform tiles by `tileWidthSb = ceil(sbCols / tileCols)` and gives the LAST tile the remainder, so any frame whose superblock count is not divisible by its tile count has a SMALLER last tile. (2) THE FIXTURE: `avif_real_mt192.avif` — 192x64 (three superblocks across, one down), the same 4px 16/235 checkerboard, encoded with `-tiles 2x1` (423 bytes) ⇒ `tileCols = 2`, `tileSbW = ceil(3 / 2) = 2` ⇒ the first tile is 2 superblocks (128 px) and the last the remaining ONE (64 px), whose mi end `32 + 32 = 64` exceeds the frame's 48 and is CLAMPED to it. (3) THE PROOF: `AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` **9/9** — `TILES n=2`, `TILEY t0(0,0)=16(4,0)=235 t1(128,0)=16(132,0)=235` (both tiles match ffmpeg's plane; a wrong clamp would walk tile 1 past the frame, or leave its last superblock undecoded, and the checker pattern at the tile origins would break) and `covered=768 frame=768` (48x16 mi). It passed on the FIRST run: a coverage proof again, recorded as such. (4) THE HONEST LIMIT: this exercises the UNIFORM-spacing rule with an unequal remainder, not the spec's other branch — explicit per-tile sizes (`uniform_tile_spacing_flag = 0`), which this encoder does not emit and which the header parses but keeps only as COUNTS. That is the remaining item, and it needs either an encoder that emits explicit sizes or a hand-built stream. NEXT (named): (a) explicit per-tile sizes (expose what the header's non-uniform branch parses); (b) the CFL AC's within-bounds handling at a frame edge; (c) the independent symbol decoder. **ONE HUNDRED AND TWELFTH PASS — the CFL AC now PADS the cells past the luma plane, as libaom's `cfl_pad` does: a real deviation found by reading the fetched reference, fixed and RED-proven.** (1) THE DEVIATION: `av1CflAcFromLuma` subsampled the luma over the block's full extent and averaged over it, but a block may OVERHANG the luma plane — the overhanging-block class the 106th pass established — and the cells past the plane were read from the frame buffer's border, which the (clipped) reconstruction never wrote ⇒ a wrong AC, hence a wrong CFL prediction. libaom's `cfl_pad` (av1/common/cfl.c) states the rule in its own comment: "Due to frame boundary issues, it is possible that the total area covered by chroma exceeds that of luma. When this happens, we fill the missing pixels by repeating the last columns and/or rows." The AC therefore covers the WHOLE block, the outside replicated from the last in-frame COLUMN and then the last in-frame ROW. (2) THE FIX: `av1CflAcFromLuma` takes the LUMA PLANE's pixel extent and pads before subtracting the average (`av1CflPad`, a transcription of libaom's two-stage replication). The replication is at the CHROMA level, not the luma one — libaom pads its STORED chroma buffer, and replicating luma samples before subsampling would average the last real sample with its own copy. The within-bounds count is `(lumaW - x + 1) >> ssX`: `x` is a BORDERED coordinate (the plane's samples are `1..lumaW`) and each chroma cell covers `1 << ssX` luma samples, which is Kof's own `av1CflSubsample` convention (`x + (i << ssX)`). (3) THE PROOF: a new `Av1CflAcPadE2ETest` (1/1) drives the AC for a 4x2 chroma block at luma origin (6,0) of an 8x8 plane — its right half past the plane, whose border is a SENTINEL (200), so an unpadded read cannot be mistaken for a real sample — and compares the AC entry by entry against an independent oracle that applies libaom's rule (q3 units included: the 4:2:0 sum shifted left by one IS the average times eight). RED-proven by MUTATION: with the `av1CflPad` call removed the test fails (the sentinel leaks into the AC); restored, it passes. (4) ZERO REGRESSION: for a block inside the plane the pad is a no-op (`wIn >= cw && hIn >= ch` returns the same array), which is why the two existing CFL suites — whose blocks are all in-frame — needed only their call sites extended with the plane's dims. NEXT (named): (a) a fixture with CFL at a frame edge (the 106th pass's overhanging fixtures are PALETTE blocks, so this fix is still unexercised by a real stream); (b) explicit per-tile sizes; (c) the independent symbol decoder. **ONE HUNDRED AND FOURTEENTH PASS — a fixture written to reach CFL at a frame edge did not reach it, but found TWO REAL CRASHES of the overhanging class instead: the coefficient walk slicing the entropy contexts past the frame, and the transform-type map.** (1) THE FIXTURE: `avif_real_grad64x40.avif` — 64x40, a chroma-correlated ramp (luma with X, U with X, V with Y), encoded so the block at the bottom edge keeps its full height ⇒ ONE 64x64 block overhanging a 40-tall frame (measured: `blocks=1 covered=160 frame=160`). (2) THE TWO CRASHES IT FOUND, both RED before the fix: (a) `Index 10 out of bounds for length 10` at `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockCore` — the coefficient walk slices the `above`/`left` ENTROPY CONTEXTS at the block's transform position, and a block at the bottom edge slices PAST the frame's mi extent (10 = the frame's mi height); (b) `Index 160 out of bounds for length 160` at `Av1TxType.readTxType` — the transform-TYPE map (`miw * mih` = 16 x 10) is indexed at the overhanging transform row. (3) THE FIX: both arrays are PADDED to the superblock grid, which is exactly what libaom does — `ALIGN_POWER_OF_TWO(num_mi_cols, MAX_MIB_SIZE_LOG2)` for the entropy contexts, and a padded transform-type map — reusing the `av1TxCtxExtent` rule the 106th pass introduced for the transform contexts. The probe allocates them; the library's contract is the same one libaom's buffers have. (4) THE PROOF: the fixture now decodes WHOLE and EXACTLY — `blocks=1 covered=160 frame=160 overlap=0 symBits=168 payloadBits=168` and `Y 00=2 032=128 063=249 320=2 3232=128 3263=249`, which is ffmpeg's plane on all six samples — with `AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` green. (5) WHAT IT DID NOT REACH, and this is the honest half: the fixture was written for CFL AT A FRAME EDGE — the case the 112th pass's AC fix exists for — and it does NOT reach it: measured `uvCFL=0`, the chroma is DC. That case therefore remains OPEN, and the fixture's value is the two overhang crashes instead; its own javadoc says so, so no future reader mistakes it for CFL coverage. (6) A NOTE ON THE INSTRUMENT: a new `uvCFL=<count>` field in the probe's summary makes "did this fixture reach the CFL path" a MEASURED fact rather than an assumption — the same discipline that caught this. NEXT (named): (a) a fixture that REALLY reaches CFL at an edge; (b) explicit per-tile sizes; (c) the independent symbol decoder. **ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTEENTH PASS — CFL AT A FRAME EDGE is finally REACHED (`uvCFL=1`), and that fixture caught a REAL BUG in the 112th pass's own fix: the subsample read the luma of the cells outside the plane, past the buffer.** (1) WHY THE EARLIER ATTEMPTS MISSED, and it was a rule I had not applied: CFL requires BOTH block dims <= 32 (`is_cfl_allowed`), so a 64x64 block can never use it — which is why the 114th pass's 64x40 gradient measured `uvCFL=0` even at `-cpu-used 4`. The fixture that reaches it is `avif_real_cfl32x24.avif` — 32x24 (one superblock, overhanging by 8 rows), the same chroma-correlated ramp, `-cpu-used 4` — where the single 32x32 block IS eligible and, measured, takes it: `uvCFL=1` and `B 0 ... 13 ...` (uvMode 13). (2) THE BUG IT CAUGHT, in my own 112th-pass fix: the pad covered the VALUES, but `av1CflSubsample` still READ the luma of the cells outside the plane, which for this frame is past the buffer — `Index 885 out of bounds for length 884` (the plane is (32+2) x (24+2) = 884). The subsample is now BOUNDED by the within-bounds cells (`wIn`/`hIn`), exactly as libaom's `cfl_store` writes only the within-bounds part and `cfl_pad` fills the rest, so the outside cells are never read at all. (3) THE PROOF: the fixture decodes WHOLE and to the reference — `blocks=1 covered=48 frame=48 overlap=0 symBits=128 payloadBits=128` and `Y 00=3 032=244 063=244 320=3 3232=244 3263=244`, which is ffmpeg's plane at the sample points — and `uvCFL=1` proves the CFL path is really taken; the fixture CRASHED before the fix, so this is a RED-proven repair. (4) The 112th pass's `Av1CflAcPadE2ETest` still passes: its oracle pads the same way, so bounding the subsample changes nothing it asserts. (5) WHAT IS STILL MISSING, and it is now the sharpest instrument gap on this front: the probe reports only LUMA samples, and CFL's effect is on the CHROMA — the crash proves the path is exercised, but a WRONG AC value would still pass. NEXT (named): (a) a `UV` sample line in the probe so a chroma oracle can pin the CFL result; (b) explicit per-tile sizes; (c) the independent symbol decoder. **ONE HUNDRED AND SIXTEENTH PASS — the CHROMA ORACLE the 115th pass named as missing is landed, and it immediately found the first CHROMA-LEVEL deviation on this front: on a CFL block at a frame edge the U plane matches ffmpeg EXACTLY (129/159 at every sample) while V is off by ±1 (Kof 129/160 where ffmpeg has 130/159).** (1) THE INSTRUMENT: the probe now prints a `UV` line beside its `Y` line, sampling U and V at the same points (the luma sample scaled by the subsampling). Until now every oracle on this front was LUMA-only, so a wrong CFL result could not be seen at all — which is exactly what the 115th pass recorded as its sharpest gap. (2) THE FINDING, first measurement: `UV 00=129,129 032=159,129 063=159,129 320=129,160 3232=159,160 3263=159,160` against ffmpeg's `129,130 / 159,130 / 129,159 / 159,159`. U is exact; V is 1 LOW at the top and 1 HIGH at the bottom, i.e. Kof's V has a slightly LARGER SPREAD — an AC or a DC difference, not a polarity one. (3) WHAT IS ALREADY EXCLUDED by reading the fetched source: the prediction's SHAPE and its scaling both match libaom (`dst = clip(dc + scaled_luma(alpha, ac))`, `get_scaled_luma_q0` = `ROUND_POWER_OF_TWO_SIGNED(alpha_q3 * ac_q3, 6)`, Kof's `av1CflScaledLuma` = the same sign-aware rounding), and libaom pads BEFORE computing the average (`cfl_compute_parameters` runs `cfl_pad` then `cfl_get_subtract_average_fn`), which is the order the 112th pass's fix established. The remaining suspects are therefore the AC's q3 values (the average's rounding, `round_offset`/`num_pel_log2`) and the chroma DC the prediction starts from. (4) THE TEST asserts U against ffmpeg and RECORDS V's measured values with the deviation stated, the repository's convention for a known-but-unfixed divergence: any change fails the test and the ±1 stays visible. (5) PROOF: `AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` 11/11, BUILD SUCCESS. NEXT (named): find the ±1's root cause — compare Kof's `av1CflSubtractAverage`'s `avg` and the chroma DC for the V plane of `avif_real_cfl32x24.avif` against hand-computed values, starting from the AC's q3 buffer. **ONE HUNDRED AND SEVENTEENTH PASS — the CFL AC is HAND-COMPUTED and EXACT, which EXONERATES the prediction and pins the V plane's ±1 to its RESIDUAL; the probe now reports the chroma transform levels, and they are three coefficients.** (1) THE HAND-COMPUTATION, done offline from the fixture's own (exact) luma: the chroma block is 16x16 with only 12 rows inside the plane, so the pad replicates the last in-frame row; the q3 subsample gives `sub(0,0) = 44`, the rounded average is `avg = 990`, and the AC is a symmetric column ramp `[-946, -582, -186, 186, 578, 946]`.
