Nothing in that is a rounding choice of Kof's — it is what `cfl_store` + `cfl_pad` + `subtract_average` must produce, and Kof produces it. (2) WHY THAT EXONERATES THE PREDICTION: the U plane matches ffmpeg EXACTLY through the SAME code path (the same AC, the same `av1CflPredict`, the same clip), and the prediction cannot vary with the row for this luma (the AC is row-independent) while the V's samples do — so the row variation, and with it the ±1, comes from the V's RESIDUAL. (3) THE INSTRUMENT: the probe's driver call was passing THROWAWAY lists for `levelsUOut`/`levelsVOut`, so the chroma residuals were decoded and discarded; it now keeps them and prints `VLEV n=<tx blocks> t<i>[<scan index>]=<level>` for every non-zero V coefficient. (4) MEASURED: `VLEV n=1 t0[0]=12 t0[16]=-5 t0[32]=-1` — ONE V transform block with three non-zero coefficients (its DC and two others). The test asserts them as RECORDED values with the reasoning stated, so any change fails and the ±1 stays visible until its root cause is found. NEXT (named): trace those three coefficients through the dequantization and the inverse transform for the V plane — the same code handles the U and the luma (both exact), so the difference has to be in the V's own coefficient data or in a per-plane step of that path. **ONE HUNDRED AND EIGHTEENTH PASS — the DEQUANTIZATION is measured EXACT, which pins the V plane's ±1 to the 16x16 INVERSE TRANSFORM.** (1) THE INSTRUMENT: the probe now prints the V transform block's dequantized coefficients beside its levels (`DQV dcq=<dc_q> acq=<ac_q> qIdx=<base_q> t<i>[<scan>]=<dequantized>`). It is the step between the 117th pass's levels and the reconstructed pixel, and it is where a ±1 can enter without any entropy being wrong. (2) MEASURED: `DQV dcq=223 acq=305 qIdx=160 t0[0]=2676 t0[16]=-1525 t0[32]=-305` against the levels `t0[0]=12 t0[16]=-5 t0[32]=-1` — and the arithmetic is EXACT: 12x223 = 2676, -5x305 = -1525, -1x305 = -305. The dequantization is a plain multiply by the quantizer (dc_q for the DC, ac_q for the rest, the AV1 tables already carrying the scaling), which is what `av1Dequant` does, and the chroma uses the same quantizers as the luma here (no separate-chroma-quantizer term in play). (3) WHAT THAT PINS: with the AC hand-computed EXACT (the 117th pass), the prediction exonerated (the U matches ffmpeg through the same path), the levels decoded from an entropy stream that consumes exactly, and now the dequantization exact, the V's ±1 can only come from the 16x16 INVERSE TRANSFORM — the one stage this fixture exercises with a NON-ZERO input, because the U's residual is zero and the luma's transform is a different size (32x32). (4) PROOF: the geo suite green with the recorded `DQV` values asserted. NEXT (named): compare Kof's 16-point inverse transform against libaom's `av1_idct16`/the spec's `inverse_transform` — the rounding stages are the usual ±1 source, and this fixture now isolates them. **ONE HUNDRED AND NINETEENTH PASS — the ratchet the 118th pass left red is CLOSED by a SPLIT BY RESPONSIBILITY: the probe's diagnostic tail is now a companion Kof function.** (1) WHAT WAS WRONG: `AvifRealTileProbeProgram.java` had reached 537 lines against the repository's 500-line ratchet, because the 117th/118th passes added the `VLEV` (chroma levels) and `DQV` (dequantized values) diagnostics to an already-large program. (2) THE FIX: the whole diagnostic tail — the `Y` and `UV` sample lines, the `VLEV`/`DQV` lines and the per-block `B` lines — moved into a Kof function `probeTail(...)` defined in a NEW companion Java file, `AvifRealTileProbeDiag.java`, which the harness injects into the SAME Kof program at a `//#DIAG` marker AFTER `String.formatted` runs (so the injected text needs no `%` doubling, unlike the program itself). The program is now 476 lines and the companion 89; `check_test_hygiene` is OK. (3) THREE SELF-INFLICTED BREAKS, all fixed on the way and all worth recording: the first extraction swallowed `main()`'s closing brace (my end-of-tail detection matched an indented brace); the companion's parameter TYPES (`Av1FrameHeader`, `Av1SeqHeader`) were undefined because the program does not import those files; and the fix for the last one is the cleaner interface anyway — the tail now takes the two VALUES it needs (`baseQIdx`, `bitDepth`) instead of the header and sequence objects. (4) PROOF: `AvifScreenContentGeoE2ETest` **11/11, BUILD SUCCESS** with every existing assertion intact (the injected program is the same Kof text), and `check_test_hygiene` rc=0. NEXT (named): the 118th pass's item stands — compare Kof's 16-point inverse transform against libaom's `av1_idct16` / the spec's `inverse_transform`, the stage the V plane's ±1 was pinned to. **ONE HUNDRED AND TWENTIETH PASS — the V plane's transform TYPE is measured (DCT_DCT), which EXONERATES the 16x16 inverse transform, and a SCAN-ORDER experiment is REFUTED by measurement — leaving the scan CONFIRMED rather than assumed.** (1) THE INSTRUMENT: the probe now reports the chroma transform blocks' TYPES (`VTXT n=<blocks> [<types>]`), the one input to the V's residual that was still invisible. MEASURED: `VTXT n=1 [0]` — the V's single transform block is plain DCT_DCT, the same type and the same 16x16 size that the other fixtures' luma exercises EXACTLY, so the inverse transform is not where the ±1 comes from. (2) THE EXPERIMENT: with the AC hand-computed EXACT, the prediction exonerated, the dequantization exact and the transform type/size proven, the remaining suspect was WHERE the coefficients land — the scan. libaom's `default_scan_16x16` reads `0, 16, 1, 2, 17, 32, 48, 33, ...` while Kof GENERATES its zigzag (`av1ZigZagScan`), and this front's own 27th pass recorded that libaom's `scan.c` and the spec's table are transposed relative to each other — so I flipped Kof's generated direction to match what I read as libaom's order. (3) THE REFUTATION, and it is the pass's real result: the flip BROKE the U plane, which had been EXACT — `UV 00=130,129` instead of `129,129` and `320=152,129` instead of `129,159` — and made the V worse (`129,162`). A change that destroys a previously-exact plane cannot be right, so Kof's original zigzag direction stands and the scan is now CONFIRMED BY MEASUREMENT (my reading of libaom's table was the transposed one — the same trap, in the opposite direction). The change was reverted in full. (4) WHAT REMAINS: the ±1 in the V is still unexplained, but the space is now very small and each exclusion is measured rather than argued — the entropy consumes exactly, the levels and their dequantization are exact, the prediction is exonerated by the U, the scan is confirmed, and the transform type/size is a path proven exact elsewhere. The next instrument is the V's RECONSTRUCTED RESIDUAL itself (its samples before the prediction is added), which `av1ReconstructBlock` can produce with a zero prediction and which nothing has printed yet. **ONE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FIRST PASS — the U's residual is measured DC-ONLY (so the 16-point inverse transform's AC butterflies are exercised ONLY by the V), the 2D SHIFTS are exonerated against the fetched table, and the V's RECONSTRUCTED RESIDUAL is instrumented — the last link of the chain without an oracle.** (1) THE U's LEVELS, measured with the same instrument the V got in the 117th pass: `ULEV n=1 t0[0]=9` — a single non-zero coefficient, its DC. That is the fact the 118th pass's inference rested on and had only assumed: the U's exactness tests the DC scaling of the inverse transform and NOTHING of its AC butterflies, so the V's three coefficients are the only place those run. (2) THE 2D SHIFTS, exonerated by reading the fetched source: libaom's table is `inv_shift_16x16 = {-2, -4}` (`av1/common/av1_inv_txfm2d.c`, the values stored negated because they adjust `cos_bit`) against Kof's `rowShift = 2` (`av1InvRowShift`, the spec's form) and `colShift = 4` — the same numbers, so the driver's shifts are not the ±1. (3) THE INSTRUMENT: the diagnostic tail now prints `VRES (0,0)=… (0,1)=… (1,0)=… (1,1)=… (15,15)=…` — the V transform block's RECONSTRUCTED RESIDUAL, obtained by calling `av1ReconstructBlock` with a ZERO prediction, which returns the inverse transform's own output. It is the only link of the chain that has never had an oracle: the entropy consumes exactly, the levels and their dequantization are exact, the prediction is exonerated by the U, the scan is confirmed by measurement, the transform type/size and the 2D shifts are now exonerated too. NEXT (named): compute the reference 2D inverse transform for the V's exact input (the DC 2676 plus the two ACs) from libaom's `av1_idct16` — seven stages, transcribed as an oracle — and compare it with `VRES`; the first stage that differs is the ±1. **ONE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-SECOND PASS — the reference 2D inverse transform is TRANSCRIBED from the fetched source and lands as a repo tool; it DISAGREES with Kof's measured residual, and the disagreement is recorded as UNRESOLVED rather than as a finding, because the transcription is not yet trusted.** (1) THE TOOL: `scripts/av1_idct16_ref.py` — libaom's `av1_idct16` (seven stages), the 12-bit `cospi` row and the 2D driver's pass order and shifts, all transcribed from the fetched files and never from memory. The 2D detail that cost a revision: libaom's "rows" pass gathers `input[c * txfm_size_row + r]`, which is the COLUMN r of the raster — a transpose — and its shifts for a 16x16 TX are `-shift[0] = 2` then `-shift[1] = 4`. (2) THE COMPARISON, on the V's exact input (DC 2676 at raster 0, -1525 at 16, -305 at 32): the reference gives `(0,0)=4 (0,1)=4 (1,0)=5 (1,1)=5 (15,15)=38` against Kof's measured `VRES (0,0)=1 (0,1)=1 (1,0)=2 (1,1)=2 (15,15)=34`. The SHAPE agrees — rows constant, the same transposition — but the level differs by about 4. (3) WHY THAT IS NOT A FINDING: the decoder reproduces the U plane EXACTLY against ffmpeg on a DC-ONLY residual (level 9, one coefficient), so Kof's DC path through the same inverse transform is right, and a transcription that differs from a proven-exact path is more likely wrong than the path. The honest state is therefore UNRESOLVED: either my transcription has a stage/rounding error or Kof's V residual differs for a reason the reference would reveal once validated — and the way to tell is to run the reference on the DC-only case the decoder gets right. (4) The tool's docstring states this status, so no future reader takes its numbers as an oracle. NEXT (named): validate the transcription against the U's DC-only residual (the decoder's known-exact case); only then use it on the V. Then (b) explicit per-tile sizes and (c) the independent symbol decoder. **ONE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-THIRD PASS — the reference transcription is VALIDATED and it EXONERATES Kof's 16x16 inverse transform: the V plane's ±1 is in the PREDICTION or the add, and the 118th pass's "the ±1 is in the IDCT" is REFUTED.** (1) THE VALIDATION the 122nd pass named, done first: a DC-ONLY input must give a CONSTANT output (linearity). The 122nd's transcription FAILED it — its DC-only output took two values, 0 and 16, which no linear transform can produce — and the comparison with the fetched source showed why: STAGE 6 had been transcribed as a pass-through where libaom combines the halves (`bf1[0] = bf0[0] + bf0[7]`, …). That is exactly why the 122nd pass refused to call its numbers a finding. (2) AFTER THE FIX: the DC-only input gives a single flat value, and the V transform block of `avif_real_cfl32x24.avif` (input: DC 2676 at raster 0, -1525 at 16, -305 at 32) reproduces Kof's measured residual EXACTLY — `(0,0)=1 (0,1)=1 (1,0)=2 (1,1)=2 (15,15)=34`. (3) THE SECOND, INDEPENDENT CHECK, from the decoder's own side: its U residual is `(0,0)=16 (0,1)=16 (1,0)=16` — FLAT at 16, which is exactly the validated reference's output for the U's single coefficient (level 9 x dc_q 223 = 2007). So the decoder's inverse transform matches the reference on BOTH the DC-only case and the AC case. (4) WHAT THAT MEANS: Kof's 16x16 inverse transform is CORRECT, and the V plane's ±1 — the first chroma-level deviation on this front — must come from the PREDICTION (the CFL alpha or the DC it starts from) or from the ADD, not from the transform. The tool now carries both checks as SELF-ASSERTIONS, so it can never again be used as an oracle while wrong. NEXT (named): instrument the V plane's PREDICTION (the CFL output before the residual is added) and compare it with the AC hand-computed in the 117th pass and the alpha the decoder used. **ONE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FOURTH PASS — the CFL ALPHAS are measured (`CFLA 1 0`): the V plane's prediction has NO AC contribution, so the ±1 cannot be the AC, and the remaining terms are narrowed to the DC predictor's value and the (validated) residual.** (1) THE INSTRUMENT, and it cost no library change: the driver already records the block's `body[4]`/`body[5]` — the CFL alphas — inside its 17-field mode record (skip, segId, cdef, qIndex, txSz, body[0..7], deltaLf[0..3]), so offsets 9 and 10 ARE the alphas and the probe now prints them. (2) MEASURED: `CFLA 1 0` — cflU = 1, cflV = 0. The V's prediction is therefore the DC predictor ALONE: `av1CflPredict` adds nothing for it, and every AC-shaped explanation of the V plane's ±1 is dead. (3) THE ARITHMETIC THAT FOLLOWS, with the validated reference (the 123rd pass) and ffmpeg's plane: prediction = ffmpeg − residual gives 129 at the top rows and 127/125 at the bottom, i.e. a variation of a few units ACROSS THE ROWS. That is a genuine open discrepancy, because with alpha_V = 0 the prediction should be a per-block CONSTANT and the residual is the validated inverse transform — so one of the two sides of that subtraction is not what it seems, and the next instrument is the decoder's own residual at the BOTTOM rows (the `VRES` line currently samples (0,0), (0,1), (1,0), (1,1) and (15,15), which validated the transform but not the row 11 the arithmetic uses). (4) WHAT IS NOW EXCLUDED, each by measurement: the entropy consumption, the levels, the dequantization, the scan, the transform type/size, the 2D shifts, the inverse transform itself (validated) and the V's AC (alpha = 0). (3) NEXT: fix §759 (print `tileSizeBytes`/`tileBits` and the group bytes of both streams and compare with libaom's `read_tile_info`), then the palette-off variant isolates whether §757 is the palette or the chroma/CFL path. **ONE HUNDRED AND FORTY-FOURTH PASS — the palette-free stream's DIAGNOSTIC crash is FIXED (per-tile `palInfos` bound + CDEF config guard), and the full walk now measures the palette-FREE decode: `symBits=7283` of `payloadBits=23888` (69% short), with CDEF strengths 46/46 against the palette baseline's 0/0.** (1) ROOT CAUSE of the crash: the diagnostic's PALBLK loop indexed the LAST tile's `palInfos` (17 blocks x 2 = 34 entries) with the FRAME-WIDE block counter — `pi2[34]`, a probe bug, not the library walk. (2) THE GUARDS: the palInfos index is bounded per tile; the CDEF configuration lookup treats an index past the header's strength list as a copy. (3) THE MEASUREMENT (§759's follow-up closed): the palette-free decode walks to its summary — 77 blocks, 299 nonzero levels, `symBits=7283 payloadBits=23888` — i.e. the palette-OFF path desyncs grossly (the §757 family), while its CDEF strengths are 46/46 (the palette baseline 0/0). (4) NEXT: use the new guards to isolate which non-palette feature desyncs, starting from the CDEF-strength discriminator (encode with CDEF off/on). **ONE HUNDRED AND FORTY-FIFTH PASS — the §757 reproducer's FEATURE MATRIX isolates the PALETTE as the switch: every palette-ON stream consumes its payload exactly (2376/2376 with CDEF on and off), every palette-OFF stream stops at ~30% (7283/23888 with CDEF on/off, 4977/14240 lossless, 3259/14928 at crf 40) — catalogued as §760.** (1) THE MATRIX (all 192x64 checkerboard, 2x1 tiles, ffmpeg/libaom): restoration and intrabc were already off (their switches produced byte-identical files); CDEF's switch changes the bytes but NOT the consumption; lossless and quality do not matter. (2) THE READING: the palette is the only feature that flips the residual path off; when blocks carry coefficients our walk stops at ~30% of the payload while still TILING the frame — and a corrupted stream tiles by construction, so coverage is NOT sync evidence (the probe's own documented pitfall). (3) NEXT: find the FIRST desynced symbol in a palette-free stream from the per-tile stage positions (`TILESTATS`) and the per-block mode records (`MODESA`); the coefficient path (`all_zero`/`eob`/`tx_type`) is the first suspect. **ONE HUNDRED AND FORTY-SIXTH PASS — the palette-off desync SCALES with residual content: a flat frame consumes EXACTLY (24/24), a DC-only residual leaves 153/200, a directional/AC ramp 340/1512 and the dense checkerboard 7283/23888.** (1) THE PROGRESSION (all 192x64, 2x1 tiles, palette off, ffmpeg/libaom): the skip/all_zero path is sound — the flat stream consumes its payload exactly; the shortfall grows with the number of nonzero coefficients and their AC content. (2) THE SMALLEST FAILING CORPUS: `m_s64`/`m_s200` are byte-identical in size and block structure; the middle block differs only in mode (DC vs D67) and its single DC level, and the stream leaves 153/200. (3) NEXT: start from the flat-residual stream (one DC coefficient), print its coefficient walk (`all_zero`/`eob`/`tx_type`/level positions) and find the symbol that stops the reader short of the payload — the coefficient path is now the only suspect. **ONE HUNDRED AND FORTY-SEVENTH PASS — the minimal stream LOCALIZES the §760 shortfall to ONE block's coefficient walk: tile 0 is EXACT (104/104), tile 1 (one 64x64 block, 96-bit payload) stops at 49.** (1) THE DATA (`m_s64`, flat 64, palette off, 2 tiles): tile 0 (SB0+SB1) consumes its payload exactly; tile 1 (SB2's single block: skip=0, txSz=4, yMode=DC) has levels starting -44, 2, its transform block ends at bit 44 and the walk stops at 49 — 47 payload bits unread. (2) THE EXCLUSIONS: the tile split, the per-tile reader, the first tile's blocks and the mode/tx syntax are sound — `m_solid` (no residual) consumes 8/8 in the same tile-1 position. The `eob`/`all_zero` path for a block with a small residual is the target. (3) NEXT: print the last block's coefficient walk on `m_s64` (`all_zero`, the `eob_pt` symbol/class, the level positions) and compare the EOB with the bits the encoder left in the 96-bit tile. **ONE HUNDRED AND FORTY-EIGHTH PASS — the failing block's per-symbol TRACE is dumped (34 symbols, identical for the 64x64 single-SB stream and `m_s64`'s last tile) and the BYTE view shows both tiles share the prefix `03 ce 15 df 62 70 e6 a3 4d fe 23`: tile 0 is consumed IN FULL, tile 1 stops after the same prefix with ~46 bits left.** (1) THE TRACE (`TRACEL`, now enabled on EVERY tile's reader): partition 10:0@16, skip 2:0@16, y 13:0@17 (DC), uv 13:0@18 (DC), palette 2:0@18, EOB(1024) 11:0@22 (class 0 = EOB 1), 3:2@22, four 4-way reads, nineteen 2-way reads, then 11:0@47 / 3:0@47 / 2:0@49 / 2:1@49 and the walk stops. The EOB alphabet matches libaom's `eob_multi_size` 6 -> 11 symbols. (2) THE BYTES: `m_s64`'s two tiles share the syntax prefix `03 ce 15 df 62 70 e6 a3 4d fe 23`; tile 0 (13 bytes) is consumed in full, tile 1 (12 bytes) stops after that same prefix — so the early stop is NOT a value-dependent misread at the tile's start; the encoder wrote additional syntax past our stop, most likely the chroma planes' coefficient data. (3) NEXT: walk the tile-1 bytes symbol by symbol against the syntax, starting with the chroma coefficient syntax after the luma EOB. **ONE HUNDRED AND FORTY-NINTH PASS — a CORRECTION plus the minimal stream's exact target.** (1) THE CORRECTION: the trace's SECOND 11-way EOB symbol is CORRECT, not evidence of a 64x64 chroma transform — `txsize_log2_minus4` maps BOTH TX_32X32 (1024 coefficients) and TX_64X64 (clamped) to the 1024-coefficient EOB CDF, so an 11-way `eob_pt` is right for the 32x32 chroma transform too; the earlier suspicion is withdrawn before it could steer a fix. (2) THE TARGET (measured): for the 64x64 flat-64 stream (`m_q64`, ONE block, ONE DC coefficient) ffmpeg decodes a FLAT `Y = 71` (the limited-range value of RGB 64) with `U = V = 128`, while Kof reconstructs `Y = 64` — a 7-unit error in the SIMPLEST possible block. (3) WHY IT MATTERS: with a DC prediction of 128 and one DC coefficient, the chain `128 + residual` is a two-number oracle; every term (the DC predictor, the dequantized DC, the 64x64 inverse transform scaling) is now individually checkable. (4) NEXT: print the single block's prediction, dequantized DC and reconstructed value, and solve which term yields 64 where the encoder produced 71. **ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTIETH PASS — the single block's THREE NUMBERS isolate a WRONG COEFFICIENT LEVEL.** (1) THE NUMBERS (`m_q64`, one 64x64 block, base_q_idx 80), printed unambiguously now: prediction `p=128` (certain — no neighbours), decoded DC level `[0]=-442`, reconstruction `r=64`. The reference decodes a flat `Y=71`, so the encoder's residual is `71-128 = -57`; at the decoder's OWN 64x64 DC scale (the level x dc_q -> residual ratio measured on the exact 16x16 fixtures is 512/dc_q) and `dc_q(80)=74`, the encoder's level is `-57*512/74 = -394.4` — the encoder coded about **-394**, Kof decoded **-442** (a 48-step magnitude error). (2) THE EXONERATIONS: both 8-bit quantizer tables (256 entries each) were compared entry by entry with the fetched `quant_common.c` — ZERO differences (`dc_q(80)=74`, `ac_q(80)=87`); the prediction is certain; the transform was validated. (3) THE TARGET: the first wrong symbol is in the LEVEL WALK (`coeff_base` / `coeff_base_eob` / `coeff_br` for the DC); the `coeff_br` context/CDF selection is the prime suspect. (4) NEXT: walk the DC level's symbols with their contexts and compare the accumulated magnitude with 394. **ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-FIRST PASS — the §760 "wrong level" is RETRACTED (stale fixture): a FRESHLY re-encoded `m_q64` decodes exactly (`[0]=-394`, `Y=71`, 32/32 bits); the real §760 defect is TILE-RELATIVE AVAILABILITY, and it is FIXED.** (1) THE CORRECTION: the regenerated 64x64 flat-64 stream (same encoder settings, palette off) gives level -394, Y=71 (ffmpeg's exact value) and consumes 32/32 bits — the -442 came from a STALE probe installation, and the trace's 19 "2-way" symbols were the golomb PLUS the chroma txb_skip symbols, not a 19-bit golomb. (2) THE ROOT CAUSE (measured): tile 1's payload is BYTE-IDENTICAL to a standalone 64x64 crop that decodes exactly (3976/3976), yet in the 2-tile stream it stopped at 2140/3976, first diverging at a 2-way mode-info symbol; forcing the tile origin to (0,0) reproduced the standalone trace EXACTLY (6291 symbols, zero diff). libaom's `set_offsets` computes above/left availability TILE-relative (`mi_row > tile->mi_row_start`), while Kof's `getTxSizeContext` (and the intra prediction) tested FRAME coordinates `r > 0`/`c > 0` — the transform-size context selected a different CDF row at the tile's first column. (3) THE FIX: `Av1TxSize` carries the tile origin (`Av1Partition.walkTile` sets it), the tx-size context uses it, and the prediction availability does too. (4) PROOF: new fixture `avif_enc_nopal_2tile192.avif` (192x64, 4x4-pixel checkerboard 16/235, libaom `-cpu-used 6 -crf 20 -tiles 2x1 -enable-palette 0`) — before: 9564/11400 bits and tile-1 samples 123/255; after: 11400/11400, `t0(0,0)=17 t1(128,0)=17` and every luma row matches ffmpeg. Test `AvifFullPlaneE2ETest#paletteFreeMultiTileStreamDecodesItsWholePayload`. (5) NEXT: the same family at `-cpu-used 0` still desyncs at a TX_16X64 block, and a skipped block (`skip_txfm=1`) still reads coefficient syntax the encoder never wrote (libaom guards `read_coeffs_tx_intra_block` on `!skip_txfm`) — both are the next slice. **ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-SECOND PASS — the SKIP path: a `skip_txfm=1` block reads NO coefficient syntax (luma AND chroma), and the tile-recon ORACLE's skip case is NON-CONFORMANT (`known-bugs` §761).** (1) THE FIX: libaom's `read_coeffs_tx_intra_block` guards the whole `av1_read_coeffs_txb` call on `!mbmi->skip_txfm`, and `decode_block` then resets the block's entropy contexts over the plane block (`av1_reset_entropy_context`). Kof read the coefficient syntax unconditionally. The luma transform-block loop and BOTH chroma planes now take the block's `skip` flag: no coefficient symbols, a zero-residual reconstruction (prediction only) and an above/left context reset. (2) PROOF (RED-first, real encoder stream `avif_enc_nopal_cu0_2tile192.avif`, 192x64 4x4 checkerboard with `-cpu-used 0`): the skipped first block ran 34 -> 528 (494 bits of syntax the encoder never wrote) and the tile read 32 transform blocks / 366 levels; with the guard it runs 34 -> 34 (ZERO bits) and the tile reads 12 / 53. The non-skipped tile is the control: unchanged at `b32/txb256/nz256` (its exact-payload decode). Test `AvifFullPlaneE2ETest#skippedBlockReadsNoCoefficientSymbols`. (3) HARNESS ROOT CAUSE FOUND: the previous pass's tile-1 anomaly was the PROBE sharing the chroma entropy-context arrays across tiles — they are per-tile state, like the store/reader; the probe now recreates them per tile (the pixel buffers stay shared because the frame is assembled across tiles). (4) §761 CATALOGUED: the tile-recon golden's case 2 writes and reads coefficient syntax for a skip block (its oracle calls `av1_read_coeffs_txb` directly, without the guard), so it characterizes the harness; case 2 is EXCLUDED from `Av1DecodeTileReconE2ETest` BY NAME (cases 0/1 stay pinned) until the oracle is rebuilt. (5) NEXT: tile 0 of the same fixture still diverges at a TX_16X64 block (the next slice), then §761's skip-aware oracle rebuild. **ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-THIRD PASS — the TX_16X64 desync gets its MINIMAL reproducer and a complete audit of the coefficient-walk machinery (nothing fixed; the divergence sits UPSTREAM of it).** (1) THE REPRODUCER: the 128x64, 4x4-checker, `-cpu-used 0` single-tile encode fails with the SAME 5 blocks and `704/7368` bits as the 192x64 fixture tile 0 (whose payload is byte-identical); committed as `avif_enc_nopal_128x64_cu0.avif` with the recorded test `AvifFullPlaneE2ETest#cu0SingleTileStreamRecordsTheTx16x64Divergence`. (2) THE AUDIT (all measured): the failing transform block's 102 base/eob contexts REPLAY EXACTLY against an independent computation from the decoded levels; the 16x64 effective scan equals the spec's `Default_Scan_16x32` entry for entry (512/512, extracted from the spec HTML); the EOB group-start/offset tables match libaom's `txb_common.c`; `eobMultiSize(17)=5` matches `txsize_log2_minus4[TX_16X64]`; the partition context tables (`partition_context_lookup`) and the context formula (`left*2 + above + bsl*4`, `bsl = log2mi(bs)-1`) are libaom-exact; the mode-CDF audit (`Av1ModeCdfE2ETest`, PART rows for bsl 1..5 x ctx 0..3) is GREEN; a 1-byte/2-byte payload shift changes the decode but NOT into the reference (8192/8192 luma samples still wrong), and the 192x64 file's tile 1 decodes exactly from the same walker, so the payload starts are right. (3) THE PARADOX THAT POINTS UPSTREAM: the reference decodes a CHECKERBOARD in the first block (x0..15, a `PARTITION_VERT_4` strip) while Kof reconstructs a flat ~127 — the reference cannot come from a `skip=1` block, yet the decode says `skip=1` at the first block with the audited skip CDF (ctx 0) and the libaom-exact partition machinery. (4) NEXT: rebuild the skip-aware instrumented oracle (§761) and diff its symbol trace against the Kof trace from the first symbol; the answer is in the first partition/skip pair, not in the coefficient walk. **ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-FOURTH PASS — `known-bugs` §760 root cause for the CU0 family: the tile walk IGNORED `use_128x128_superblock` (every `-cpu-used 0` fixture sets it, every cpu-used-6 fixture does not), so the whole family was misparsed from its FIRST partition symbol; the walk now honours it, validated SYMBOL-FOR-SYMBOL against libaom's own reader.** (1) THE MEASUREMENT: `readAvifItemObus(path).seq.use128x128Superblock` is TRUE for `avif_enc_nopal_128x64_cu0`, `avif_enc_nopal_cu0_2tile192` and the ad-hoc cu0 encodes, and FALSE for `avif_enc_nopal_2tile192`, `avif_enc_check192`, `avif_enc_nopal192` and the ad-hoc cpu6 encodes — exactly the split between the streams that fail and those that decode. The walk started `decodePartition(r, c, 12)` and stepped 16 mi unconditionally, so a 128-superblock stream read a 10-symbol/row-12 partition CDF where the stream uses the gathered or 8-symbol row-16 CDF. (2) THE FIX: `av1SuperblockSize(use128)` (12/15) + `Av1Partition.setSuperblockSize`/`walkTileSb(r0,c0,r1,c1,sb)` (128-mi step, decoder notified) + `Av1TileDecode.setSuperblockSize` feeding `av1IntraAvail` (luma and both chroma planes) + `Partition_Subsize` 128x128 entries for HORZ_A/B (128x64 = 14) and VERT_A/B (64x128 = 13), which were 22/INVALID. The probe passes the sequence flag. (3) THE ORACLE: a MINIMAL C harness was built from a fresh libaom clone (tip 9440aeedd): `aom_dsp/bitreader.c`+`entdec.c`+`entcode.c` with the default CDFs copied from `entropymode.c` (`partition_cdf`, `skip_txfm_cdfs`, `kf_y_mode_cdf`, `uv_mode_cdf`, `palette_y_mode_cdf`, `filter_intra_cdfs`, `tx_size_cdf`). On the failing 128x64 payload it decodes the first block as `split_or_horz=1, partition=9, skip=1, y_mode=0, uv_mode=0, palette=0, filter_intra=0, tx_size=2` — IDENTICAL to Kof's trace after the fix. The harness also showed the gathered-CDF names are mirrored versus libaom but the formulas/branches are correct (an attempted swap was reverted), and that the uv/palette/filter/tx CDF rows match entry for entry. (4) THE RECORDED DECODES MOVED: 128x64 cu0 `704 -> 585` of 7368 bits (mode records `(1,2,0,0) (0,2,0,0) (1,2,0,0) (0,17,6,2) (0,2,9,0)`); cu0 2-tile `4632 -> 4513` (tile 0 `b5/txb29/nz100`, skipped block stage span 30 -> 30 = ZERO bits; tile 1 unchanged `b32/txb256/nz256`); the 64-superblock fixtures are untouched (`AvifFullPlaneE2ETest` pins 11400/11400 for `avif_enc_nopal_2tile192`). (5) NEXT: the first divergence after the fixed first block is block 1's skip (`skip=0` in Kof where libaom decodes 1 at the same chain) — the oracle harness now exists to walk further, symbol by symbol. **ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-FIFTH PASS — the libaom symbol oracle walks the CU0 stream further (30+ symbols match through block 3) and the probe's last hard-coded superblock size is fixed.** (1) THE ORACLE CHAIN (built 154th, extended here): with `r.allow_update_cdf = 1` (the first harness bug: adaptation was OFF), the chain `split_or_horz -> partition -> mode info` matched libaom vs Kof for blocks 0..3 including block 1's COEFFICIENT walk — the four luma `txb_skip` symbols (16x16, ctx 1, all 1) plus the U/V ones (8x32, ctx 7) that the first chain omitted — and block 3's skip/y (`y_mode=6` with the `uv(0,6)` row). The first unexplained divergence is block 3's `uv_mode` (Kof 0, chain 6); the harness needed three corrections of exactly that kind (adaptation, missing coefficient symbols, the wrong uv row), so the full per-tile oracle — not the hand-rolled chain — is the next instrument. (2) THE ADAPTATION IS EXACT (checked while chasing that divergence): Kof's `txb_skip` row walks 10692 -> 10024 -> 9398 -> 8811 -> 8261 (forward form) which is 22744 -> 23370 -> 23957 -> 24507 in libaom's inverted form — the same values libaom's `aom_read_symbol` produces, symbol for symbol. (3) THE FIX LANDED: the probe's `Av1ModeTail` was hard-coding `sbSize = 12` while `readDeltaQ`/`readDeltaLf` gate on it (`bs == sbSize && skip` early return, and the `r % sb4`/`c % sb4` first-block-of-superblock test) — for a 128-superblock stream those gates would be wrong whenever delta-q/delta-lf are present. It now passes `av1SuperblockSize(seq.use128x128Superblock)`. (4) NEXT: promote the hand-rolled chain into a FULL oracle (partition walk + mode info + coefficient syntax in C over libaom's CDFs) so every symbol can be diffed; then the b3 uv_mode question and the TX_16X64 desync behind it. **ONE HUNDRED AND FORTY-THIRD PASS — `known-bugs` §759 FIXED: the tile-group per-tile size is LITTLE-endian (`tile_size_minus_1 le(TileSizeBytes)`), so the palette-FREE libaom stream's group splits `2 -> 1981 + 1005`.** (1) ROOT CAUSE (measured): the walk accumulated the size bytes BIG-endian; identical for the palette baseline's 1-byte fields, wrong for this stream's 2-byte fields (its first field `BC 07` was read `0xBC07` = 48135, far past the group, hence `IMAGE: truncated avif tile group`). (2) THE FIX (`libs/image/AvifGroup.kf`): little-endian accumulation (`v |= byte << (8*k)`), per §5.11.1 `le(n)`. (3) PROOF RED-first: `AvifEncodedFilmGrainE2ETest#encodedPaletteFreeStreamSplitsItsTiles` on the new fixture `avif_enc_nopal192.avif` (3300 bytes, `-enable-palette 0`): `sizes 2 1981 1005`; before the fix the same read threw (`measured`). (4) FOLLOW-UP (recorded in §759): the FULL tile probe on this stream now reaches the block decode but crashes inside the DIAGNOSTIC tail (`ArrayList.get` index), not the library walk — guard it, then the palette-off variant can isolate §757's palette vs chroma/CFL path. **ONE HUNDRED AND THIRTY-FIFTH PASS — `known-bugs` §753 FIXED: the unaligned 20x20 frame now decodes EXACTLY, every sample.** (1) ROOT CAUSE (measured): the fixture's single 32x32 PALETTE block overhangs the 20x20 frame, and libaom's `av1_get_block_dimensions` (fetched) clamps the palette map's wavefront to the ALIGNED mi grid — `block_rows = block_height + (mi_rows - bh - mi_row) * 4` = 24, not the 20-pixel frame. Kof clamped to the pixel frame, so the wavefront diverged at diagonal 20, and because the chroma tokens follow the luma map in the SAME reader, the whole chroma decode started at the wrong bit (U(0,0)=235 vs 16). (2) THE FIX (`libs/image/Av1DecodeTileRecon.kf`): the map is decoded over the aligned extent `Min(block, (miH - r) * 4)` x `Min(block, (miW - c) * 4)`, while PAINTING stays clamped to the visible plane (`frameH`/`frameW`, `chroma.hU`/`wU`) so the off-screen coded cells are never written; the chroma follows the same rule subsampled. (3) PROOF: the new `AvifFullPlaneE2ETest#unalignedFrameMatchesFfmpegOnEverySample` compares ALL 600 samples (Y 20x20, U/V 10x10) against ffmpeg (`avif_ref_ua20.yuv`; the frame's CDEF strengths are 0) — RED on the old clamp (luma from row 6, all chroma), GREEN after. (4) REGRESSION: palette 6/6 + tokens 2/2 + mode-ctx 1/1 + geo 11/11 + full-plane 2/2 + CDEF 2/2 = 24/24; the open queue is back to 9. NEXT: the loop filter and the `decodeRaster` route.
> **AVIF slice 3bc LANDED 14/10 (pure Kof + a REAL encoder's bytes): the COMPOSITION decodes a real AVIF correctly — and `known-bugs` §731 is RETRACTED (the decoder was right; the glue was wrong).** Driving the solid-gray 64x64 AVIF per stage showed ONE mode-info block but 256 transform blocks of garbage with `txSz=0` (TX_4X4) for a 64x64 block: the tile driver's `txMode` takes the AV1 `TxMode` VALUE (0=ONLY_4X4, 1=LARGEST, 2=SELECT), NOT the raw frame-header `tx_mode_select` bit. The glue passed the bit, so `tx_mode_select == 0` became TX_MODE_ONLY_4X4. Mapping it (bit 0 -> LARGEST(1), bit 1 -> SELECT(2)) makes the frame read ONE block / one TX_64X64 transform and reconstruct **`Y=126` — EXACTLY ffmpeg's libdav1d output**. **This is the first END-TO-END pixel match on a real encoder's file.** **Proof (executed):** `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **6/6** (JVM/Script × plain + canonicalized + the two new solid-gray faces). **Next:** the same mapping on the complex real frame (testsrc), then the multi-block / chroma / frame-assembly composition and the `decodeRaster` AVIF route.
> **`known-bugs` §731 CATALOGUED 14/10 (diagnosis, nothing landed in libs — tree clean): the pure-Kof AVIF tile decode DIVERGES from the reference decoder on the SIMPLEST real frame.** A solid-gray 64x64 AVIF (one block, a 2-byte tile payload) reconstructs as `Y = 0,173,165` where ffmpeg's `libdav1d` reads a flat `126`. Every stage ABOVE the tile decode is pinned green against the real encoder's bytes (container, sequence header 3ay/3ba, frame header 3ax/3bb — the cfg is correct, `deltaQPresent=0`), so the divergence is inside the composed tile decode itself. This is the first end-to-end check against REAL bytes (earlier slices pinned stages on synthetic streams), and it fails at the simplest input; the reproducer is 2 bytes of tile, so it is cheap to drive. Recipe + evidence in §731.
> **AVIF slice 3bb LANDED 14/10 (pure Kof): the frame header EXPOSES the delta-Q/delta-LF facts the tile driver's `Av1ModeTail` needs — and the REAL ffmpeg frame reads `dq=0 dfl=0`, REFUTING the "wrong cfg" hypothesis.** `frameTailWalk` read (bit-counted) `delta_q_params`/`delta_lf_params` without carrying `delta_q_present`/`_res`, `delta_lf_present`/`_res`/`_multi` — the exact fields `Av1ModeTail` takes. `AvifFrameHeader` now exposes all five; the synthetic fixtures read them as `0` and `headerBytes` is UNCHANGED (the bit positions are identical). **The measurement that matters:** the real 64x64 ffmpeg frame reads `cdefBits=0`, `deltaQPresent=0`, `deltaLfPresent=0`, `seq enableCdef=1` — so the driver's `cfg` was ALREADY correct on those, and the pixel divergence is NOT a cfg problem. **Proof (executed):** `AvifFrameE2ETest` **16/16**, `AvifSeqE2ETest` **8/8**, `AvifObuE2ETest` **8/8**, `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **4/4** (the native faces now pass too — `known-bugs` §723 was fixed by the codegen lane). **Boundary (measured, NOT landed):** the Kof tile decode on the real payload still reads `Y0=129,112,122` against ffmpeg's `Y0=16,145,235` — the divergence is inside the mode-info/coefficient parse on a real tile, the next slice.
> **AVIF slice 3ba LANDED 14/10 (pure Kof + a REAL encoder's bytes): the item-data read of a real ffmpeg AVIF, unblocked test-side, PINS the real sequence facts — `enable_filter_intra = 1`, `enable_intra_edge_filter = 1`.** 3az recorded that ffmpeg's item is `[OBU_SEQUENCE_HEADER][OBU_FRAME]` with no leading temporal delimiter, which the Kof item walk requires. The fix needs no Kof change and no subset decision: prepending the delimiter yields the CANONICAL AV1 order with identical semantics — same real sequence header, same real frame, same real tile. On that canonicalized item the Kof walk reads `total=3 seqH=1 frames=1`, the sequence facts `bd=8 sub=4:2:0 fi=1 ie=1`, and the frame header `bq=160 ll=0 tx=1 rts=0` with the real 293-byte tile payload. **The finding that matters:** the sequence flags are `fi=1 ie=1`, NOT the `0` earlier passes assumed — the real sequence header was unreadable before, so every decode attempt fed the driver the WRONG filter-intra/edge-filter config. **Proof (executed):** `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **4/4** (JVM/Script × plain + canonicalized). **Boundary (measured, NOT landed):** with the correct flags the tile decode still RUNS (no throw) but its pixels do NOT yet match ffmpeg's — that reconciliation (the composition) is the next slice; the oracle is `ffmpeg -i x.avif -pix_fmt yuv420p -f rawvideo`.
> **AVIF slice 3az DIAGNOSED 14/10 (pure Kof + real file; nothing landed — tree clean): a REAL ffmpeg/libavif AVIF item begins with the SEQUENCE HEADER, not a temporal delimiter, and the Kof item-data OBU walk REFUSES it — this is the blocker between the 3ay walk and the pixel composition.** `avif_real_64.avif`'s item data starts with `0x0a` = `OBU_SEQUENCE_HEADER` (type 1) and never leads with a temporal delimiter (type 2); `AvifObu.avifItemObus` requires `type == 2` for the FIRST OBU (`IMAGE: avif item does not begin with a temporal delimiter`, pinned by `AvifObuE2ETest`'s `nodelim` error face). So `readAvifItemObus(path)` cannot yield the real sequence header (`enable_filter_intra`, `enable_intra_edge_filter`, bit depth) the tile decode needs — the composition can only fall back to the sentinel seq. **Why this is a GAP and not a silent fix:** the delimiter-first rule is a DELIBERATE, TESTED conformance choice (the `nodelim` face exists to keep it), so relaxing it is a subset decision — not something an agent flips alone. The two resolutions: (a) relax it for ISOBMFF items (accept `OBU_SEQUENCE_HEADER` first, update the `nodelim` face), or (b) require the sequence header in the `av1C` `configOBUs` (ffmpeg's `-f avif` puts it in the item data by default). **What IS landed from this pass:** the ffmpeg fixture + walk proof (3ay) and the exposed seq facts (`enable_filter_intra`/`enable_intra_edge_filter`, 3ay), so the composition is one decision away. **Measured, honest boundary:** the Kof tile decode RUNS on the real payload only when fed the sentinel seq, and its pixels do not yet match ffmpeg's — that reconciliation is the next slice, gated by this gap.
> **AVIF slice 3ay LANDED 14/10 (pure Kof + a REAL-FILE proof): the SEQUENCE header exposes `enable_filter_intra` / `enable_intra_edge_filter`, and a real ffmpeg-encoded AVIF pins the whole container→header→tile-group read path.** The sequence walk read (skipped) the two AV1 §5.5.1 flags behind `use_128x128_superblock` but never carried them, so the tile driver's `enableFilterIntra` and its intra edge filter control had NO source — the composition could only guess, and the guess was wrong (a real frame diverged). `AvifSeqHeader` now exposes both. **A REAL fixture:** `resources/avif_real_64.avif` is a 64x64 AVIF produced by ffmpeg's `libaom-av1` encoder through the `avif` muxer (the whole suite before this used SYNTHESISED boxes); it pins metadata, the frame header (with the slice-3ax `lossless`/`txModeSelect`/`reducedTxSet`) and the tile-group walk with its 293-byte tile payload against bytes Kof did not write. ffmpeg (`libdav1d`/`libaom-av1`) is ALSO the pixel oracle for the reconstruction slice. **Proof (executed):** `AvifRealFileE2ETest` **2/2** (JVM/Script); `AvifSeqE2ETest` **8/8** (the four fixtures' new `fi=0 ie=0` facts match the independent Java reader); `AvifFrameE2ETest` **14/16**, the 2 failures being the pre-existing `known-bugs` §723 native faces (A/B'd identical before this slice). **Boundary (measured, NOT landed):** driving the Kof tile decode on the real payload RUNS (21 blocks, no throw) but its pixels do NOT yet match ffmpeg's — the next slice; this is why the real-file test stops at the payload and claims no pixel result.
> **AVIF slice 3ax LANDED 14/10 (pure Kof): the frame header now EXPOSES the three tile-decode facts it computed and discarded — `lossless`, `tx_mode_select`, `reduced_tx_set`.** The tail walk (`AvifFrame.frameTailWalk`) already derived `CodedLossless` (base_q_idx == 0 and no non-zero segment qindex) and already SKIPPED the `tx_mode_select` (present only when not CodedLossless) and `reduced_tx_set` bits — but it returned only `{bits, baseQ, lf0, cdefBits}`, so the composition (`decodeRaster` AVIF) had no way to obtain the three frame-level values the tile driver takes as `lossless` / `txMode` / `reducedTxSet` without re-deriving them. The walk now reads and returns all three (`AvifFrameHeader` gains `lossless`, `txModeSelect`, `reducedTxSet`), keeping `headerBytes` unchanged (the bit positions are identical — the skip became a read). Spec: `CodedLossless` forces `TX_MODE_ONLY_4X4` (`tx_mode_select` absent), `read_tx_mode` = `TX_MODE_SELECT` when the bit is 1, else `TX_MODE_LARGEST`; `reduced_tx_set` forces `TX_SET_INTRA_2`. **Proof (executed):** the Kof probe, the independent Java reader (`AvifFrameJavaSupport`) and the golden all carry the new facts and agree — the three non-native faces (JVM/Script/Java-reader-agrees) **3/3**; `AvifFrameE2ETest` **14/16**, the 2 failures being `avifFrameHeaderOnNativeX86` + `avifTileGroupsOnNativeX86`, measured IDENTICAL on the pre-slice tree (pre-existing `known-bugs` §723, the register-locals default — not this slice).
> **AVIF slice 3aw LANDED 14/10 (test infra, pure Kof): the real interleaved tile's RECONSTRUCTED PIXELS pinned — the 3au "decodes end to end" claim now checks the pixel output, not only the mode records.** The slice-3au tree probe asserted the driver's per-block mode records and final entropy context but never read the reconstructed plane, so a wrong prediction/transform/plane-offset would have passed. The probe now also prints the four BLOCK_32X32 quadrant DC samples and the interior SUM, and the golden pins them (`P 128 128 128 128`, `S 524288`). The values are spec-correct: `DC_PRED` with no neighbours (`haveAbove == haveLeft == 0` → `1 << (bitDepth - 1)`) and a zero residual is flat mid-gray 128 at bit depth 8, so 64x64x128 = 524288. **Cross-target:** the tree face now runs on JVM + Script + JS (3/3) — the native faces of this probe are blocked by `known-bugs` §723 (the register-locals default), the same boundary the driver/partition native faces carry. **Proof (executed, hatch OFF):** `Av1PartitionE2ETest` **19/19** (17 → 19: the two new Script/JS tree faces); compile rc=0.
> **AVIF slice 3au RE-LANDED 14/10 (pure Kof): the tile driver is a CLASS and the walk DRIVES it — and `known-bugs` §705 is FIXED (the native x86 spin no longer reproduces).** 3au was reverted on 13/10 because its class extraction turned a CLEAN native bounds error into a SPIN (§705). > **AVIF slice 3au: §705 SEVENTH PASS 14/10 (pure Kof + test infra; nothing landed — tree clean): the 16-byte stack-argument alignment hypothesis is WEAKENED.** The sixth pass read ONE observation (a `pushq $0` pad made the hanging probe exit cleanly) as the cause. Two measurements this pass undercut it: a MINIMAL 21-arg reproducer (15 stack args, odd — the same shape as `decodeBlock`'s call) runs correctly `rc=0`/`21` BOTH with the pad and WITHOUT it, and the emitted runtime has no 16-byte-aligned SSE access to fault on (every SSE use is register-only `movd`/`movq`/`ucomiss`/`ucomisd`; `kof_alloc` is `lock cmpxchg` + `futex`) — a misaligned frame is benign here. Reverted again — the decision to land the pad as ABI hardening belongs to the compiler/native lane. See `known-bugs` §705 (seventh pass) and §723.
> **AVIF slice 3au: §705 FIFTH PASS 14/10 (pure Kof + test infra; nothing landed — tree clean): the hang IS INPUT-DEPENDENT (correcting the second pass) and 3au turns a CLEAN native error into a spin.** Four measurements on the emitted binary: (1) with `data[0] = 236` the native binary SPINS, with `data[0] = 11` the SAME binary exits cleanly with `Runtime error: array index out of bounds` — so the trigger depends on the entropy bytes and the second pass's "structural, not data-dependent" claim is **RETRACTED** (that claim came from a different harness); (2) the **pre-3au** top-level `av1DecodeTileRecon`, driven with the SAME 1-byte buffer and the same single `BLOCK_32X32`, exits with the bounds error (rc=1) where 3au spins — so 3au does not merely move the error, it converts a clean diagnosable error into a non-terminating spin; (3) forcing an in-`decodeBlock` bounds error (`above`/`left` of size 2) makes the 3au binary exit cleanly, so it is NOT "any runtime error inside a class method"; (4) with 256 bytes of data the 3au binary also exits cleanly — only the short/degenerate stream spins. **Together these point at one shape:** the spin needs the entropy reader to run out mid-block AND the exhaustion to land in the transform-block loop's later iterations — the interaction between the reader's exhaustion path and the loop, not the exhaustion itself and not the error path in general. **Also ruled out this pass:** an uncaught `throw` at top level exits cleanly (rc=1, message printed), and a `throw` from two levels deep in class methods (`Tile.decode` → `Reader.readSym` → `Reader.raw`) also exits cleanly — the native throw machinery is fine in both simple and nested cases. > **AVIF slice 3au: §705 FOURTH PASS 14/10 (pure Kof + test infra; nothing landed — tree clean): the spin is in `decodeBlock`'s TRANSFORM-BLOCK LOOP, on the 3rd of 16 blocks, and my third-pass lead (`av1InvTx2d`) is RETRACTED.** Instrumenting the callees (prints + runaway guards, never inside the suspect call) gives a stable trail for one `tile.decodeBlock(0, 0, 9)` on native: `DB grid n=16 size=32 txSz=1 bw=8 bh=8`, then iterations 0 and 1 complete (`post-coeffs → post-avail → post-pred → DB post-recon` each), then `DB iter 2 of 16 → ordervals 0 4 → brbc 0 4 → (nothing)`. > **AVIF slice 3au: §705 THIRD PASS 14/10 (pure Kof + test infra; nothing landed — tree clean): it is an INFINITE COMPUTE LOOP, not a stall, and the loop is AFTER `av1Dequant`'s rows.** The second pass left one ambiguity — "the function never returns" could be a deadlock or a spin. Settled by measurement on the emitted binary: `strace` shows the ONLY syscalls after startup are a repeating `futex(0x453820, FUTEX_WAKE, 0) = 0`, and `nm` resolves that address to **`kof_alloc_lock`** — so the program is ALLOCATING in a loop (each `kof_alloc` takes the lock with `lock cmpxchg` and releases it with a `FUTEX_WAKE`), not waiting on anything; and a clean single-process `ps` reading shows state **R** with CPU time accumulating and no output. **I also corrected a measurement error of my own:** an earlier `pgrep` had picked up an unrelated Electron process and reported state `S`/0 CPU, which would have supported the deadlock reading; and `pkill -f <name>` matched — and killed — my own shell, so exact-name `pkill -x` is required. **The assembly is NOT the culprit at the boundaries inspected:** `decodeBlock`'s 11-argument `av1ReconBlockInto` call site is correctly balanced (5 stack args, `addq $48, %rsp` after) and `av1Dequant`'s emitted loops have correct heads/increments/back-edges with a clean `movq %rbp, %rsp; popq %rbp; ret` epilogue. **Sharpest hypothesis to test first:** repeated successful `kof_alloc` means the runaway loop ALLOCATES per iteration, and `av1InvTx2d` (`libs/image/Av1InvTx.kf`) does exactly that — `var T = new Int[w]` inside `while (i < h)` and `var T = new Int[h]` inside `while (col < w)` — so a bogus large `h = 1 << av1TxHeightLog2(txSz)` there would produce precisely this. Note `av1Dequant` printed CORRECT bounds (`log2W=5 log2H=5 tw=32 th=32`), so if a bound is wrong it is the SECOND call to the log2 helper (the one `av1InvTx2d` makes), not the first. Next: instrument `av1InvTx2d`'s entry/exit and print its `h`/`w`/`log2H`, or hand §705 to the compiler/native lane; 3au stays reverted and the front continues with the tile-group/OBU layer, which does not depend on it.
> **AVIF slice 3au: §705 SECOND PASS 13/10 (pure Kof + test infra; nothing landed — tree clean): the hang is at `av1Dequant`'s EXIT, it is STRUCTURAL, and the original "46 locals" reading is now fully REFUTED.** New measurements, each from a print-marker run of the emitted native binary (marks emitted by instrumenting the CALLEES only, never `decodeBlock`): a single `tile.decodeBlock(0, 0, 9)` prints `BEFORE`/`AFTER` on the JVM and HANGS on native (one source, one input); the probe prints all 46 fields BEFORE the call and every one is correct; changing the input byte to `0` STILL hangs (structural, not value-dependent); the marker trail reaches `DQ in` → `DQ log2W=5 log2H=5 tw=32 th=32 denom=2 total=1024` → rows `i=0 … i=31` and then stops — the dequant loop COMPLETES and the hang is at its exit / `return out`; runaway guards inside both loops never fire (NOT an infinite loop); **removing the 46 `var x = this.x` hoists does NOT fix it** (frame size / local count RULED OUT); `ulimit -s unlimited` changes nothing (not stack exhaustion). **The load-bearing clue:** the pre-3au top-level `av1DecodeTileRecon` calls the SAME `av1ReconBlockInto`→`av1ReconstructBlock`→`av1Dequant` chain on the same targets and does NOT hang, so the callee is fine and the fault is in the CALLER/CALLEE frame interaction when `decodeBlock` is the caller. > **AVIF slice 3au DIAGNOSED further and CATALOGUED 13/10 (pure Kof + test infra; nothing landed — tree clean): the native x86 hang is a real cross-target divergence, now `known-bugs` §705.** Re-applied 3au locally and reproduced: the JVM/Script/JS faces pass, the native x86 binary SPINS with no output, and reverting to pre-3au makes the same face pass in 54–58s. **Narrowed by measurement:** a marked native probe prints `M1`…`M4 after-ctor` and stops at `M5 before-direct-decodeBlock`, so class construction and `planes()` WORK and the hang is a direct `tile.decodeBlock(0, 0, 9)` call; the arguments are right (`av1TxWidth(3)` → 32, `av1BlockNum4x4Wide(9)` → 8). **Inspected the emitted assembly (`KOF_KEEP_ASM=1`):** the prologue is `subq $992, %rsp` with all 46 slots written, the deepest slot `-720(%rbp)` is inside the frame, and a scan of the whole body finds ZERO offsets beyond the frame — so the earlier "the frame is too small for 46 locals" reading is REFUTED. **Four minimal native repros were REFUTED** (recorded in §705 so they are not repeated): many locals in a method, plain class-field access, a many-argument call inside a loop, and a method call on a field-held object inside a loop — all run. > **AVIF slice 3av LANDED 13/10 (pure Kof): the FRAME ASSEMBLY — and slice 3au REVERTED, because verifying the boundary I flagged last cycle caught a real native regression.** `libs/image/Av1FrameAssembly.kf` (new): `record Av1Frame(y, width, height, u, v, chromaWidth, chromaHeight)`, `av1StripPlaneBorder(buf, stride, w, h)` and `av1AssembleFrame(luma, frameW, frameH, chroma, hasChroma)`. They were, and they came back RED: the native x86 driver binary HANGS with no output. Attributed by measurement, not by guessing — with the library reverted to pre-3au the same face PASSES in 54–58s, and restoring 3au makes it hang again. Per `AGENTS.md` (no-bug-ships; own red must be fixed or reverted) 3au is REVERTED and the finding recorded rather than shipped.
> **AVIF slice 3au LANDED 13/10 (pure Kof): the tile driver becomes a CLASS and the walk DRIVES it — the interleaved tile stream now decodes end to end.** The blocker recorded in 3at was structural: libaom calls `decode_block` from INSIDE `decode_partition` (`av1/decoder/decodeframe.c:1296`), so the partition recursion and the block decode must run in the same scope — and Kof has no closures, so a top-level function cannot be re-entered per block. The driver's per-block stage (mode info, tx size, coefficients, predict, reconstruct) moved out of the top-level `av1DecodeTileRecon` into a new `class Av1TileDecode` (`libs/image/Av1DecodeTileRecon.kf`), whose `decodeBlock(r, c, bs)` is exactly the old loop body and whose `run(blocks)` is the old loop; the top-level function is now a 3-line wrapper at the ORIGINAL 38-parameter signature, so every existing harness is unchanged. `Av1Partition` gained `bindDecoder(Av1TileDecode)`: with a decoder bound, the `decode_block` step of `addStep` decodes that block IMMEDIATELY — before the walk reads the next partition symbol, which is libaom's own interleaving and the only way the two stay in sync on a shared reader. **Proof:** `Av1PartitionE2ETest` **17/17** — the new `av1TileTreeDecodesRealInterleavedTile` walks the real interleaved stream and reproduces the oracle's OWN decode (4 `BLOCK_32X32`/TX_32X32/DC_PRED blocks + final entropy context 17). **RED-first:** making `bindDecoder` a no-op yields NO block records and an all-zero entropy context — the stream desyncs exactly as expected. **Behaviour preservation proved separately:** the extraction was validated against the existing driver tests before the new feature was added (JVM faces of `Av1DecodeTileReconE2ETest`, `Av1DecodeTileTxTypeE2ETest`, `Av1DecodeChromaE2ETest` all green), so the refactor and the feature are independently evidenced. **Next:** `av1AssembleFrame` — stitch the driver's planes into a frame; then the tile-group/OBU layer and `decodeRaster` AVIF.
> **AVIF slice 3at LANDED 13/10 (pure Kof + test infra): the SHARED READER seam and the FIRST REAL INTERLEAVED TILE STREAM.** `Av1Partition.useReader(Av1Symbol)` (plus `position()`) lets a caller bind the entropy reader it already owns. That seam is load-bearing and not cosmetic: libaom's `decode_partition` reads the partition symbol and then calls `decode_block` on the SAME `aom_reader` (`av1/decoder/decodeframe.c:1296`), so a real tile's partition symbols and block payloads are INTERLEAVED in ONE stream — a walk that must stay in sync with a block decoder cannot own its reader. Every harness before this one fed the walk a stream carrying ONLY partition symbols. **The new oracle** (`oracle4.c`, /tmp, not versioned): the same block encoder the 3ag/3t harnesses use, plus libaom's own partition helpers (`partition_plane_context`, `partition_cdf_length`, `partition_cdf_length`, `update_partition_context` over `partition_context_lookup`) writing PARTITION_SPLIT then PARTITION_NONE per quadrant, and a VERBATIM copy of libaom's `read_partition` reading it back — the stream is proven valid by libaom's own reader before Kof ever sees it. **Proof:** `Av1PartitionE2ETest` **16/16** (was 10) — 4 seam faces (behaviour-preserving: the walk bound to a caller-owned reader emits the SAME decisions as the constructor-owned one, on JVM/Script/x86-64/JS), 1 binding face, and 1 REAL-TILE face: the walk consumes an interleaved stream (`180, 72, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 16`) and yields PARTITION_SPLIT(64x64) then PARTITION_NONE(32x32). **RED-first, twice:** making `useReader` a no-op fails the binding face (the shared reader stays at its 15-bit seed while the walk's own reader reaches 24); the no-op still PASSES the 4 golden faces, which is exactly why the binding face exists — behaviour-preservation alone does not prove the seam binds. **An honest boundary:** the real-tile face asserts only the first TWO decisions. The walk has no block decoder to consume the payload between symbols, so it is in sync exactly until the first `decode_block`; closing that needs the partition recursion to call the driver per leaf (in Kof the recursion and the block decode have to share one class — there are no closures). **An oracle finding, NOT a Kof bug:** the directional-mode variant of `oracle4.c` desyncs (the decoder read 19 luma transform blocks where 4 are expected). Isolated by measurement — with all blocks DC_PRED the stream is self-consistent (4 blocks, TX_32X32, final context 17); the divergence is in the angle-delta path and lives in MY harness, so no golden was landed from that variant. **Lesson:** `mvn test` INCREMENTALLY compiles test classes and silently reused a stale `Av1PartitionSupport`; a clean recompile flipped the face from RED to GREEN. Recompile clean before believing a face.
> **AVIF slice 3as LANDED 13/10 (pure Kof): the BLOCK GRID the tile driver consumes — `av1TileBlockGrid` in `libs/image/Av1Partition.kf`.** The frame assembly needs `blocks` (the `(r, c, bs)` triples `av1DecodeTileRecon` walks), and the open question was who produces them for a REAL tile. **Measured:** libaom's `decode_partition` calls `decode_block` at every leaf of the partition recursion and nowhere else, so the walk's `kind == 1` steps ARE the mode-info blocks — the grid falls straight out of the slice-3o walk, no new decode. The glue filters those steps in decode order. **A correction I owe:** the recorded NEXT STEP said "use `qchroma.avif`'s real tile stream as the golden", and that fixture has NO tile payload — it is `delimiter + sequence header + frame header` only (`frameReducedQ` writes no tile-group OBU, and `containerWithItem` wraps just those bytes in `mdat`). The tile payload is the real gap, now scoped. **Proof:** `Av1PartitionE2ETest` 10/10 (was 6) — the 4 new faces compare `av1TileBlockGrid` against the golden's OWN `B r c subSize` records (libaom's `decode_block` calls, 30 across the 4 shapes) on JVM/Script/x86-64/JS; RED-first, flipping the filter to `kind == 0` takes the `@@partition` steps instead and the JVM face fails. **Next:** the tile PAYLOAD — extend the `oracle3.c` harness to also ENCODE the partition symbols (its block encoder already writes mode info + tx size + coefficients for an explicit block list; only the partition-tree encoding is missing) and emit a tile group, so a real stream's grid can be re-derived by the walk; then `av1AssembleFrame` with that stream as the end-to-end golden.
> **AVIF slice 3ar LANDED 13/10 (test infra): the missing fixture — a NON-MONOCHROME + NON-LOSSLESS reduced frame, with its round-trip pinned.** The frame-assembly glue needs a stream that has CHROMA and a real qindex, and no fixture in the suite provided one. Why: every sequence writer here emits `mono_chrome = 1` — `q32` is non-lossless but MONO, `redSeq128` has the chroma planes but writes `base_q_idx = 0` (CODED_LOSSLESS). Regression: frame 16/16, group 8/8, seq 8/8, metadata 8/8. Next: the glue — stitch `av1DecodeTileRecon`'s planes into a frame using this fixture's real tile stream as the end-to-end golden.
> **AVIF slice 3ap FIXED 13/10 (pure Kof): `known-bugs` §689 — the CFL alpha SIGN was inverted.** `readBodyBd` (`libs/image/Av1ModeInfo.kf`) negated the CFL alpha on `signU == 2`; libaom's `CFL_SIGN_TYPE` is `{ ZERO, NEG, POS }` (`CFL_SIGN_NEG == 1`) and `cfl_idx_to_alpha` returns `-(abs + 1)` for everything but `CFL_SIGN_POS`, so every `UV_CFL_PRED` block with a negative alpha decoded the OPPOSITE sign. Fixed with `signU == 1` / `signV == 1`. **Proof:** the new `chromaAndLumaPixelsMatchLibaom` test — the driver's reconstructed LUMA (256/256 samples) and CHROMA (64/64) are byte-identical to libaom's own reconstruction for the CFL case; RED-first, reverting the polarity makes it fail. This is the end-to-end CFL PIXEL golden that four cycles of narrowing were building toward. **Collateral:** `av1_modebody_golden.txt.b64` had the same inversion baked in (it PASSED with the inverted code, which is what proves its generator shared the convention) — 479 alpha fields across 321 CFL rows re-signed; the slice-3am chroma-plane characterization lock caught the change too (only the CFL case's checksums moved) and was re-pinned. Also closed by their lanes: `known-bugs` §687 (my riscv64 >2 KiB frame limit) and §678. Next: the tile-group/frame assembly, then `decodeRaster` AVIF.
> **AVIF slice 3ao ROOT CAUSE FOUND 13/10 (pure Kof, oracle-side; nothing landed — tree clean): the oracle/driver divergence was MY ORACLE's bug, and the Kof chroma reconstruction now matches libaom on 63 of 64 samples.** Printing what the driver DECODED for the CFL case's luma gave `N 3 256 9 0:1` — 256 levels, **tx_type 9 (V_DCT)**, one non-zero at index 0 with value 1. **The oracle had been using tx_type 0 (DCT_DCT)** because I captured `av1_get_tx_type` right after `kof_read_tx_size`, BEFORE the coefficient decode, and libaom only stores the type INSIDE `av1_read_coeffs_txb`. That was the whole divergence. After moving the capture: the oracle's luma is `137 128 128 …` — byte-identical to the driver's 256 samples (the earlier flat `129` was an artifact of reconstructing with the wrong transform). The chroma now differs in exactly ONE sample: the driver has `130` where the oracle has `128` at the block's first position; the other 63 of 64 match on both planes. Eliminated on the way, all by measurement: the dequant shift, the DC prediction (128 both sides), and last cycle's "the divergence is in the residual" reading — itself a consequence of the wrong tx type. **Next: the one-sample difference — both sides predict flat 128 and the AC comes from the same luma, so the +2 is either the DC predictor's first sample or the AC's first element; compare `ac[0]` on both sides first. Then re-add the raw `L`/`W` test and land the end-to-end CFL pixel golden.**
> **AVIF slice 3ao follow-up 2 — 13/10 (pure Kof, still NOT landed — tree clean): the degenerate hash is replaced by a RAW per-sample comparison, and the divergence has a SHAPE.** The rolling 16-bit hash returned powers of two (a degenerating multiplicative chain), so both sides now emit raw samples of the block area. For the CFL case (`ci == 3`: one BLOCK_16X16 at the origin, luma DC_PRED, DC-only residual) the ORACLE (libaom `idct.c` + `cfl.c`) reconstructs the luma as a FLAT **129** over all 256 samples — a uniform +1 residual over the flat 128 prediction, exactly what a DC-only transform gives. The KOF DRIVER reconstructs **128 in 255 samples with ONE sample at 137 (position 0)** — essentially NO residual. So the divergence is in the LUMA RESIDUAL and it is a SHAPE difference, now visible without a checksum. Attribution is STILL not established and this is NOT recorded as a Kof bug: the lone 137 could also mean the probe reads the block area at the wrong offset, or that the driver's luma path took another branch — the U/D/A records of the same case DO match, so the stream is in sync. **Next, cheapest probes in order: (1) print the Kof `levels` for that luma block (DC-only 74, or empty?) — `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockEdgeTx` already appends it to `levelsOut`; (2) print its `txType`; (3) only then look at `av1ReconstructBlock`/`av1Dequant` for a DC-only TX_16X16.**

Setting the oracle to 129 moved its luma hash 4096 → 8192 and still did not match the driver's 31447, which is consistent. (3) WARNING: that rolling 16-bit hash returned POWERS OF TWO on this data — the signature of a degenerating multiplicative chain — so the mismatch reported last cycle may be a checksum artifact rather than a real divergence. **Next: replace the `L`/`W` hash with a sound comparison (sum of absolute differences, or a real CRC over the raw samples) and re-run BEFORE hunting a residual bug; if it survives, the divergence is in the RESIDUAL (dequant value or the inverse transform), since the shift and the prediction are excluded.** Then re-add the test and land the end-to-end CFL pixel golden.

It stops the geometry from regressing silently; it does not prove the pixels match libaom. That reference still needs an oracle that reconstructs — the recorded next step. Then the tile-group/frame assembly, then `decodeRaster` AVIF.

> **AVIF slice 3ai UNBLOCKED 12/10 (audit, code REVERTED — `known-bugs` §682 🟡 OPEN): the chroma planes cannot be composed into the tile driver yet (multi-block chroma desync; the recorded entropy-context hypothesis was MEASURED FALSE — the two formulas are identical over 42592 rows).** libaom's `decode_token_recon_block` runs the chroma planes INSIDE each mode-info block, after the luma plane of that same block; chroma's transform size is DERIVED per block (`av1_get_max_uv_txsize`, slice 3ah) and its transform type is DERIVED from the UV mode (`intra_mode_to_tx_type` over `get_uv_mode`, slice 3b) — libaom transmits `tx_type` for the luma plane only. I built `av1DecodeChromaBlock` (`libs/image/Av1DecodeChroma.kf`) composing the landed primitives (derived tx type → coefficients → availability with `ssX`/`ssY` → prediction with the UV mode → dequantize/inverse/reconstruct), extended `av1DecodeTileRecon` to call it after each block's luma loop, built a libaom-oracle chroma golden and a probe driving the full tile driver — and the multi-block case desynchronised. **The recorded root — RETRACTED 12/10, measured FALSE:** I had recorded that `av1TxbCtx`'s chroma branch used the wrong formula (Kof's `av1AllZeroCtxUV`, `7 + above + left + 3*bigger`, vs libaom's `get_entropy_context(tx_size, a, l) + ctx_offset`). **The two are IDENTICAL** — `ctx_offset` is 10 exactly when the plane block is bigger, and `get_entropy_context` folds to `(above != 0) + (left != 0)`, so libaom's value is the same `7 + above + left + 3*bigger`. A C oracle computing BOTH formulas side by side from libaom's OWN tables over 16 transform sizes x 22 block sizes x 11 x 11 neighbour byte patterns — 42592 rows — reports `diffs=0`; the Kof tables `av1BlockNumPelsLog2`/`av1TxSizeToBsize` match `num_pels_log2_lookup`/`txsize_to_bsize` entry for entry. `av1TxbCtx` must NOT be 'fixed'. The real cause is still unlocalized; the leading candidate is the chroma transform-block iteration/offset geometry. **The composition was REVERTED cleanly** (`git status` empty; `Av1DecodeTileReconE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeTileTxTypeE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1UvTxSizeE2ETest` 8/8 green): shipping it over a wrong context would desynchronise real bitstreams. **ROOT CAUSE FOUND + FIXED 12/10 — it was the ORACLE, not Kof:** the oracle's decoder-pass setup `init_dcb` reset only the LUMA context arrays and left the four CHROMA ones — which are SHARED with the encoder pass that had already written its packed contexts — so the decoder read the encoder's leftovers as its own decoded neighbours; that is why one block passed and every block following a residual diverged. Instrumenting BOTH sides at the `get_txb_ctx` call showed the decoder at `a0=17 l0=17` (chroma `txb_skip_ctx` 9, CDF 7613) where the encoder was at `a0=0 l0=0` (`ctx` 7, CDF 29718). Fix: clear all six arrays in `init_dcb`; the encoder and decoder now agree symbol for symbol (51 vs 51, zero divergences) and the golden is byte-deterministic (sha256 `e61ff2ad…`). The Kof chroma path was never implicated. **NEXT STEP (the re-trigger reads this):** re-land the chroma composition (`Av1DecodeChroma.kf` + the driver call + the probe/golden) against the now-VALID multi-block chroma golden produced by the fixed oracle. Do NOT change `av1TxbCtx` (proven correct). Then

> **AVIF slice 3ah LANDED 12/10 (pure Kof, no new bug): the CHROMA transform size derivation (`av1PlaneBlockSize` + `av1GetMaxUvTxSize`, `libs/image/Av1IntraAvail.kf`).** libaom's `av1_get_max_uv_txsize(bsize, ssX, ssY)` (`av1/common/blockd.h`) is what `decode_reconstruct_tx` uses for every `plane > 0`: it takes the plane's own block size through `get_plane_block_size` (`av1_ss_size_lookup`), looks up its rectangular max transform and applies `av1_get_adjusted_tx_size` (which folds 64x64-and-larger down to 32x32). Chroma is NOT subdivided per block — the whole chroma plane of a mode-info block is ONE transform, unlike luma's per-block `read_tx_size` walk. **The trap this slice exists to close:** `get_plane_block_size` is NOT `scale_chroma_bsize` (`reconintra.c`, already in this library as `av1ScaleChromaBsize`). The former SCALES DOWN for the plane's extent (8x8 -> 4x4 under 4:2:0); the latter REMAPS the smallest blocks UP for the intra predictor (4x4 -> 8x8 under 4:2:0). Wiring the predictor helper into the coefficient path was my first attempt and the oracle caught it immediately — the two functions disagree on every subsampled size. **Oracle = libaom's OWN C tables** compiled into a standalone tool that calls `av1_get_max_uv_txsize` for every `(bsize, ssX, ssY)`: 88 combinations, 72 valid, ZERO differences; the 16 impossible ones (`BLOCK_INVALID`, where libaom's `assert` fires) are refused by name in Kof rather than silently indexing past the tables. The table transcription had two real errors (BLOCK_8X32/BLOCK_16X64 at 4:2:0), found by the oracle, not by inspection. **Proof (executed):** new `Av1UvTxSizeE2ETest` **8 run / 0F / 0 skipped** — JVM + Script + native x86-64 + riscv64(qemu) + aarch64(qemu) + JS re-emit all 72 golden rows, plus a refusal face asserting exactly 16 named refusals; RED-first confirmed (renaming the primitive fails the test with `SEM015`). Non-regression: `Av1DecodeTileTxTypeE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeTileReconE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeTileE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeTilePlaneE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeCoeffPlaneE2ETest` 8/8, `Av1DecodeBlockE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1TxTypeE2ETest` 6/6, `Av1TxSizeE2ETest` 6/6, `Av1IntraAvailE2ETest` 7/7 (121 run / 0F / 4 skipped). **NEXT STEP (the re-trigger reads this):** the chroma transform SIZE is now derived; the next chroma work is the chroma coefficient + reconstruction loop itself (per-plane `above`/`left` entropy context, the chroma tx-type via `av1ComputeTxType`, and the U/V reconstruction into the frame), then the tile-group/frame assembly, then `decodeRaster` AVIF. `decodeRaster` AVIF still refused until the chain closes.**

> **AVIF slice 3ag-c LANDED 12/10 (pure Kof, `known-bugs` §680 FIXED): the transform type is read BETWEEN `all_zero` and the coefficient levels, the order libaom uses.** The slice-3ag-b fix (`known-bugs` §679) wired `readTxType` BEFORE `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockEdge`, but libaom's `av1_read_coeffs_txb` reads the `all_zero` flag FIRST and calls `av1_read_tx_type` only when it is 0 — the type symbol sits BETWEEN the two. On any transform block with a residual the two symbols differ, so the driver read `tx_type` where the stream had `all_zero` and the entropy stream desynchronised from there: every later read (the next block's mode info and tx size) was garbage, which is what §680 recorded as a "wrong transform size for the 2nd and later blocks". `BLOCK_32X32` never showed it (`TX_32X32` signals no `tx_type`) and the §679 golden's one-byte tile had all-zero symbols, so the transposition was invisible — the same shape of mistake as §679, one level down. **Fix:** `Av1Coeffs.decodeBlock` split into `readTxbSkip(allZeroCtx)` + `setTxType(txType)` + `decodeLevels(dcSignCtx)`; `libs/image/Av1CoeffPlane.kf` gained `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockEdgeTx` (a thin wrapper over a shared `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockCore`) reading `all_zero` → luma `tx_type` → levels and stamping the type into the frame `txTypes` map; the driver calls it and reports the decoded type. The tx-size-context hypothesis §680 originally recorded was DISPROVEN (Kof ctx == libaom ctx on every block, including the failing ones). **Proof (executed):** `Av1DecodeTileTxTypeE2ETest` **7 run / 0F / 0 skipped** (JVM + Script + native x86-64 + riscv64/aarch64 qemu + JS) over a REGENERATED golden (`SHA256 5d40c024…`) whose case 1 is the four-block `BLOCK_16X16` plane that exposed the bug — block 0 (no residual) and blocks 1–3 (residual, types 2/2/1) all match the oracle. Non-regression: `Av1DecodeTileReconE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeTileE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeCoeffPlaneE2ETest` 8/8, `Av1DecodeBlockE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1TxTypeE2ETest` 6/6 (42 run / 0F / 2 skipped). **NEXT STEP (the re-trigger reads this):** the luma intra tile now decodes multi-block planes correctly; the next AVIF slice is the CHROMA planes (U/V through the same loop with the chroma transform sizes and the `compute_tx_type` derivation, whose non-DCT_DCT path is not yet exercised), then the tile-group/frame assembly, then `decodeRaster` AVIF. `decodeRaster` AVIF still refused until the chain closes.**

> **AVIF slice 3ag-b LANDED 12/10 (pure Kof, `known-bugs` §679 FIXED): the AV1 intra tile driver now reads `tx_type` for intra transform blocks <= 16x16.** The slice-3ag golden only exercised `BLOCK_32X32` -> `TX_32X32` -> `get_ext_tx_types` = `EXT_TX_SET_DCTONLY` = 1 (no symbol), so the driver's hardcoded `txType = 0` agreed with the oracle by accident; libaom signals the symbol for every non-skipped intra transform block of size <= 16x16 (`av1_read_tx_type` at `av1/decoder/decodemv.c:626` from `av1_read_coeffs_txb` at `av1/decoder/decodetxb.c:158`; `TX_16X16` -> `EXT_TX_SET_DTT4_IDTX` -> 5 symbols), so a real bitstream desynchronised at the next symbol. `av1DecodeTileRecon` now takes a `reducedTxSet` flag, an `Av1TxType` store, the frame `txTypes` map and a `txTypeOut` record, and per transform block calls `readTxType(txSz, reducedTxSet, qIndex, yMode, useFilterIntra, filterIntraMode, txTypes, frameW >> 2, bc, br)` BEFORE `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockEdge`, threading the DECODED type into both the coefficient walk and `av1ReconBlockInto`. **Oracle = the REAL libaom path** (the encoder writes the mode info + tx size + a VARYING tx type + coefficients with libaom's OWN default CDFs and range encoder; the decoder drives libaom's REAL `read_intra_frame_mode_info` + `read_tx_size` + `av1_read_coeffs_txb` + `av1_get_tx_type`), zero symbol mismatches, pinned (`SHA256 42b7c972…`). **Proof (executed):** new `Av1DecodeTileTxTypeE2ETest` **7 run / 0F / 0 skipped** — JVM + Script + native x86-64 + riscv64(qemu) + aarch64(qemu) + JS assert the golden's per-block mode info, the DECODED transform type and the nonzero coefficient positions; non-regression `Av1DecodeTileReconE2ETest` 7/7. **New gap found while building it: `known-bugs` §680 🟡 OPEN** — a MULTI-block plane of `BLOCK_16X16` reads the wrong tx size for the 2nd and later blocks (the driver keeps splitting to TX_4X4 where libaom reads TX_16X16); the golden is single-block by design so it does not hide the finding, and the root cause is a hypothesis (the `frameW >> 2` mi-extent assumption in the tx-size context and `av1IntraAvail`), recorded rather than patched on a guess. **NEXT STEP (the re-trigger reads this):** prove and fix §680 (build the multi-block <= 16x16 oracle case, derive the tx-size context from the block's position and the plane's true mi extent), then the CHROMA planes, then the tile-group/frame assembly, then `decodeRaster` AVIF. `decodeRaster` AVIF still refused until the chain closes.**

> **AVIF slice 3ag-a AUDIT 12/10 (pure Kof, no code change — `known-bugs` §679 OPEN): the tile driver never reads `tx_type` for intra transform blocks <= 16x16.** The slice-3ag golden only exercises `BLOCK_32X32` -> `TX_32X32` -> `get_ext_tx_types` = `EXT_TX_SET_DCTONLY` = 1, so no `tx_type` symbol exists there; but libaom signals it for every non-skipped intra transform block of size <= 16x16 (`av1_read_tx_type` at `av1/decoder/decodemv.c:626`, reached from `av1_read_coeffs_txb` at `av1/decoder/decodetxb.c:158`; `TX_16X16` -> `EXT_TX_SET_DTT4_IDTX` -> 5 symbols). `libs/image/Av1DecodeTileRecon.kf` passes a hardcoded `txType = 0` to `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockEdge` (`libs/image/Av1CoeffPlane.kf:87`), so a real bitstream desynchronises at the next symbol. The `readTxType` of `libs/image/Av1TxType.kf` (slice 3t) already implements the read — it is simply never called from the driver. **Measured (not yet a golden):** the re-provisioned libaom oracle (`/tmp/s3ag/oracle2.c`; the encoder writes the mode info + tx size + coefficients with libaom's OWN default CDFs and range encoder, the decoder drives libaom's REAL `read_intra_frame_mode_info` + `read_tx_size` + `av1_read_coeffs_txb` + `av1_get_tx_type`), instrumented on both sides, prints for a `BLOCK_16X16` block `DTTX tx=2 mode=0 reduced=0 ext=5` then `MS n=5 s=0` (the 5-symbol `intra_ext_tx_cdf` read) where the encoder's stream has NO `tx_type` write; a single-block 16x16 case DOES round-trip once the encoder writes it (`av1_ext_tx_inv[EXT_TX_SET_DTT4_IDTX][DCT_DCT] = 9`). The multi-block 16x16 cases still desync in the ORACLE HARNESS (an encoder-side `txb_skip` context mismatch), so the driver fix is held back rather than shipped as an unverified path (Q1/Q5). **NEXT STEP (the re-trigger reads this):** land the `readTxType` wiring in `Av1DecodeTileRecon.kf` (init an `Av1TxType` store; per transform block call `readTxType(txSz, reducedTxSet, qIndex, yMode, useFilterIntra, filterIntraMode, txTypes, stride, x4, y4)` BEFORE `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockEdge` and pass the result), FIRST finishing the oracle harness's multi-block 16x16 sync so a verified golden for the <= 16x16 path exists; then the CHROMA planes, then the tile-group/frame assembly, then `decodeRaster` AVIF. `decodeRaster` AVIF still refused until the chain closes.**
